Namo tassa bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa

Homage to the Blessed One, the Arahant, the Fully Self-Enlightened One.

Abhidhammapiṭake

In the Abhidhamma Piṭaka

Puggalapaññattipāḷi

The Designation of Persons

Mātikā

The Outline

1. Ekakauddeso

1. The Singular Section

1. Cha [T 0.0129][P 0.0001][M 0.0101][V 0.0103] paññattiyo – khandhapaññatti, āyatanapaññatti, dhātupaññatti, saccapaññatti, indriyapaññatti, puggalapaññattīti.

1. There are six designations: the designation of aggregates, the designation of sense-bases, the designation of elements, the designation of truths, the designation of faculties, and the designation of persons.

2. Kittāvatā khandhānaṃ khandhapaññatti? Yāvatā pañcakkhandhā – rūpakkhandho, vedanākkhandho, saññākkhandho, saṅkhārakkhandho, viññāṇakkhandho; ettāvatā khandhānaṃ khandhapaññatti.

2. To what extent is there the designation of aggregates? To the extent that there are the five aggregates—the form aggregate, the feeling aggregate, the perception aggregate, the volitional formations aggregate, and the consciousness aggregate; to this extent there is the designation of aggregates.

3. Kittāvatā [V 0.0104] āyatanānaṃ āyatanapaññatti? Yāvatā dvādasāyatanāni – cakkhāyatanaṃ, rūpāyatanaṃ, sotāyatanaṃ, saddāyatanaṃ, ghānāyatanaṃ, gandhāyatanaṃ, jivhāyatanaṃ, rasāyatanaṃ, kāyāyatanaṃ, phoṭṭhabbāyatanaṃ, manāyatanaṃ, dhammāyatanaṃ; ettāvatā āyatanānaṃ āyatanapaññatti.

3. To what extent is there the designation of sense-bases? To the extent that there are the twelve sense-bases—the eye base, the form base, the ear base, the sound base, the nose base, the smell base, the tongue base, the taste base, the body base, the tangible object base, the mind base, and the mental-object base; to this extent there is the designation of sense-bases.

4. Kittāvatā [T 0.0130] dhātūnaṃ dhātupaññatti? Yāvatā aṭṭhārasa dhātuyo – cakkhudhātu, rūpadhātu, cakkhuviññāṇadhātu, sotadhātu, saddadhātu, sotaviññāṇadhātu, ghānadhātu, gandhadhātu, ghānaviññāṇadhātu, jivhādhātu, rasadhātu, jivhāviññāṇadhātu, kāyadhātu, phoṭṭhabbadhātu, kāyaviññāṇadhātu, manodhātu, dhammadhātu, manoviññāṇadhātu; ettāvatā dhātūnaṃ dhātupaññatti.

4. To what extent is there the designation of elements? To the extent that there are the eighteen elements—the eye element, the form element, the eye-consciousness element; the ear element, the sound element, the ear-consciousness element; the nose element, the smell element, the nose-consciousness element; the tongue element, the taste element, the tongue-consciousness element; the body element, the tangible object element, the body-consciousness element; the mind element, the mental-object element, and the mind-consciousness element; to this extent there is the designation of elements.

5. Kittāvatā [P 0.0002][M 0.0102] saccānaṃ saccapaññatti? Yāvatā cattāri saccāni – dukkhasaccaṃ, samudayasaccaṃ, nirodhasaccaṃ, maggasaccaṃ; ettāvatā saccānaṃ saccapaññatti.

5. To what extent is there the designation of truths? To the extent that there are the four truths—the truth of suffering, the truth of the origin of suffering, the truth of the cessation of suffering, and the truth of the path; to this extent there is the designation of truths.

6. Kittāvatā indriyānaṃ indriyapaññatti? Yāvatā bāvīsatindriyāni – cakkhundriyaṃ, sotindriyaṃ, ghānindriyaṃ, jivhindriyaṃ, kāyindriyaṃ, manindriyaṃ, itthindriyaṃ, purisindriyaṃ, jīvitindriyaṃ, sukhindriyaṃ, dukkhindriyaṃ, somanassindriyaṃ, domanassindriyaṃ, upekkhindriyaṃ, saddhindriyaṃ, vīriyindriyaṃ, satindriyaṃ, samādhindriyaṃ, paññindriyaṃ, anaññātaññassāmītindriyaṃ, aññindriyaṃ, aññātāvindriyaṃ; ettāvatā indriyānaṃ indriyapaññatti.

6. To what extent is there the designation of faculties? To the extent that there are the twenty-two faculties—the eye faculty, the ear faculty, the nose faculty, the tongue faculty, the body faculty, the mind faculty, the femininity faculty, the masculinity faculty, the life faculty, the pleasure faculty, the pain faculty, the joy faculty, the sorrow faculty, the equanimity faculty, the faith faculty, the energy faculty, the mindfulness faculty, the concentration faculty, the wisdom faculty, the faculty of the thought, 'I shall know the unknown', the faculty of final knowledge, and the faculty of one who has final knowledge; to this extent there is the designation of faculties.

7. Kittāvatā [T 0.0131] puggalānaṃ puggalapaññatti?

7. To what extent is there the designation of persons?

(1) Samayavimutto

(1) One liberated on occasion

(2) Asamayavimutto

(2) One liberated without occasion

(3) Kuppadhammo

(3) One of a shakeable nature

(4) Akuppadhammo

(4) One of an unshakeable nature

(5) Parihānadhammo

(5) One who is of a nature to decline

(6) Aparihānadhammo

(6) One who is not of a nature to decline

(7) Cetanābhabbo [V 0.0105]

(7) One capable of exertion

(8) Anurakkhaṇābhabbo

(8) One capable of safeguarding

(9) Puthujjano

(9) A worldling

(10) Gotrabhū

(10) One who changes lineage

(11) Bhayūparato

(11) One who refrains through fear

(12) Abhayūparato

(12) One who refrains not through fear

(13) Bhabbāgamano

(13) One capable of attainment

(14) Abhabbāgamano

(14) One incapable of attainment

(15) Niyato

(15) One who is fixed

(16) Aniyato

(16) One who is not fixed

(17) Paṭipannako [M 0.0103]

(17) One practicing the path

(18) Phaleṭhito

(18) One established in the fruit

(19) Samasīsī

(19) One who is equal-headed

(20) Ṭhitakappī

(20) One who endures for an aeon

(21) Ariyo

(21) A noble one

(22) Anariyo

(22) An ignoble one

(23) Sekkho

(23) A trainee

(24) Asekkho

(24) One beyond training

(25) Nevasekkhanāsekkho

(25) One who is neither a trainee nor beyond training

(26) Tevijjo

(26) One endowed with the threefold knowledge

(27) Chaḷabhiñño

(27) One endowed with the six higher knowledges

(28) Sammāsambuddho [P 0.0003][V 0.0106]

(28) A Perfectly Enlightened One

(29) Paccekasambuddho paccekabuddho (sī.)

A Solitary Enlightened One

(30) Ubhatobhāgavimutto

One liberated in both ways

(31) Paññāvimutto

One liberated by wisdom

(32) Kāyasakkhī

A body-witness

(33) Diṭṭhippatto

One who has attained to the view

(34) Saddhāvimutto

One liberated by conviction

(35) Dhammānusārī

A Dhamma-follower

(36) Saddhānusārī

A conviction-follower

(37) Sattakkhattuparamo

One with at most seven more rebirths

(38) Kolaṅkolo

One who goes from clan to clan

(39) Ekabījī

A one-seeder

(40) Sakadāgāmī [M 0.0104]

A once-returner

(41) Anāgāmī

A non-returner

(42) Antarāparinibbāyī

One who attains Parinibbāna in the interim

(43) Upahaccaparinibbāyī

One who attains Parinibbāna upon reaching

(44) Asaṅkhāraparinibbāyī

One who attains Parinibbāna without exertion

(45) Sasaṅkhāraparinibbāyī

One who attains Parinibbāna with exertion

(46) Uddhaṃsotoakaniṭṭhagāmī

An upstream-goer bound for the Akaniṭṭha realm

(47) Sotāpanno

A stream-enterer

(48) Sotāpattiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno

One practicing for the realization of the fruit of stream-entry

(49) Sakadāgāmī [V 0.0107]

A once-returner

(50) Sakadāgāmiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno

One practicing for the realization of the fruit of once-returning

(51) Anāgāmī

A non-returner

(52) Anāgāmiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno

One practicing for the realization of the fruit of non-returning

(53) Arahā

An Arahant

(54) Arahattaphalasacchikiriyāya arahattāya (sī.) paṭipanno

One practicing for the realization of the fruit of Arahantship

Ekakaṃ.

The Section of Ones.

2. Dukauddeso

2. The Matrix of Pairs

8. Dve [T 0.0132] puggalā –

8. Two kinds of individuals:

(1) Kodhano ca, upanāhī ca.

The angry one and the grudge-bearer.

(2) Makkhī ca, paḷāsī palāsī (syā. ka.) ca.

The denigrator and the rivalrous one.

(3) Issukī ca, maccharī ca.

The envious one and the miserly one.

(4) Saṭho ca, māyāvī ca.

The fraudulent one and the schemer.

(5) Ahiriko [M 0.0105] ca, anottappī ca.

The shameless one and the one with no fear of wrongdoing.

(6) Dubbaco ca, pāpamitto ca.

The one difficult to admonish and the one with bad friends.

(7) Indriyesu [P 0.0004] aguttadvāro ca, bhojane amattaññū ca.

The one with unguarded doors in the sense faculties and the one who is immoderate in eating.

(8) Muṭṭhassati ca, asampajāno ca.

The one with lapsed mindfulness and the one who lacks clear comprehension.

(9) Sīlavipanno ca, diṭṭhivipanno ca.

The one corrupt in virtue and the one corrupt in view.

(10) Ajjhattasaṃyojano [V 0.0108] ca, bahiddhāsaṃyojano ca.

The one with internal fetters and the one with external fetters.

(11) Akkodhano ca, anupanāhī ca.

The one who is not angry and the one who does not bear a grudge.

(12) Amakkhī ca, apaḷāsī ca.

The person who is not ungrateful and the person who is not disparaging.

(13) Anissukī ca, amaccharī ca.

The person who is not envious and the person who is not miserly.

(14) Asaṭho ca, amāyāvī ca.

The person who is not fraudulent and the person who is not scheming.

(15) Hirimā ca, ottappī ca.

The person who has moral shame and the person who has moral dread.

(16) Suvaco ca, kalyāṇamitto ca.

The person who is easy to admonish and the person who has good friends.

(17) Indriyesu guttadvāro ca, bhojane mattaññū ca.

The person with guarded sense faculties and the person who is moderate in eating.

(18) Upaṭṭhitassati ca, sampajāno ca.

The person with established mindfulness and the person who has clear comprehension.

(19) Sīlasampanno ca, diṭṭhisampanno ca.

The person who is accomplished in virtue and the person who is accomplished in view.

(20) Dve puggalā dullabhā lokasmiṃ.

Two persons are rare in the world.

(21) Dve puggalā duttappayā.

Two persons are hard to please.

(22) Dve puggalā sutappayā.

Two persons are easily satisfied.

(23) Dvinnaṃ puggalānaṃ āsavā vaḍḍhanti.

For two persons, the taints increase.

(24) Dvinnaṃ puggalānaṃ āsavā na vaḍḍhanti.

For two persons, the taints do not increase.

(25) Hīnādhimutto ca, paṇītādhimutto ca.

The person with inferior inclinations and the person with sublime inclinations.

(26) Titto ca, tappetā ca.

The person who is satisfied and the person who causes satisfaction.

Dukaṃ.

The Dyad.

3. Tikauddeso

3. Exposition of the Triads

9. Tayo [M 0.0106][V 0.0109] puggalā –

9. Three persons:

(1) Nirāso, āsaṃso, vigatāso.

The person without longing, the person with longing, and the person whose longing has departed.

(2) Tayo gilānūpamā puggalā.

Three persons comparable to a sick person.

(3) Kāyasakkhī, diṭṭhippatto, saddhāvimutto.

The body-witness, the one attained-to-view, and the one liberated by faith.

(4) Gūthabhāṇī, pupphabhāṇī, madhubhāṇī.

The person whose speech is like excrement, the person whose speech is like a flower, and the person whose speech is like honey.

(5) Arukūpamacitto puggalo, vijjūpamacitto puggalo[T 0.0133], vajirūpamacitto puggalo.

A person with a mind like a festering sore, a person with a mind like lightning, and a person with a mind like a diamond.

(6) Andho, ekacakkhu, dvicakkhu.

The blind person, the one-eyed person, and the two-eyed person.

(7) Avakujjapañño puggalo, ucchaṅgapañño uccaṅgupañño (syā.) puggalo, puthupañño puggalo.

The person with overturned wisdom, the person with lap-wisdom, and the person with broad wisdom.

(8) Atthekacco [P 0.0005] puggalo kāmesu ca bhavesu ca avītarāgo, atthekacco puggalo kāmesu vītarāgo bhavesu avītarāgo, atthekacco puggalo kāmesu ca bhavesu ca vītarāgo.

There is a certain person who is not free from passion for sensual pleasures and for existences; there is a certain person who is free from passion for sensual pleasures but not free from passion for existences; and there is a certain person who is free from passion for sensual pleasures and for existences.

(9) Pāsāṇalekhūpamo puggalo, pathavilekhūpamo puggalo, udakalekhūpamo puggalo.

The person comparable to a line on rock, the person comparable to a line on earth, and the person comparable to a line on water.

(10) Tayo potthakūpamā puggalā.

Three persons comparable to linen cloths.

(11) Tayo kāsikavatthūpamā puggalā.

Three persons comparable to cloths from Kāsī.

(12) Suppameyyo, duppameyyo, appameyyo.

The person who is easily measured, the person who is difficult to measure, and the person who is immeasurable.

(13) Atthekacco puggalo na sevitabbo na bhajitabbo na payirupāsitabbo, atthekacco puggalo sevitabbo bhajitabbo payirupāsitabbo, atthekacco puggalo sakkatvā garuṃ katvā garukatvā (sī.) sevitabbo bhajitabbo payirupāsitabbo.

There is a certain person who is not to be associated with, not to be cultivated, and not to be attended upon; there is a certain person who is to be associated with, to be cultivated, and to be attended upon; and there is a certain person who, after being respected and venerated, is to be associated with, to be cultivated, and to be attended upon.

(14) Atthekacco [M 0.0107] puggalo jigucchitabbo na sevitabbo na bhajitabbo na payirupāsitabbo, atthekacco puggalo ajjhupekkhitabbo na sevitabbo na bhajitabbo na payirupāsitabbo; atthekacco puggalo sevitabbo bhajitabbo payirupāsitabbo.

There is a certain person who is to be loathed, not to be associated with, not to be cultivated, and not to be attended upon; there is a certain person who is to be regarded with equanimity, not to be associated with, not to be cultivated, and not to be attended upon; and there is a certain person who is to be associated with, to be cultivated, and to be attended upon.

(15) Atthekacco puggalo sīlesu paripūrakārī paripūrīkārī (syā.), samādhismiṃ mattaso kārī, paññāya mattaso [V 0.0110] kārī; atthekacco puggalo sīlesu ca paripūrakārī, samādhismiñca paripūrakārī, paññāya mattaso kārī; atthekacco puggalo sīlesu ca paripūrakārī, samādhismiñca paripūrakārī, paññāya ca paripūrakārī.

There is a certain person who is a full practitioner in virtue, a moderate practitioner in concentration, and a moderate practitioner in wisdom; there is a certain person who is a full practitioner in virtue and a full practitioner in concentration, but a moderate practitioner in wisdom; and there is a certain person who is a full practitioner in virtue, a full practitioner in concentration, and a full practitioner in wisdom.

(16) Tayo satthāro.

Three teachers.

(17) Aparepi tayo satthāro.

Also, three other teachers.

Tikaṃ.

The Triad.

4. Catukkauddeso

4. Exposition of the Fours

10. Cattāro [T 0.0134] puggalā –

10. Four persons:

(1) Asappuriso, asappurisena asappurisataro, sappuriso, sappurisena sappurisataro.

An ignoble person, one more ignoble than an ignoble person; a noble person, one more noble than a noble person.

(2) Pāpo, pāpena pāpataro, kalyāṇo, kalyāṇena kalyāṇataro.

An evil person, one more evil than an evil person; a virtuous person, one more virtuous than a virtuous person.

(3) Pāpadhammo[P 0.0006], pāpadhammena pāpadhammataro, kalyāṇadhammo, kalyāṇadhammena kalyāṇadhammataro.

A person of evil conduct; one of more evil conduct than a person of evil conduct; a person of good conduct; one of better conduct than a person of good conduct.

(4) Sāvajjo, vajjabahulo, appavajjo appasāvajjo (syā. ka.) a. ni. 4.135, anavajjo.

A blameworthy person; one full of blame; one with little blame; a blameless person.

(5) Ugghaṭitaññū, vipañcitaññū vipacitaññū (sī.) a. ni. 4.133, neyyo, padaparamo.

One who understands upon a brief exposition; one who understands upon a detailed explanation; one who requires guidance; one for whom the text is the absolute limit.

(6) Yuttappaṭibhāno[M 0.0108], no muttappaṭibhāno, muttappaṭibhāno, no yuttappaṭibhāno, yuttappaṭibhāno ca muttappaṭibhāno ca, neva yuttappaṭibhāno no muttappaṭibhāno.

One whose exposition is coherent but not fluent; one whose exposition is fluent but not coherent; one whose exposition is both coherent and fluent; one whose exposition is neither coherent nor fluent.

(7) Cattāro dhammakathikā puggalā.

There are four types of persons who are speakers of the Dhamma.

(8) Cattāro valāhakūpamā puggalā.

There are four types of persons comparable to clouds.

(9) Cattāro mūsikūpamā puggalā.

There are four types of persons comparable to mice.

(10) Cattāro [V 0.0111] ambūpamā puggalā.

There are four types of persons comparable to mangoes.

(11) Cattāro kumbhūpamā puggalā.

There are four types of persons comparable to pots.

(12) Cattāro udakarahadūpamā puggalā.

There are four types of persons comparable to pools of water.

(13) Cattāro balībaddūpamā balibaddūpamā (sī.) puggalā.

There are four types of persons comparable to bulls.

(14) Cattāro āsīvisūpamā puggalā.

There are four types of persons comparable to venomous snakes.

(15) Atthekacco puggalo ananuvicca apariyogāhetvā avaṇṇārahassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti, atthekacco puggalo ananuvicca apariyogāhetvā vaṇṇārahassa avaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti, atthekacco puggalo ananuvicca apariyogāhetvā appasādanīye ṭhāne pasādaṃ upadaṃsitā hoti, atthekacco puggalo ananuvicca apariyogāhetvā pasādanīye ṭhāne appasādaṃ upadaṃsitā hoti.

There is a certain person who, without investigating and without examining, speaks in praise of one who is unworthy of praise. There is a certain person who, without investigating and without examining, speaks in dispraise of one who is worthy of praise. There is a certain person who, without investigating and without examining, shows confidence in a matter that should not inspire confidence. There is a certain person who, without investigating and without examining, shows a lack of confidence in a matter that should inspire confidence.

(16) Atthekacco puggalo anuvicca pariyogāhetvā avaṇṇārahassa [T 0.0135] avaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti, atthekacco puggalo anuvicca pariyogāhetvā vaṇṇārahassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti, atthekacco puggalo anuvicca pariyogāhetvā appasādanīye ṭhāne appasādaṃ upadaṃsitā hoti, atthekacco puggalo anuvicca pariyogāhetvā pasādanīye ṭhāne pasādaṃ upadaṃsitā hoti.

There is a certain person who, after investigating and examining, speaks in dispraise of one who is unworthy of praise. There is a certain person who, after investigating and examining, speaks in praise of one who is worthy of praise. There is a certain person who, after investigating and examining, shows a lack of confidence in a matter that should not inspire confidence. There is a certain person who, after investigating and examining, shows confidence in a matter that should inspire confidence.

(17) Atthekacco puggalo avaṇṇārahassa avaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena, no ca kho vaṇṇārahassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena; atthekacco puggalo vaṇṇārahassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā [M 0.0109] hoti bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena, no ca kho avaṇṇārahassa avaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena; atthekacco puggalo avaṇṇārahassa ca avaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti bhūtaṃ [P 0.0007] tacchaṃ kālena; vaṇṇārahassa ca vaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena, atthekacco puggalo neva avaṇṇārahassa avaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena, no ca vaṇṇārahassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena.

There is a certain person who speaks in dispraise of one who is unworthy of praise—factually, truthfully, and at the proper time—but does not speak in praise of one who is worthy of praise—factually, truthfully, and at the proper time. There is a certain person who speaks in praise of one who is worthy of praise—factually, truthfully, and at the proper time—but does not speak in dispraise of one who is unworthy of praise—factually, truthfully, and at the proper time. There is a certain person who speaks both in dispraise of one who is unworthy of praise—factually, truthfully, and at the proper time—and in praise of one who is worthy of praise—factually, truthfully, and at the proper time. There is a certain person who speaks neither in dispraise of one who is unworthy of praise—factually, truthfully, and at the proper time—nor in praise of one who is worthy of praise—factually, truthfully, and at the proper time.

(18) Uṭṭhānaphalūpajīvī no puññaphalūpajīvī, puññaphalūpajīvī no uṭṭhānaphalūpajīvī, uṭṭhānaphalūpajīvī ca puññaphalūpajīvī ca, neva uṭṭhānaphalūpajīvī no puññaphalūpajīvī.

One who lives by the fruit of effort, not by the fruit of merit; one who lives by the fruit of merit, not by the fruit of effort; one who lives by both the fruit of effort and the fruit of merit; one who lives by neither the fruit of effort nor the fruit of merit.

(19) Tamo [V 0.0112] tamaparāyano, tamo jotiparāyano, joti tamaparāyano, joti jotiparāyano.

One from darkness destined for darkness; one from darkness destined for light; one from light destined for darkness; one from light destined for light.

(20) Oṇatoṇato, oṇatuṇṇato, uṇṇatoṇato, uṇṇatuṇṇato.

Low and tending lower; low and tending higher; high and tending lower; high and tending higher.

(21) Cattāro rukkhūpamā puggalā.

There are four types of persons comparable to trees.

(22) Rūpappamāṇo, rūpappasanno, ghosappamāṇo, ghosappasanno.

One who measures by form; one who is pleased by form; one who measures by sound; one who is pleased by sound.

(23) Lūkhappamāṇo, lūkhappasanno[T 0.0136], dhammappamāṇo, dhammappasanno.

One who measures by austerity; one who is pleased by austerity; one who measures by the Dhamma; one who is pleased by the Dhamma.

(24) Atthekacco puggalo attahitāya paṭipanno hoti, no parahitāya; atthekacco puggalo parahitāya paṭipanno hoti, no attahitāya; atthekacco puggalo attahitāya ceva paṭipanno hoti parahitāya ca; atthekacco puggalo neva attahitāya paṭipanno hoti no parahitāya.

There is a certain person who is practicing for his own welfare, but not for the welfare of others. There is a certain person who is practicing for the welfare of others, but not for his own welfare. There is a certain person who is practicing for both his own welfare and for the welfare of others. There is a certain person who is practicing neither for his own welfare nor for the welfare of others.

(25) Atthekacco puggalo attantapo hoti attaparitāpanānuyogamanuyutto; atthekacco puggalo parantapo hoti paraparitāpanānuyogamanuyutto; atthekacco puggalo attantapo ca hoti attaparitāpanānuyogamanuyutto[M 0.0110], parantapo ca paraparitāpanānuyogamanuyutto; atthekacco puggalo neva attantapo hoti na attaparitāpanānuyogamanuyutto, na parantapo na paraparitāpanānuyogamanuyutto. So anattantapo aparantapo diṭṭheva dhamme nicchāto nibbuto sītībhūto sītibhūto (sī. ka.) sukhappaṭisaṃvedī brahmabhūtena attanā viharati.

There is a certain person who torments himself and is devoted to the practice of self-torment. There is a certain person who torments others and is devoted to the practice of tormenting others. There is a certain person who torments himself and is devoted to the practice of self-torment, and who torments others and is devoted to the practice of tormenting others. There is a certain person who neither torments himself nor is devoted to the practice of self-torment, and who neither torments others nor is devoted to the practice of tormenting others. That person, not tormenting himself and not tormenting others, right in this present life is without craving, extinguished, cooled, experiencing bliss, and dwells with a self that has become sublime.

(26) Sarāgo, sadoso, samoho, samāno.

One with lust; one with hatred; one with delusion; one with conceit.

(27) Atthekacco puggalo lābhī hoti ajjhattaṃ cetosamathassa, na lābhī adhipaññādhammavipassanāya; atthekacco [P 0.0008] puggalo lābhī hoti adhipaññādhammavipassanāya, na lābhī ajjhattaṃ cetosamathassa; atthekacco puggalo lābhī ceva hoti ajjhattaṃ cetosamathassa, lābhī ca adhipaññādhammavipassanāya; atthekacco puggalo neva lābhī hoti ajjhattaṃ cetosamathassa, na lābhī adhipaññādhammavipassanāya.

There is a certain person who obtains internal serenity of mind but does not obtain insight into phenomena through higher wisdom. There is a certain person who obtains insight into phenomena through higher wisdom but does not obtain internal serenity of mind. There is a certain person who obtains both internal serenity of mind and insight into phenomena through higher wisdom. There is a certain person who obtains neither internal serenity of mind nor insight into phenomena through higher wisdom.

(28) Anusotagāmī [V 0.0113] puggalo, paṭisotagāmī puggalo, ṭhitatto puggalo, tiṇṇo pāraṅgato pāragato (sī. syā.) thale tiṭṭhati brāhmaṇo.

The person who goes with the stream; the person who goes against the stream; the person with a steadfast self; and the brāhmaṇa who has crossed over, gone to the far shore, and stands on high ground.

(29) Appassuto [T 0.0137] sutena anupapanno, appassuto sutena upapanno, bahussuto sutena anupapanno, bahussuto sutena upapanno.

One of little learning, not endowed with learning; one of little learning, endowed with learning; one of much learning, not endowed with learning; one of much learning, endowed with learning.

(30) Samaṇamacalo, samaṇapadumo, samaṇapuṇḍarīko, samaṇesu samaṇasukhumālo.

An unshakeable recluse; a red lotus recluse; a white lotus recluse; and among recluses, a most refined recluse.

Catukkaṃ.

The Group of Four.

5. Pañcakauddeso

5. The Section on Fives

11. Pañca puggalā –

11. There are five types of persons—

(1) Atthekacco puggalo ārabhati ārambhati (sī. syā.) ca vippaṭisārī ca hoti, tañca cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti, yatthassa te uppannā pāpakā [M 0.0111] akusalā dhammā aparisesā nirujjhanti. Atthekacco puggalo ārabhati na vippaṭisārī ca hoti, tañca cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti, yatthassa te uppannā pāpakā akusalā dhammā aparisesā nirujjhanti. Atthekacco puggalo nārabhati vippaṭisārī ca hoti, tañca cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti, yatthassa te uppannā pāpakā akusalā dhammā aparisesā nirujjhanti. Atthekacco puggalo nārabhati na vippaṭisārī hoti, tañca cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti, yatthassa te uppannā pāpakā akusalā dhammā aparisesā nirujjhanti. Atthekacco puggalo nārabhati na vippaṭisārī hoti, tañca cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, yatthassa te uppannā pāpakā akusalā dhammā aparisesā [T 0.0138] nirujjhanti.

There is a certain person who commits an offense and is remorseful, and does not know as it really is that liberation of mind and liberation by wisdom wherein those evil, unwholesome states that have arisen in him cease without remainder. There is a certain person who commits an offense and is not remorseful, and does not know as it really is that liberation of mind and liberation by wisdom wherein those evil, unwholesome states that have arisen in him cease without remainder. There is a certain person who does not commit an offense and is remorseful, and does not know as it really is that liberation of mind and liberation by wisdom wherein those evil, unwholesome states that have arisen in him cease without remainder. There is a certain person who does not commit an offense and is not remorseful, and does not know as it really is that liberation of mind and liberation by wisdom wherein those evil, unwholesome states that have arisen in him cease without remainder. There is a certain person who does not commit an offense and is not remorseful, and knows as it really is that liberation of mind and liberation by wisdom wherein those evil, unwholesome states that have arisen in him cease without remainder.

(2) Datvā [P 0.0009][V 0.0114] avajānāti, saṃvāsena avajānāti, ādheyyamukho hoti, lolo hoti, mando momūho hoti.

Having given, he despises; through association, he despises; he is credulous; he is fickle; he is dull and deluded.

(3) Pañca yodhājīvūpamā puggalā.

Five persons comparable to professional soldiers.

(4) Pañca piṇḍapātikā.

Five who are alms-round-farers.

(5) Pañca khalupacchābhattikā.

Five who refuse food offered after having started to eat.

(6) Pañca ekāsanikā.

Five who are one-session-eaters.

(7) Pañca paṃsukūlikā.

Five who are refuse-rag-robe-wearers.

(8) Pañca tecīvarikā.

Five who are triple-robe-wearers.

(9) Pañca āraññikā.

Five who are forest-dwellers.

(10) Pañca rukkhamūlikā.

Five who are tree-root-dwellers.

(11) Pañca abbhokāsikā.

Five who are open-air-dwellers.

(12) Pañca nesajjikā.

Five who undertake the sitter's practice.

(13) Pañca yathāsanthatikā.

Five who are any-lodging-acceptors.

(14) Pañca sosānikā.

Five who are charnel-ground-dwellers.

Pañcakaṃ.

The Group of Five.

6. Chakkauddeso

6. The Section on Sixes

12. Cha [M 0.0112] puggalā –

12. Six persons—

(1) Atthekacco puggalo pubbe ananussutesu dhammesu sāmaṃ saccāni abhisambujjhati, tattha ca sabbaññutaṃ pāpuṇāti balesu phalesu (pī.) ca vasībhāvaṃ. Atthekacco puggalo pubbe ananussutesu dhammesu [V 0.0115] sāmaṃ saccāni abhisambujjhati, na ca tattha sabbaññutaṃ pāpuṇāti na ca balesu vasībhāvaṃ. Atthekacco puggalo pubbe ananussutesu dhammesu sāmaṃ saccāni anabhisambujjhati, diṭṭhe ceva dhamme dukkhassantakaro hoti sāvakapāramiñca pāpuṇāti. Atthekacco puggalo pubbe ananussutesu dhammesu sāmaṃ saccāni anabhisambujjhati[T 0.0139], diṭṭheva dhamme dukkhassantakaro hoti, na ca sāvakapāramiṃ pāpuṇāti. Atthekacco puggalo pubbe ananussutesu dhammesu sāmaṃ saccāni anabhisambujjhati, na ca diṭṭheva dhamme dukkhassantakaro hoti, anāgāmī hoti anāgantā anāgantvā (syā. ka.) a. ni. 4.171 itthattaṃ. Atthekacco puggalo pubbe ananussutesu dhammesu sāmaṃ saccāni anabhisambujjhati, na ca diṭṭheva dhamme dukkhassantakaro hoti, āgāmī sotāpannasakadāgāmī (syā. ka.) hoti āgantā itthattaṃ.

There is a certain person who, in regard to teachings unheard before, awakens by himself to the truths, and therein he attains omniscience and mastery over the powers. There is a certain person who, in regard to teachings unheard before, awakens by himself to the truths, but he does not therein attain omniscience nor mastery over the powers. There is a certain person who, in regard to teachings unheard before, does not awaken by himself to the truths, but he makes an end of suffering in this very life and attains the perfection of a disciple. There is a certain person who, in regard to teachings unheard before, does not awaken by himself to the truths, but he makes an end of suffering in this very life, yet does not attain the perfection of a disciple. There is a certain person who, in regard to teachings unheard before, does not awaken by himself to the truths, nor does he make an end of suffering in this very life; he is a non-returner, not coming back to this state of existence. There is a certain person who, in regard to teachings unheard before, does not awaken by himself to the truths, nor does he make an end of suffering in this very life; he is a returner, coming back to this state of existence.

Chakkaṃ.

The Group of Six.

7. Sattakauddeso

7. The Matrix of Sevens.

13. Satta puggalā –

13. Seven persons—

(1) Satta [P 0.0010] udakūpamā puggalā. Sakiṃ nimuggo nimuggova hoti, ummujjitvā nimujjati, ummujjitvā ṭhito hoti, ummujjitvā vipassati viloketi, ummujjitvā patarati, ummujjitvā paṭigādhappatto hoti, ummujjitvā tiṇṇo hoti pāraṅgato thale tiṭṭhati brāhmaṇo.

Seven persons with the simile of a swimmer: one who, having sunk once, remains sunk; one who, having emerged, sinks; one who, having emerged, stands still; one who, having emerged, looks intently and looks around; one who, having emerged, swims across; one who, having emerged, reaches a firm footing; one who, having emerged, has crossed over, has gone to the far shore, and as a brāhmaṇa, stands on the dry ground.

(2) Ubhatobhāgavimutto[M 0.0113], paññāvimutto, kāyasakkhī, diṭṭhippatto, saddhāvimutto, dhammānusārī, saddhānusārī.

One liberated in both ways, one liberated by wisdom, a body-witness, one who has attained to the view, one liberated by faith, a Dhamma-follower, a faith-follower.

Sattakaṃ.

The Group of Sevens.

8. Aṭṭhakauddeso

8. The Matrix of Eights.

14. Aṭṭha [V 0.0116] puggalā –

14. Eight persons—

(1) Cattāro maggasamaṅgino, cattāro phalasamaṅgino puggalā.

Four persons endowed with the path, and four persons endowed with the fruit.

Aṭṭhakaṃ.

The Group of Eights.

9. Navakauddeso

9. The Matrix of Nines.

15. Nava [T 0.0140] puggalā –

15. Nine persons—

(1) Sammāsambuddho, paccekasambuddho, ubhatobhāgavimutto, paññāvimutto, kāyasakkhī, diṭṭhippatto, saddhāvimutto, dhammānusārī, saddhānusārī.

The Perfectly Self-Enlightened One, a Paccekabuddha, one liberated in both ways, one liberated by wisdom, a body-witness, one who has attained to the view, one liberated by faith, a Dhamma-follower, a faith-follower.

Navakaṃ.

The Group of Nines.

10. Dasakauddeso

10. The Matrix of Tens.

16. Dasa puggalā –

16. Ten persons—

(1) Pañcannaṃ idha niṭṭhā, pañcannaṃ idha vihāya niṭṭhā.

Of five, the completion is here; of five, the completion is after having abandoned this existence.

Dasakaṃ.

The Group of Tens.

Puggalapaññattimātikā niṭṭhitā.

The Matrix of the Designation of Persons is completed.

Niddeso

Exposition

1. Ekakapuggalapaññatti

1. Designation of Persons: The Group of Ones

1. Katamo [P 0.0011][M 0.0114][V 0.0117] ca puggalo samayavimutto? Idhekacco puggalo kālena kālaṃ samayena samayaṃ aṭṭha vimokkhe kāyena phusitvā phassitvā (sī. pī.) viharati, paññāya cassa disvā ekacce āsavā parikkhīṇā honti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “samayavimutto”.

1. And which is the person liberated by occasion? Here, a certain person from time to time, on occasion, abides having attained the eight liberations with the body; and for him, having seen with wisdom, some cankers are destroyed. This person is called 'liberated by occasion'.

2. Katamo ca puggalo asamayavimutto? Idhekacco puggalo na [T 0.0141] heva kho kālena kālaṃ samayena samayaṃ aṭṭha vimokkhe kāyena phusitvā viharati, paññāya cassa disvā āsavā parikkhīṇā honti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “asamayavimutto”. Sabbepi ariyapuggalā ariye vimokkhe asamayavimuttā.

2. And which is the person liberated without occasion? Here, a certain person does not, from time to time and on occasion, dwell having touched the eight liberations with the body; but for him, having seen with wisdom, the taints are destroyed. This person is called 'liberated without occasion'. All noble persons are liberated without occasion in the noble liberation.

3. Katamo ca puggalo kuppadhammo? Idhekacco puggalo lābhī hoti rūpasahagatānaṃ vā arūpasahagatānaṃ vā samāpattīnaṃ. So ca kho na nikāmalābhī hoti na akicchalābhī na akasiralābhī; na yatthicchakaṃ yadicchakaṃ yāvaticchakaṃ samāpajjatipi vuṭṭhātipi. Ṭhānaṃ kho panetaṃ vijjati, yaṃ tassa puggalassa pamādamāgamma tā samāpattiyo kuppeyyuṃ – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “kuppadhammo”.

3. And which is the person of a nature to be shaken? Here, a certain person is an obtainer of the attainments associated with form or the formless. But he is not an obtainer at will, nor an obtainer without difficulty; he does not enter and emerge whenever he wishes, just as he wishes, for as long as he wishes. There is this possibility, that on account of that person's negligence, those attainments might be shaken. This person is called 'of a nature to be shaken'.

4. Katamo ca puggalo akuppadhammo? Idhekacco puggalo lābhī hoti rūpasahagatānaṃ vā arūpasahagatānaṃ vā samāpattīnaṃ. So ca kho nikāmalābhī hoti akicchalābhī akasiralābhī; yatthicchakaṃ yadicchakaṃ yāvaticchakaṃ samāpajjatipi vuṭṭhātipi. Aṭṭhānametaṃ anavakāso [V 0.0118] yaṃ tassa puggalassa pamādamāgamma tā samāpattiyo kuppeyyuṃ – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “akuppadhammo”. Sabbepi ariyapuggalā ariye vimokkhe akuppadhammā.

4. And which is the person of an unshakable nature? Here, a certain person is an obtainer of the attainments associated with form or the formless. He is an obtainer at will, an obtainer without difficulty, an obtainer without trouble; he enters and emerges whenever he wishes, just as he wishes, for as long as he wishes. This is impossible, there is no opportunity, that on account of that person's negligence, those attainments might be shaken. This person is called 'of an unshakable nature'. All noble persons are of an unshakable nature in the noble liberation.

5. Katamo ca puggalo parihānadhammo? Idhekacco puggalo lābhī hoti rūpasahagatānaṃ vā arūpasahagatānaṃ [P 0.0012] vā samāpattīnaṃ. So ca kho na nikāmalābhī hoti na akicchalābhī na akasiralābhī; na [M 0.0115] yatthicchakaṃ [T 0.0142] yadicchakaṃ yāvaticchakaṃ samāpajjatipi vuṭṭhātipi. Ṭhānaṃ kho panetaṃ vijjati, yaṃ so puggalo pamādamāgamma tāhi samāpattīhi parihāyeyya – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “parihānadhammo”.

5. And which is the person of a nature to decline? Here, a certain person is an obtainer of the attainments associated with form or the formless. But he is not an obtainer at will, not an obtainer without difficulty, not an obtainer without trouble; he does not enter and emerge whenever he wishes, just as he wishes, for as long as he wishes. There is this possibility, that on account of that person's negligence, he might decline from those attainments. This person is called 'of a nature to decline'.

6. Katamo ca puggalo aparihānadhammo? Idhekacco puggalo lābhī hoti rūpasahagatānaṃ vā arūpasahagatānaṃ vā samāpattīnaṃ. So ca kho nikāmalābhī hoti akicchalābhī akasiralābhī; yatthicchakaṃ yadicchakaṃ yāvaticchakaṃ samāpajjatipi vuṭṭhātipi. Aṭṭhānametaṃ anavakāso yaṃ so puggalo pamādamāgamma tāhi samāpattīhi parihāyeyya – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “aparihānadhammo”. Sabbepi ariyapuggalā ariye vimokkhe aparihānadhammā.

6. And which is the person of a nature not to decline? Here, a certain person is an obtainer of the attainments associated with form or the formless. He is an obtainer at will, an obtainer without difficulty, an obtainer without trouble; he enters and emerges whenever he wishes, just as he wishes, for as long as he wishes. This is impossible, there is no opportunity, that on account of that person's negligence, he might decline from those attainments. This person is called 'of a nature not to decline'. All noble persons are of a nature not to decline in the noble liberation.

7. Katamo ca puggalo cetanābhabbo? Idhekacco puggalo lābhī hoti rūpasahagatānaṃ vā arūpasahagatānaṃ vā samāpattīnaṃ. So ca kho na nikāmalābhī hoti na akicchalābhī na akasiralābhī; na yatthicchakaṃ yadicchakaṃ yāvaticchakaṃ samāpajjatipi vuṭṭhātipi. Sace anusañceteti, na parihāyati tāhi samāpattīhi. Sace na anusañceteti, parihāyati tāhi samāpattīhi – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “cetanābhabbo”.

7. And which is the person who is capable through volition? Here, a certain person is an obtainer of the attainments associated with form or the formless. But he is not an obtainer at will, not an obtainer without difficulty, not an obtainer without trouble; he does not enter and emerge whenever he wishes, just as he wishes, for as long as he wishes. If he makes an effort of will, he does not decline from those attainments. If he does not make an effort of will, he declines from those attainments. This person is called 'one who is capable through volition'.

8. Katamo ca puggalo anurakkhaṇābhabbo? Idhekacco puggalo lābhī hoti rūpasahagatānaṃ vā arūpasahagatānaṃ vā samāpattīnaṃ. So ca kho na nikāmalābhī hoti na akicchalābhī na akasiralābhī; na yatthicchakaṃ yadicchakaṃ yāvaticchakaṃ samāpajjatipi vuṭṭhātipi. Sace anurakkhati, na [T 0.0143] parihāyati tāhi samāpattīhi. Sace na anurakkhati, parihāyati tāhi samāpattīhi – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “anurakkhaṇābhabbo”.

8. And which is the person who is capable through guarding? Here, a certain person is an obtainer of the attainments associated with form or the formless. But he is not an obtainer at will, not an obtainer without difficulty, not an obtainer without trouble; he does not enter and emerge whenever he wishes, just as he wishes, for as long as he wishes. If he guards them, he does not decline from those attainments. If he does not guard them, he declines from those attainments. This person is called 'one who is capable through guarding'.

9. Katamo [V 0.0119] ca puggalo puthujjano? Yassa puggalassa tīṇi saṃyojanāni appahīnāni; na ca tesaṃ dhammānaṃ pahānāya paṭipanno – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “puthujjano”.

9. And which is the person who is a worldling? A person for whom the three fetters are unabandoned, and who has not entered upon the practice for the abandoning of those states. This person is called a 'worldling'.

10. Katamo ca puggalo gotrabhū? Yesaṃ [P 0.0013] dhammānaṃ samanantarā ariyadhammassa avakkanti hoti tehi dhammehi samannāgato – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “gotrabhū”.

10. And which is the person at the change-of-lineage? One who is endowed with those states immediately following which there is the entry into the noble state. This person is called 'one at the change-of-lineage'.

11. Katamo [M 0.0116] ca puggalo bhayūparato? Satta sekkhā bhayūparatā, ye ca puthujjanā sīlavanto. Arahā abhayūparato.

11. And which person is one who abstains from what is fearful? The seven trainees are those who abstain from what is fearful, as are those virtuous worldlings. The Arahant is one who has abstained from what is fearful.

12. Katamo ca puggalo abhabbāgamano? Ye te puggalā kammāvaraṇena samannāgatā, kilesāvaraṇena samannāgatā, vipākāvaraṇena samannāgatā, assaddhā acchandikā duppaññā eḷā, abhabbā niyāmaṃ okkamituṃ kusalesu dhammesu sammattaṃ – ime vuccanti puggalā “abhabbāgamanā”.

12. And which person is incapable of attainment? Those persons who are possessed of the obstruction of kamma, possessed of the obstruction of defilements, possessed of the obstruction of resultants; who are faithless, lacking in wholesome desire, unwise, mute and deaf; they are incapable of entering the fixed course of rightness in wholesome states. These persons are called 'incapable of attainment'.

13. Katamo ca puggalo bhabbāgamano? Ye te puggalā na kammāvaraṇena samannāgatā, na kilesāvaraṇena samannāgatā, na vipākāvaraṇena samannāgatā, saddhā chandikā paññavanto paññavantā (sī.) aneḷā, bhabbā niyāmaṃ okkamituṃ kusalesu dhammesu sammattaṃ – ime vuccanti puggalā “bhabbāgamanā”.

13. And which person is capable of attainment? Those persons who are not possessed of the obstruction of kamma, not possessed of the obstruction of defilements, not possessed of the obstruction of resultants; who have faith, have wholesome desire, are wise, and are not mute and deaf; they are capable of entering the fixed course of rightness in wholesome states. These persons are called 'capable of attainment'.

14. Katamo [T 0.0144] ca puggalo niyato? Pañca puggalā ānantarikā, ye ca micchādiṭṭhikā niyatā, aṭṭha ca ariyapuggalā niyatā. Avasesā puggalā aniyatā.

14. And which person is fixed? The five persons who have committed a heinous act and those with fixed wrong view are fixed. The eight noble persons are also fixed. The remaining persons are unfixed.

15. Katamo ca puggalo paṭipannako? Cattāro maggasamaṅgino puggalā paṭipannakā, cattāro phalasamaṅgino puggalā phale ṭhitā.

15. And which person is a practiser? The four persons endowed with the path are practisers; the four persons endowed with the fruit are established in the fruit.

16. Katamo ca puggalo samasīsī? Yassa puggalassa apubbaṃ acarimaṃ āsavapariyādānañca hoti jīvitapariyādānañca – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “samasīsī”.

16. And which person is a 'samasīsī'? A person for whom the exhaustion of the taints and the exhaustion of life occur neither before nor after one another. This person is called a 'samasīsī'.

17. Katamo [V 0.0120] ca puggalo ṭhitakappī? Ayañca puggalo sotāpattiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno assa, kappassa ca uḍḍayhanavelā assa, neva tāva kappo uḍḍayheyya yāvāyaṃ puggalo na sotāpattiphalaṃ sacchikaroti. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “ṭhitakappī”. Sabbepi [P 0.0014] maggasamaṅgino puggalā ṭhitakappino.

17. And which person is one who makes the aeon stand? Suppose this person is practising for the realization of the fruit of stream-entry, and it is the time for the aeon to burn up; the aeon will not burn up so long as this person has not realized the fruit of stream-entry. This person is called 'one who makes the aeon stand'. All persons endowed with the path are ones who make the aeon stand.

18. Katamo ca puggalo ariyo? Aṭṭha ariyapuggalā ariyā. Avasesā puggalā anariyā.

18. And which person is noble? The eight noble persons are noble. The remaining persons are not noble.

19. Katamo [M 0.0117] ca puggalo sekkho? Cattāro maggasamaṅgino tayo phalasamaṅgino puggalā “sekkhā”. Arahā asekkho. Avasesā puggalā nevasekkhanāsekkhā.

19. And which person is a trainee? The four persons endowed with the path and the three persons endowed with the fruit are trainees. The Arahant is one beyond training. The remaining persons are neither trainees nor ones beyond training.

20. Katamo ca puggalo tevijjo? Tīhi vijjāhi samannāgato puggalo [T 0.0145] “tevijjo”.

20. And which person is one with the threefold knowledge? A person endowed with the three knowledges is called 'one with the threefold knowledge'.

21. Katamo ca puggalo chaḷabhiñño? Chahi abhiññāhi samannāgato puggalo “chaḷabhiñño”.

21. And which person is one with the six supernormal knowledges? A person endowed with the six supernormal knowledges is called 'one with the six supernormal knowledges'.

22. Katamo ca puggalo sammāsambuddho? Idhekacco puggalo pubbe ananussutesu dhammesu sāmaṃ saccāni abhisambujjhati; tattha ca sabbaññutaṃ pāpuṇāti, balesu ca vasībhāvaṃ – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “sammāsambuddho”.

22. And which person is a Perfectly Enlightened One? Here, a certain person, regarding dhammas previously unheard, awakens fully to the truths by himself; and therein he attains omniscience and mastery in the powers. This person is called a 'Perfectly Enlightened One'.

23. Katamo ca puggalo paccekasambuddho? Idhekacco puggalo pubbe ananussutesu dhammesu sāmaṃ saccāni abhisambujjhati; na ca tattha sabbaññutaṃ pāpuṇāti, na ca balesu vasībhāvaṃ – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “paccekasambuddho”.

23. And which person is a Paccekasambuddha? Here, a certain person, regarding dhammas previously unheard, awakens fully to the truths by himself; but he does not therein attain omniscience, nor does he attain mastery in the powers. This person is called a 'Paccekasambuddha'.

24. Katamo ca puggalo ubhatobhāgavimutto? Idhekacco puggalo aṭṭha vimokkhe kāyena phusitvā viharati; paññāya cassa disvā āsavā parikkhīṇā honti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “ubhatobhāgavimutto”.

24. And which person is one liberated in both parts? Here, a certain person abides having touched the eight liberations with the mental body; and having seen with wisdom, their taints are destroyed. This person is called 'one liberated in both parts'.

25. Katamo [V 0.0121] ca puggalo paññāvimutto? Idhekacco puggalo na heva kho aṭṭha vimokkhe kāyena phusitvā viharati; paññāya cassa disvā āsavā parikkhīṇā honti. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “paññāvimutto”.

25. And which person is one liberated by wisdom? Here, a certain person does not abide having touched the eight liberations with the mental body; but having seen with wisdom, their taints are destroyed. This person is called 'one liberated by wisdom'.

26. Katamo ca puggalo kāyasakkhī? Idhekacco puggalo aṭṭha vimokkhe kāyena phusitvā viharati[P 0.0015]; paññāya cassa disvā ekacce [T 0.0146] āsavā parikkhīṇā honti. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “kāyasakkhī”.

26. And which person is a body-witness? Here, a certain person abides having touched the eight liberations with the mental body; and having seen with wisdom, some of their taints are destroyed. This person is called a 'body-witness'.

27. Katamo ca puggalo diṭṭhippatto? Idhekacco puggalo “idaṃ dukkha”nti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhasamudayo”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhanirodho”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti. Tathāgatappaveditā cassa [M 0.0118] dhammā paññāya vodiṭṭhā honti vocaritā. Paññāya cassa disvā ekacce āsavā parikkhīṇā honti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “diṭṭhippatto”.

27. And which person is one who has attained the view? Here, a certain person understands as it really is: 'This is suffering,' 'This is the origin of suffering,' 'This is the cessation of suffering,' 'This is the path leading to the cessation of suffering.' And the dhammas declared by the Tathāgata are well seen and well practiced by them with wisdom. Having seen with wisdom, some of their taints are destroyed. This person is called 'one who has attained the view'.

28. Katamo ca puggalo saddhāvimutto? Idhekacco puggalo “idaṃ dukkha”nti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhasamudayo”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhanirodho”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti. Tathāgatappaveditā cassa dhammā paññāya vodiṭṭhā honti vocaritā. Paññāya cassa disvā ekacce āsavā parikkhīṇā honti, no ca kho yathā diṭṭhippattassa – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “saddhāvimutto”.

28. And which person is one liberated by faith? Here, a certain person understands as it really is: 'This is suffering,' 'This is the origin of suffering,' 'This is the cessation of suffering,' 'This is the path leading to the cessation of suffering.' And the dhammas declared by the Tathāgata are well seen and well practiced by them with wisdom. Having seen with wisdom, some of their taints are destroyed, but not in the same way as for one who has attained the view. This person is called 'one liberated by faith'.

29. Katamo ca puggalo dhammānusārī? Yassa puggalassa sotāpattiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipannassa paññindriyaṃ adhimattaṃ hoti, paññāvāhiṃ paññāpubbaṅgamaṃ ariyamaggaṃ bhāveti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “dhammānusārī”. Sotāpattiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno puggalo dhammānusārī phale ṭhito diṭṭhippatto.

29. And which person is a Dhamma-follower? A person who is practising for the realization of the fruit of stream-entry, for whom the faculty of wisdom is predominant, develops the noble path that is borne by wisdom and preceded by wisdom. This person is called a 'Dhamma-follower'. The person practising for the realization of the fruit of stream-entry is a Dhamma-follower; one established in the fruit is one who has attained the view.

30. Katamo [T 0.0147] ca puggalo saddhānusārī? Yassa puggalassa sotāpattiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipannassa saddhindriyaṃ adhimattaṃ hoti, saddhāvāhiṃ saddhāpubbaṅgamaṃ ariyamaggaṃ bhāveti – ayaṃ vuccati [V 0.0122] puggalo “saddhānusārī”. Sotāpattiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno puggalo saddhānusārī phale ṭhito saddhāvimutto.

30. And which person is a faith-follower? A person who is practicing for the realization of the fruit of stream-entry, for whom the faculty of faith is predominant, develops the noble path that is borne by faith and preceded by faith. This person is called a 'faith-follower'. A person practicing for the realization of the fruit of stream-entry is a faith-follower; one established in the fruit is one liberated by faith.

31. Katamo ca puggalo sattakkhattuparamo? Idhekacco puggalo tiṇṇaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ parikkhayā sotāpanno [P 0.0016] hoti avinipātadhammo niyato sambodhiparāyano sambodhiparāyaṇo (sī. ka.). So sattakkhattuṃ deve ca mānuse ca sandhāvitvā saṃsaritvā dukkhassantaṃ karoti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “sattakkhattuparamo”.

31. And which person is a 'seven-times-at-most' person? Here, a certain person, with the destruction of the three fetters, becomes a stream-enterer, is of a nature not to fall into states of woe, is assured, and is bound for enlightenment. After transmigrating and wandering among devas and humans at most seven times, he makes an end of suffering. This person is called a 'seven-times-at-most' person.

32. Katamo ca puggalo kolaṅkolo? Idhekacco puggalo tiṇṇaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ parikkhayā sotāpanno hoti avinipātadhammo niyato [M 0.0119] sambodhiparāyano. So dve vā tīṇi vā kulāni sandhāvitvā saṃsaritvā dukkhassantaṃ karoti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “kolaṅkolo”.

32. And which person is a 'family-to-family' person? Here, a certain person, with the destruction of the three fetters, becomes a stream-enterer, is of a nature not to fall into states of woe, is assured, and is bound for enlightenment. After transmigrating and wandering through two or three families, he makes an end of suffering. This person is called a 'family-to-family' person.

33. Katamo ca puggalo ekabījī? Idhekacco puggalo tiṇṇaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ parikkhayā sotāpanno hoti avinipātadhammo niyato sambodhiparāyano. So ekaṃyeva mānusakaṃ bhavaṃ nibbattetvā dukkhassantaṃ karoti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “ekabījī”.

33. And which person is a 'one-seed' person? Here, a certain person, with the destruction of the three fetters, becomes a stream-enterer, is of a nature not to fall into states of woe, is assured, and is bound for enlightenment. After taking rebirth in just one human existence, he makes an end of suffering. This person is called a 'one-seed' person.

34. Katamo ca puggalo sakadāgāmī? Idhekacco puggalo tiṇṇaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ parikkhayā rāgadosamohānaṃ tanuttā sakadāgāmī hoti[T 0.0148], sakideva imaṃ lokaṃ āgantvā dukkhassantaṃ karoti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “sakadāgāmī”.

34. And which person is a once-returner? Here, a certain person, with the destruction of the three fetters and the attenuation of greed, hatred, and delusion, becomes a once-returner. Having come to this world only once, he makes an end of suffering. This person is called a 'once-returner'.

35. Katamo ca puggalo anāgāmī? Idhekacco puggalo pañcannaṃ orambhāgiyānaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ parikkhayā opapātiko hoti, tattha parinibbāyī anāvattidhammo tasmā lokā – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “anāgāmī”.

35. And what person is a non-returner? Here, a certain person, with the destruction of the five lower fetters, is of spontaneous birth, will attain Parinibbāna there, and is of a nature not to return from that world. This person is called a 'non-returner'.

36. Katamo ca puggalo antarāparinibbāyī? Idhekacco puggalo pañcannaṃ orambhāgiyānaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ parikkhayā opapātiko hoti, tattha parinibbāyī anāvattidhammo tasmā lokā. So upapannaṃ vā samanantarā appattaṃ vā vemajjhaṃ āyuppamāṇaṃ ariyamaggaṃ sañjaneti upariṭṭhimānaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ pahānāya – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “antarāparinibbāyī”.

36. And what person is one who attains Parinibbāna in the interval? Here, a certain person, with the destruction of the five lower fetters, is of spontaneous birth, will attain Parinibbāna there, and is of a nature not to return from that world. Immediately after being reborn or before reaching the midpoint of the lifespan, he generates the Noble Path for the abandonment of the higher fetters. This person is called 'one who attains Parinibbāna in the interval'.

37. Katamo [V 0.0123] ca puggalo upahaccaparinibbāyī? Idhekacco puggalo pañcannaṃ orambhāgiyānaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ [P 0.0017] parikkhayā opapātiko hoti, tattha parinibbāyī anāvattidhammo tasmā lokā. So atikkamitvā vemajjhaṃ āyuppamāṇaṃ upahacca vā kālakiriyaṃ kālaṃ kiriyaṃ (ka.) ariyamaggaṃ sañjaneti upariṭṭhimānaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ pahānāya – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “upahaccaparinibbāyī”.

37. And what person is one who attains Parinibbāna upon reaching? Here, a certain person, with the destruction of the five lower fetters, is of spontaneous birth, will attain Parinibbāna there, and is of a nature not to return from that world. Having surpassed the midpoint of the lifespan or upon reaching the time of death, he generates the Noble Path for the abandonment of the higher fetters. This person is called 'one who attains Parinibbāna upon reaching'.

38. Katamo ca puggalo asaṅkhāraparinibbāyī? Idhekacco puggalo pañcannaṃ orambhāgiyānaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ parikkhayā opapātiko hoti, tattha [T 0.0149] parinibbāyī [M 0.0120] anāvattidhammo tasmā lokā. So asaṅkhārena ariyamaggaṃ sañjaneti upariṭṭhimānaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ pahānāya – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “asaṅkhāraparinibbāyī”.

38. And what person is one who attains Parinibbāna without prompting? Here, a certain person, with the destruction of the five lower fetters, is of spontaneous birth, will attain Parinibbāna there, and is of a nature not to return from that world. Without prompting, he generates the Noble Path for the abandonment of the higher fetters. This person is called 'one who attains Parinibbāna without prompting'.

39. Katamo ca puggalo sasaṅkhāraparinibbāyī? Idhekacco puggalo pañcannaṃ orambhāgiyānaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ parikkhayā opapātiko hoti, tattha parinibbāyī anāvattidhammo tasmā lokā. So sasaṅkhārena ariyamaggaṃ sañjaneti upariṭṭhimānaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ pahānāya – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “sasaṅkhāraparinibbāyī”.

39. And what person is one who attains Parinibbāna with prompting? Here, a certain person, with the destruction of the five lower fetters, is of spontaneous birth, will attain Parinibbāna there, and is of a nature not to return from that world. With prompting, he generates the Noble Path for the abandonment of the higher fetters. This person is called 'one who attains Parinibbāna with prompting'.

40. Katamo ca puggalo uddhaṃsoto akaniṭṭhagāmī? Idhekacco puggalo pañcannaṃ orambhāgiyānaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ parikkhayā opapātiko hoti, tattha parinibbāyī anāvattidhammo tasmā lokā. So avihā cuto atappaṃ gacchati, atappā cuto sudassaṃ gacchati, sudassā cuto sudassiṃ gacchati, sudassiyā cuto akaniṭṭhaṃ gacchati; akaniṭṭhe ariyamaggaṃ sañjaneti upariṭṭhimānaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ pahānāya – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “uddhaṃsoto akaniṭṭhagāmī”.

40. And what person is one who goes upstream to Akaniṭṭha? Here, a certain person, with the destruction of the five lower fetters, is of spontaneous birth, will attain Parinibbāna there, and is of a nature not to return from that world. Having passed away from Avihā, he goes to Atappa. Having passed away from Atappa, he goes to Sudassa. Having passed away from Sudassa, he goes to Sudassī. Having passed away from Sudassī, he goes to Akaniṭṭha. In Akaniṭṭha, he generates the Noble Path for the abandonment of the higher fetters. This person is called 'one who goes upstream to Akaniṭṭha'.

41. Katamo ca puggalo sotāpanno sotāpattiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno? Tiṇṇaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ pahānāya paṭipanno puggalo sotāpattiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno. Yassa puggalassa tīṇi saṃyojanāni pahīnāni – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “sotāpanno”.

41. And which person is a stream-enterer, and which is one practicing for the realization of the fruit of stream-entry? A person practicing for the abandonment of the three fetters is one practicing for the realization of the fruit of stream-entry. A person for whom the three fetters have been abandoned is called a 'stream-enterer'.

42. Kāmarāgabyāpādānaṃ tanubhāvāya paṭipanno puggalo sakadāgāmiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno[V 0.0124]. Yassa [T 0.0150] puggalassa kāmarāgabyāpādā tanubhūtā – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “sakadāgāmī”.

42. A person practicing for the attenuation of sensual lust and ill will is one practicing for the realization of the fruit of once-returning. A person for whom sensual lust and ill will have become attenuated is called a 'once-returner'.

43. Kāmarāgabyāpādānaṃ anavasesappahānāya paṭipanno puggalo [P 0.0018] anāgāmiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno. Yassa puggalassa kāmarāgabyāpādā anavasesā pahīnā – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “anāgāmī”.

43. A person practicing for the remainderless abandonment of sensual lust and ill will is one practicing for the realization of the fruit of non-returning. A person for whom sensual lust and ill will have been abandoned without remainder is called a 'non-returner'.

44. Rūparāgaarūparāgamānauddhaccaavijjāya anavasesappahānāya paṭipanno puggalo arahattaphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno. Yassa puggalassa rūparāgo [M 0.0121] arūparāgo māno uddhaccaṃ avijjā anavasesā pahīnā – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “arahā”.

44. A person practicing for the remainderless abandonment of lust for form, lust for the formless, conceit, restlessness, and ignorance is one practicing for the realization of the fruit of Arahantship. A person for whom lust for form, lust for the formless, conceit, restlessness, and ignorance have been abandoned without remainder is called an 'Arahant'.

Ekakaniddeso.

The Exposition of the Ones.

2. Dukapuggalapaññatti

2. Designation of Persons in Pairs

45. Katamo ca puggalo kodhano? Tattha katamo kodho? Yo kodho kujjhanā kujjhitattaṃ doso dussanā dussitattaṃ dūsanā dūsitattaṃ (syā.) byāpatti byāpajjanā byāpajjitattaṃ virodho paṭivirodho caṇḍikkaṃ asuropo anattamanatā cittassa – ayaṃ vuccati kodho. Yassa puggalassa ayaṃ kodho appahīno – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “kodhano”.

45. And what person is an angry one? Herein, what is anger? Whatever is anger, being angry, the state of being angry; whatever is aversion, being averse, the state of being averse; whatever is ill will, the act of being ill-willed, the state of being ill-willed; whatever is opposition, resistance; whatever is ferocity, tearfulness, and displeasure of mind—this is called anger. A person in whom this anger is unabandoned is called an 'angry one'.

46. Katamo ca puggalo upanāhī? Tattha katamo upanāho? Pubbakālaṃ kodho aparakālaṃ upanāho. Yo evarūpo upanāho upanayhanā upanayhitattaṃ aṭṭhapanā āṭhapanā (ka.) vibha. 891 ṭhapanā saṇṭhapanā anusaṃsandanā anuppabandhanā daḷhīkammaṃ kodhassa – ayaṃ vuccati upanāho[T 0.0151]. Yassa puggalassa ayaṃ upanāho appahīno – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “upanāhī”.

46. And what person is a resentful one? Herein, what is resentment? Anger in a former time; resentment in a later time. Whatever such resentment, bearing resentment, the state of bearing resentment, establishing, fixing, settling, connecting in sequence, binding in sequence, the strengthening of anger—this is called resentment. A person in whom this resentment is unabandoned is called a 'resentful one'.

47. Katamo ca puggalo makkhī? Tattha katamo makkho? Yo makkho makkhāyanā makkhāyitattaṃ makkhīyanā makkhīyitattaṃ (sī.), makkhiyanā makkhiyitattaṃ (ka.) [V 0.0125] niṭṭhuriyaṃ niṭṭhuriyakammaṃ – ayaṃ vuccati makkho. Yassa puggalassa ayaṃ makkho appahīno – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “makkhī”.

47. And what person is a denigrator of others' virtue? Herein, what is denigration of others' virtue? Whatever is denigration of others' virtue, the act of denigrating others' virtue, the state of denigrating others' virtue; whatever is the rejection of others' virtue, the act of rejecting others' virtue—this is called denigration of others' virtue. A person in whom this denigration of others' virtue is unabandoned is called a 'denigrator of others' virtue'.

48. Katamo ca puggalo paḷāsī? Tattha katamo paḷāso? Yo paḷāso paḷāsāyanā paḷāsāyitattaṃ [P 0.0019] paḷāsāhāro vivādaṭṭhānaṃ yugaggāho appaṭinissaggo – ayaṃ vuccati paḷāso. Yassa puggalassa ayaṃ paḷāso appahīno – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “paḷāsī”.

48. And what person is a competitive one? Herein, what is competitiveness? Whatever is competitiveness, being competitive, the state of being competitive; whatever is competitive speech, grounds for dispute, and taking sides—this is called competitiveness. A person in whom this competitiveness is unabandoned is called a 'competitive one'.

49. Katamo [M 0.0122] ca puggalo issukī? Tattha katamā issā? Yā paralābhasakkāragarukāramānanavandanapūjanāsu issā issāyanā issāyitattaṃ usūyā usūyanā ussuyā ussuyanā (ka.) vibha. 893 usūyitattaṃ – ayaṃ vuccati issā. Yassa puggalassa ayaṃ issā appahīnā – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “issukī”.

49. And what person is an envious one? Herein, what is envy? Whatever is envy, being envious, the state of being envious; jealousy, being jealous, the state of being jealous, with regard to others’ gains, honor, respect, reverence, homage, and offerings—this is called envy. A person in whom this envy is unabandoned is called an 'envious one'.

50. Katamo ca puggalo maccharī? Tattha katamaṃ macchariyaṃ? Pañca macchariyāni – āvāsamacchariyaṃ, kulamacchariyaṃ, lābhamacchariyaṃ, vaṇṇamacchariyaṃ, dhammamacchariyaṃ. Yaṃ evarūpaṃ maccheraṃ maccharāyanā maccharāyitattaṃ vevicchaṃ kadariyaṃ kaṭukañcukatā aggahitattaṃ cittassa – idaṃ vuccati macchariyaṃ. Yassa puggalassa idaṃ macchariyaṃ appahīnaṃ – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “maccharī”.

50. And what person is a stingy one? Herein, what is stinginess? There are five kinds of stinginess: stinginess regarding dwellings, stinginess regarding families, stinginess regarding gains, stinginess regarding appearance, and stinginess regarding the Dhamma. Whatever is such stinginess, being stingy, the state of being stingy, reluctance, miserliness, acrimony, and the unaccepting state of the mind—this is called stinginess. A person in whom this stinginess is unabandoned is called a 'stingy one'.

51. Katamo ca puggalo saṭho? Tattha katamaṃ sāṭheyyaṃ? Idhekacco saṭho hoti parisaṭho. Yaṃ tattha saṭhaṃ saṭhatā sāṭheyyaṃ [T 0.0152] kakkaratā kakkariyaṃ kakkhaḷatā kakkhaḷiyaṃ (syā.) evaṃ khuddakavibhaṅgadukaniddesepi parikkhattattā pārikkhattiyaṃ – idaṃ vuccati sāṭheyyaṃ. Yassa puggalassa idaṃ sāṭheyyaṃ appahīnaṃ – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “saṭho”.

51. And what person is a deceitful one? Herein, what is deceitfulness? Here, a certain person is deceitful, thoroughly deceitful. Whatever is deceit, the state of being deceitful, craftiness, harshness, roughness, and guardedness—this is called deceitfulness. A person in whom this deceitfulness is unabandoned is called a 'deceitful one'.

52. Katamo ca puggalo māyāvī? Tattha katamā māyā? Idhekacco kāyena duccaritaṃ caritvā vācāya duccaritaṃ caritvā manasā duccaritaṃ caritvā tassa paṭicchādanahetu pāpikaṃ icchaṃ paṇidahati – “mā maṃ jaññā”ti icchati, “mā maṃ jaññā”ti saṅkappati “mā maṃ jaññā”ti vācaṃ bhāsati, “mā maṃ jaññā”ti kāyena parakkamati. Yā evarūpā māyā māyāvitā accāsarā vañcanā nikati vikiraṇā pariharaṇā gūhanā parigūhanā chādanā paṭicchādanā [V 0.0126] anuttānīkammaṃ anāvikammaṃ vocchādanā pāpakiriyā – ayaṃ vuccati māyā. Yassa puggalassa ayaṃ māyā appahīnā – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “māyāvī”.

52. And what person is a deceptive one? Herein, what is deception? Here, a certain person, having engaged in misconduct by body, speech, and mind, for the sake of concealing it, forms a wicked wish. He wishes, 'May they not know me.' He resolves, 'May they not know me.' He speaks, 'May they not know me.' He strives with his body, 'May they not know me.' Whatever is such deception, the state of being deceptive, tricking others, fraud, dissimulation, subterfuge, evasion, concealment, complete concealment, covering up, hiding, the act of not making things plain, the act of not making things manifest, thorough concealment, and evil action—this is called deception. A person in whom this deception is unabandoned is called a 'deceptive one'.

53. Katamo ca puggalo ahiriko? Tattha katamaṃ ahirikaṃ? Yaṃ [P 0.0020] na hirīyati hiriyitabbena na hirīyati pāpakānaṃ akusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ samāpattiyā – idaṃ vuccati ahirikaṃ. Iminā ahirikena samannāgato puggalo “ahiriko”.

53. And what person is a shameless one? Herein, what is shamelessness? It is not being ashamed of what is shameful; not being ashamed of the attainment of evil, unwholesome states—this is called shamelessness. A person endowed with this shamelessness is called a 'shameless one'.

54. Katamo [M 0.0123] ca puggalo anottappī? Tattha katamaṃ anottappaṃ? Yaṃ na ottappati ottappitabbena na ottappati pāpakānaṃ akusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ samāpattiyā – idaṃ vuccati anottappaṃ. Iminā anottappena samannāgato puggalo “anottappī”.

54. And what person is one without moral dread? Herein, what is lack of moral dread? It is not dreading what is dreadful; not dreading the attainment of evil, unwholesome states—this is called lack of moral dread. A person endowed with this lack of moral dread is called 'one without moral dread'.

55. Katamo ca puggalo dubbaco? Tattha katamo dovacassatā? Sahadhammike vuccamāne dovacassāyaṃ dovacassiyaṃ dovacassatā vippaṭikulaggāhitā [T 0.0153] vipaccanīkasātatā anādariyaṃ anādariyatā agāravatā appatissavatā – ayaṃ vuccati dovacassatā. Imāya dovacassatāya samannāgato puggalo “dubbaco”.

55. And what person is difficult to admonish? Herein, what is the state of being difficult to admonish? When being spoken to by companions in the holy life, whatever is the state of being difficult to admonish, the quality of being difficult to admonish, the fact of being difficult to admonish, the taking up of a contrary view, delighting in opposition, disrespect, the state of being disrespectful, lack of reverence, and unresponsiveness—this is called the state of being difficult to admonish. A person endowed with this state of being difficult to admonish is called 'difficult to admonish'.

56. Katamo ca puggalo pāpamitto? Tattha katamā pāpamittatā? Ye te puggalā assaddhā dussīlā appassutā maccharino duppaññā, yā tesaṃ sevanā nisevanā saṃsevanā bhajanā sambhajanā bhatti sambhatti sampavaṅkatā – ayaṃ vuccati pāpamittatā. Imāya pāpamittatāya samannāgato puggalo “pāpamitto”.

56. And what person is one who has evil friends? Herein, what is the state of having evil friends? Those persons who are faithless, immoral, of little learning, stingy, and unwise—whatever is the associating with them, frequenting them, keeping close company with them, serving them, serving them intimately, devotion to them, intimate devotion to them, and the state of inclining towards them—this is called the state of having evil friends. A person endowed with this state of having evil friends is called 'one who has evil friends'.

57. Katamo ca puggalo indriyesu aguttadvāro? Tattha katamā indriyesu aguttadvāratā? Idhekacco puggalo cakkhunā rūpaṃ disvā nimittaggāhī hoti anubyañjanaggāhī; yatvādhikaraṇamenaṃ cakkhundriyaṃ asaṃvutaṃ viharantaṃ abhijjhādomanassā pāpakā akusalā dhammā anvāssaveyyuṃ, tassa saṃvarāya na paṭipajjati, na rakkhati cakkhundriyaṃ, cakkhundriye na saṃvaraṃ āpajjati. Sotena saddaṃ sutvā…pe… ghānena gandhaṃ ghāyitvā…pe… jivhāya rasaṃ sāyitvā…pe… kāyena phoṭṭhabbaṃ phusitvā…pe… manasā dhammaṃ viññāya nimittaggāhī hoti anubyañjanaggāhī; yatvādhikaraṇamenaṃ manindriyaṃ asaṃvutaṃ viharantaṃ [V 0.0127] abhijjhādomanassā pāpakā akusalā dhammā anvāssaveyyuṃ, tassa saṃvarāya na paṭipajjati, na rakkhati manindriyaṃ, manindriye [P 0.0021] na saṃvaraṃ āpajjati. Yā imesaṃ channaṃ indriyānaṃ agutti agopanā anārakkho asaṃvaro – ayaṃ vuccati indriyesu aguttadvāratā. Imāya indriyesu aguttadvāratāya [T 0.0154] samannāgato puggalo “indriyesu aguttadvāro”.

57. And what person is one with unguarded doors in the sense faculties? Herein, what is the state of having unguarded doors in the sense faculties? Here, a certain person, seeing a form with the eye, grasps its sign and grasps its minor features. Because of which cause, while dwelling with the eye faculty unrestrained, evil, unwholesome states—covetousness and dejection—would flow in upon him, he does not practice for its restraint, does not guard the eye faculty, and does not achieve restraint in the eye faculty. Having heard a sound with the ear… having smelled an odor with the nose… having tasted a flavor with the tongue… having touched a tangible object with the body… having cognized a mental object with the mind, he grasps its sign and grasps its minor features. Because of which cause, while dwelling with the mind faculty unrestrained, evil, unwholesome states—covetousness and dejection—would flow in upon him, he does not practice for its restraint, does not guard the mind faculty, and does not achieve restraint in the mind faculty. Whatever, regarding these six sense faculties, is the lack of guarding, lack of protection, lack of watchfulness, and lack of restraint—this is called the state of having unguarded doors in the sense faculties. A person endowed with this state of having unguarded doors in the sense faculties is called 'one with unguarded doors in the sense faculties'.

58. Katamo ca puggalo bhojane amattaññū? Tattha katamā bhojane amattaññutā? Idhekacco puggalo appaṭisaṅkhā ayoniso āhāraṃ [M 0.0124] āhāreti davāya madāya maṇḍanāya vibhūsanāya, yā tattha asantuṭṭhitā amattaññutā appaṭisaṅkhā bhojane – ayaṃ vuccati bhojane amattaññutā. Imāya bhojane amattaññutāya samannāgato puggalo “bhojane amattaññū”.

58. And what person is one who does not know the measure in eating? Herein, what is not knowing the measure in eating? Here, a certain person, improperly and without reflection, consumes food for amusement, for intoxication, for adornment, for beautification. Whatever therein is the lack of contentment, the lack of knowing the measure, and the lack of reflection in eating—this is called not knowing the measure in eating. A person endowed with this lack of knowing the measure in eating is called 'one who does not know the measure in eating'.

59. Katamo ca puggalo muṭṭhassati? Tattha katamaṃ muṭṭhassaccaṃ? Yā assati ananussati appaṭissati assati assaraṇatā adhāraṇatā pilāpanatā sammusanatā – idaṃ vuccati muṭṭhassaccaṃ. Iminā muṭṭhassaccena samannāgato puggalo “muṭṭhassati”.

59. And what person is one with lapsed mindfulness? Herein, what is the state of lapsed mindfulness? Whatever is lack of mindfulness, non-recollection, non-remembrance, forgetfulness, non-retention, superficiality, and bewilderment—this is called the state of lapsed mindfulness. A person endowed with this state of lapsed mindfulness is called 'one with lapsed mindfulness'.

60. Katamo ca puggalo asampajāno? Tattha katamaṃ asampajaññaṃ? Yaṃ aññāṇaṃ adassanaṃ anabhisamayo ananubodho asambodho appaṭivedho asaṅgāhaṇā apariyogāhaṇā asaṃgāhanā apariyogāhanā (sī. syā. ka.) asamapekkhaṇā apaccavekkhaṇā apaccakkhakammaṃ dummejjhaṃ bālyaṃ asampajaññaṃ moho pamoho sammoho avijjā avijjogho avijjāyogo avijjānusayo avijjāpariyuṭṭhānaṃ avijjālaṅgī moho akusalamūlaṃ – idaṃ vuccati asampajaññaṃ. Iminā asampajaññena samannāgato puggalo “asampajāno”.

60. And what person is one without clear comprehension? Herein, what is the lack of clear comprehension? Whatever is not-knowing, not-seeing, not-realizing, not-understanding, not-awakening, not-penetrating, not-grasping, not-fathoming, not-considering, not-reflecting, not-directly-experiencing, dullness, foolishness, lack of clear comprehension, delusion, great delusion, utter delusion, ignorance, the flood of ignorance, the yoke of ignorance, the underlying tendency of ignorance, the obsession of ignorance, the bar of ignorance, and delusion which is an unwholesome root—this is called the lack of clear comprehension. A person endowed with this lack of clear comprehension is called 'one without clear comprehension'.

61. Katamo ca puggalo sīlavipanno? Tattha katamā sīlavipatti? Kāyiko vītikkamo vācasiko vītikkamo kāyikavācasiko vītikkamo – ayaṃ vuccati sīlavipatti. Sabbampi dussilyaṃ sīlavipatti. Imāya sīlavipattiyā [T 0.0155] samannāgato puggalo “sīlavipanno”.

61. And what person is one corrupted in virtue? Herein, what is the corruption of virtue? Bodily transgression, verbal transgression, and bodily and verbal transgression—this is called the corruption of virtue. All immorality is the corruption of virtue. A person endowed with this corruption of virtue is called 'one corrupted in virtue'.

62. Katamo ca puggalo diṭṭhivipanno? Tattha katamā diṭṭhivipatti? “Natthi dinnaṃ, natthi yiṭṭhaṃ, natthi hutaṃ, natthi sukatadukkaṭānaṃ sukaṭadukkaṭānaṃ (sī.) kammānaṃ phalaṃ vipāko, natthi ayaṃ loko[P 0.0022], natthi paro loko[V 0.0128], natthi mātā, natthi pitā, natthi sattā opapātikā, natthi loke samaṇabrāhmaṇā sammaggatā samaggatā (ka.) sammāpaṭipannā ye imañca lokaṃ parañca lokaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā pavedentī”ti. Yā evarūpā diṭṭhi diṭṭhigataṃ diṭṭhigahanaṃ diṭṭhikantāro diṭṭhivisūkāyikaṃ diṭṭhivipphanditaṃ diṭṭhisaṃyojanaṃ gāho paṭiggāho abhiniveso parāmāso kummaggo micchāpatho micchattaṃ titthāyatanaṃ vipariyāsaggāho vipariyesaggāho (sabbattha) padasiddhi cintetabbā, ayaṃ [M 0.0125] vuccati diṭṭhivipatti. Sabbāpi micchādiṭṭhi diṭṭhivipatti. Imāya diṭṭhivipattiyā samannāgato puggalo “diṭṭhivipanno”.

62. And what person is one corrupted in view? Herein, what is the corruption of view? 'There is no result of giving, no result of sacrifice, no result of offerings; there is no fruit or result of good and bad deeds; there is no this world, no other world; there is no mother, no father; there are no spontaneously arisen beings; there are in the world no ascetics or brahmins who are well-gone and well-practicing, who, having realized this world and the other world for themselves through direct knowledge, make it known.' Whatever is such a view, a tenet of view, a thicket of views, a wilderness of views, the spike of views, the agitation of views, the fetter of views; a grasping, a firm grasping, an adherence, a misapprehension, a wrong path, a false way, wrongness, a sectarian basis, a grasping of perversion—this is called the corruption of view. All wrong view is the corruption of view. A person endowed with this corruption of view is called 'one corrupted in view'.

63. Katamo ca puggalo ajjhattasaṃyojano? Yassa puggalassa pañcorambhāgiyāni saṃyojanāni appahīnāni – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “ajjhattasaṃyojano”.

63. And what person is one with internal fetters? A person for whom the five lower fetters are unabandoned—this person is called 'one with internal fetters'.

64. Katamo ca puggalo bahiddhāsaṃyojano? Yassa puggalassa pañcuddhambhāgiyāni saṃyojanāni appahīnāni – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “bahiddhāsaṃyojano”.

64. And what person is one with external fetters? A person for whom the five higher fetters are unabandoned—this person is called 'one with external fetters'.

65. Katamo ca puggalo akkodhano? Tattha katamo kodho? Yo kodho kujjhanā kujjhitattaṃ doso dussanā dussitattaṃ byāpatti byāpajjanā byāpajjitattaṃ virodho paṭivirodho caṇḍikkaṃ asuropo anattamanatā cittassa – ayaṃ vuccati kodho. Yassa puggalassa ayaṃ kodho pahīno [T 0.0156] – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “akkodhano”.

65. And what person is one without anger? Herein, what is anger? Whatever is anger, the act of being angry, the state of being angry; hatred, the act of hating, the state of hating; ill will, the act of being ill-disposed, the state of being ill-disposed; opposition, contrariness, harshness, irascibility, and displeasure of mind—this is called anger. A person for whom this anger is abandoned—this person is called 'one without anger'.

66. Katamo ca puggalo anupanāhī? Tattha katamo upanāho? Pubbakālaṃ kodho aparakālaṃ upanāho yo evarūpo upanāho upanayhanā upanayhitattaṃ aṭṭhapanā ṭhapanā saṇṭhapanā anusaṃsandanā anuppabandhanā daḷhīkammaṃ kodhassa – ayaṃ vuccati upanāho. Yassa puggalassa ayaṃ upanāho pahīno – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “anupanāhī”.

66. And what person is one without resentment? Herein, what is resentment? In a former time there is anger; in a later time there is resentment. Whatever is such resentment, the act of resenting, the state of being resentful; a placing, a setting down, a firm establishing, a continuous joining, a continuous binding, and the solidification of anger—this is called resentment. A person for whom this resentment is abandoned—this person is called 'one without resentment'.

67. Katamo ca puggalo amakkhī? Tattha katamo makkho? Yo makkho makkhāyanā makkhāyitattaṃ niṭṭhuriyaṃ niṭṭhuriyakammaṃ – ayaṃ vuccati makkho. Yassa puggalassa ayaṃ makkho pahīno – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “amakkhī”.

67. And what person is one without disparagement? Herein, what is disparagement? Whatever is disparagement, the act of disparaging, the state of being disparaging; harshness, the act of being harsh—this is called disparagement. A person for whom this disparagement is abandoned—this person is called 'one without disparagement'.

68. Katamo ca puggalo apaḷāsī? Tattha katamo paḷāso? Yo paḷāso paḷāsāyanā paḷāsāyitattaṃ [V 0.0129] paḷāsāhāro vivādaṭṭhānaṃ yugaggāho appaṭinissaggo – ayaṃ vuccati paḷāso. Yassa puggalassa ayaṃ paḷāso pahīno – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “apaḷāsī”.

68. And what person is without contentiousness? Herein, what is contentiousness? That which is contentiousness, the manner of being contentious, the state of being contentious, the bearing of contentiousness, a ground for dispute, a stubborn grasp, non-relinquishing—this is called contentiousness. The person in whom this contentiousness is abandoned is called a person 'without contentiousness'.

69. Katamo [P 0.0023][M 0.0126] ca puggalo anissukī? Tattha katamā issā? Yā paralābhasakkāragarukāramānanavandanapūjanāsu issā issāyanā issāyitattaṃ usūyā usūyanā usūyitattaṃ – ayaṃ vuccati issā. Yassa puggalassa ayaṃ issā pahīnā – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “anissukī”.
