69. And what person is without envy? Herein, what is envy? The envy, the manner of being envious, the state of being envious, the jealousy, the manner of being jealous, the state of being jealous in regard to others' gains, honor, respect, reverence, homage, and veneration—this is called envy. The person in whom this envy is abandoned is called a person 'without envy'.

70. Katamo ca pugalo amaccharī? Tattha katamaṃ macchariyaṃ? Pañca macchariyāni – āvāsamacchariyaṃ, kulamacchariyaṃ, lābhamacchariyaṃ, vaṇṇamacchariyaṃ, dhammamacchariyaṃ. Yaṃ evarūpaṃ maccheraṃ maccharāyanā maccharāyitattaṃ vevicchaṃ kadariyaṃ kaṭukañcukatā [T 0.0157] aggahitattaṃ cittassa – idaṃ vuccati macchariyaṃ. Yassa puggalassa idaṃ macchariyaṃ pahīnaṃ – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “amaccharī”.

70. And what person is without miserliness? Herein, what is miserliness? There are five kinds of miserliness: miserliness in regard to dwellings, miserliness in regard to families, miserliness in regard to gains, miserliness in regard to appearance, and miserliness in regard to the Dhamma. Whatever miserliness of this sort, the manner of being miserly, the state of being miserly, illiberality, stinginess, a bitter disposition, a state of un-receptiveness of mind—this is called miserliness. The person in whom this miserliness is abandoned is called a person 'without miserliness'.

71. Katamo ca puggalo asaṭho? Tattha katamaṃ sāṭheyyaṃ? Idhekacco saṭho hoti parisaṭho. Yaṃ tattha saṭhaṃ saṭhatā sāṭheyyaṃ kakkaratā kakkariyaṃ parikkhattatā pārikkhattiyaṃ – idaṃ vuccati sāṭheyyaṃ. Yassa puggalassa idaṃ sāṭheyyaṃ pahīnaṃ – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “asaṭho”.

71. And what person is without deceit? Herein, what is deceit? Here, some person is deceitful, thoroughly deceitful. That which is deceit, the state of being deceitful, fraud, harshness, the state of being harsh, deviousness, the state of being devious—this is called deceit. The person in whom this deceit is abandoned is called a person 'without deceit'.

72. Katamo ca puggalo amāyāvī? Tattha katamā māyā? Idhekacco puggalo kāyena duccaritaṃ caritvā vācāya duccaritaṃ caritvā manasā duccaritaṃ caritvā tassa paṭicchādanahetu pāpikaṃ icchaṃ paṇidahati – “mā maṃ jaññā”ti icchati, “mā maṃ jaññā”ti saṅkappati, “mā maṃ jaññā”ti vācaṃ bhāsati, “mā maṃ jaññā”ti kāyena parakkamati. Yā evarūpā māyā māyāvitā accāsarā vañcanā nikati vikiraṇā pariharaṇā gūhanā parigūhanā chādanā paṭicchādanā anuttānīkammaṃ anāvikammaṃ vocchādanā pāpakiriyā – ayaṃ vuccati māyā. Yassa puggalassa ayaṃ māyā pahīnā – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “amāyāvī”.

72. And what person is without trickery? Herein, what is trickery? Here, a certain person, having committed misconduct by body, speech, or mind, makes a wicked wish for the sake of concealing it: 'May no one know me,' he wishes; 'May no one know me,' he resolves; 'May no one know me,' he speaks; 'May no one know me,' he strives with his body. Whatever trickery of this sort—craftiness, transgressive deception, fraud, cheating, duplicity, evasion, hiding, thorough hiding, concealing, covering up, the act of not making manifest, the act of not making clear, obscuring, evil action—this is called trickery. The person in whom this trickery is abandoned is called a person 'without trickery'.

73. Katamo [V 0.0130] ca puggalo hirimā? Tattha katamā hirī? Yaṃ [P 0.0024] hirīyati hiriyitabbena hirīyati pāpakānaṃ akusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ samāpattiyā – ayaṃ vuccati hirī. Imāya hiriyā samannāgato puggalo “hirimā”.

73. And what person has moral shame? Herein, what is moral shame? It is to be ashamed of what ought to be ashamed of; to be ashamed of the attainment of evil, unwholesome states—this is called moral shame. A person endowed with this moral shame is called 'one with moral shame'.

74. Katamo ca puggalo ottappī? Tattha katamaṃ ottappaṃ? Yaṃ ottappati ottappitabbena ottappati pāpakānaṃ akusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ samāpattiyā – idaṃ [T 0.0158] vuccati ottappaṃ. Iminā ottappena samannāgato puggalo “ottappī”.

74. And what person has moral dread? Herein, what is moral dread? It is to dread what ought to be dreaded; to dread the attainment of evil, unwholesome states—this is called moral dread. A person endowed with this moral dread is called 'one with moral dread'.

75. Katamo [M 0.0127] ca puggalo suvaco? Tattha katamā sovacassatā? Sahadhammike vuccamāne sovacassāyaṃ sovacassiyaṃ sovacassatā avippaṭikulaggāhitā avipaccanīkasātatā sādariyaṃ sādariyatā sagāravatā sappatissavatā – ayaṃ vuccati sovacassatā. Imāya sovacassatāya samannāgato puggalo “suvaco”.

75. And what person is easy to admonish? Herein, what is amenability? When one is being addressed by spiritual companions, it is the ease of being admonished, the manner of being amenable, the state of being amenable, not taking up a contrary view, not delighting in opposition, deference, the state of being deferential, respectfulness, and compliance—this is called amenability. A person endowed with this amenability is called 'one who is easy to admonish'.

76. Katamo ca puggalo kalyāṇamitto? Tattha katamā kalyāṇamittatā? Ye te puggalā saddhā sīlavanto bahussutā cāgavanto paññavanto, yā tesaṃ sevanā nisevanā saṃsevanā bhajanā sambhajanā bhatti sambhatti sampavaṅkatā – ayaṃ vuccati kalyāṇamittatā. Imāya kalyāṇamittatāya samannāgato puggalo “kalyāṇamitto”.

76. And what person has admirable friends? Herein, what is admirable friendship? It is associating with, frequenting, keeping company with, resorting to, consorting with, devotion to, complete devotion to, and inclining towards those persons who are faithful, virtuous, learned, generous, and wise—this is called admirable friendship. A person endowed with this admirable friendship is called 'one who has admirable friends'.

77. Katamo ca puggalo indriyesu guttadvāro? Tattha katamā indriyesu guttadvāratā? Idhekacco puggalo cakkhunā rūpaṃ disvā na nimittaggāhī hoti nānubyañjanaggāhī; yatvādhikaraṇamenaṃ cakkhundriyaṃ asaṃvutaṃ viharantaṃ abhijjhādomanassā pāpakā akusalā dhammā anvāssaveyyuṃ, tassa saṃvarāya paṭipajjati, rakkhati cakkhundriyaṃ cakkhundriye saṃvaraṃ āpajjati. Sotena saddaṃ sutvā…pe… ghānena gandhaṃ ghāyitvā…pe… jivhāya rasaṃ sāyitvā…pe… kāyena phoṭṭhabbaṃ phusitvā…pe… manasā dhammaṃ viññāya na nimittaggāhī hoti nānubyañjanaggāhī; yatvādhikaraṇamenaṃ manindriyaṃ asaṃvutaṃ viharantaṃ [T 0.0159] abhijjhādomanassā pāpakā akusalā dhammā anvāssaveyyuṃ, tassa saṃvarāya paṭipajjati, rakkhati manindriyaṃ, manindriye saṃvaraṃ āpajjati. Yā imesaṃ channaṃ indriyānaṃ gutti gopanā ārakkho saṃvaro [P 0.0025] – ayaṃ [V 0.0131] vuccati indriyesu guttadvāratā. Imāya indriyesu guttadvāratāya samannāgato puggalo “indriyesu guttadvāro”.

77. And what person has guarded sense doors? Herein, what is the guarding of the sense doors? Here, a certain person, on seeing a form with the eye, does not grasp at its signs and features. Since, if he were to dwell with the eye faculty unrestrained, evil unwholesome states of covetousness and displeasure might invade him, he practices for its restraint, guards the eye faculty, and undertakes the restraint of the eye faculty. On hearing a sound with the ear… on smelling an odor with the nose… on tasting a flavor with the tongue… on feeling a tactile object with the body… on cognizing a mental phenomenon with the mind, he does not grasp at its signs and features. Since, if he were to dwell with the mind faculty unrestrained, evil unwholesome states of covetousness and displeasure might invade him, he practices for its restraint, guards the mind faculty, and undertakes the restraint of the mind faculty. This guarding, protecting, watching over, and restraint of these six faculties—this is called guarding the sense doors. A person endowed with this guarding of the sense doors is called 'one with guarded sense doors'.

78. Katamo ca puggalo bhojane mattaññū? Tattha katamā bhojane mattaññutā? Idhekacco puggalo paṭisaṅkhā yoniso āhāraṃ āhāreti – “neva davāya, na madāya, na maṇḍanāya, na vibhūsanāya; yāvadeva imassa kāyassa ṭhitiyā yāpanāya vihiṃsūparatiyā brahmacariyānuggahāya. Iti purāṇañca vedanaṃ paṭihaṅkhāmi, navañca vedanaṃ na uppādessāmi, yātrā ca me bhavissati anavajjatā ca phāsuvihāro cā”ti. Yā tattha santuṭṭhitā mattaññutā [M 0.0128] paṭisaṅkhā bhojane – ayaṃ vuccati bhojane mattaññutā. Imāya bhojane mattaññutāya samannāgato puggalo “bhojane mattaññū”.

78. And what person is moderate in eating? Herein, what is moderation in eating? Here, a certain person, reflecting wisely, takes food—not for amusement, not for intoxication, not for adornment, not for embellishment, but only for the endurance and maintenance of this body, for the cessation of affliction, and for the support of the holy life, thinking: 'Thus I shall ward off the old feeling and not give rise to a new feeling. And for me there will be maintenance, blamelessness, and dwelling in comfort.' Whatever contentment, moderation, and reflection there is in regard to food—this is called moderation in eating. A person endowed with this moderation in eating is called 'one who is moderate in eating'.

79. Katamo ca puggalo upaṭṭhitassati? Tattha katamā sati? Yā sati anussati paṭissati sati saraṇatā dhāraṇatā apilāpanatā asammusanatā sati satindriyaṃ satibalaṃ sammāsati – ayaṃ vuccati sati. Imāya satiyā samannāgato puggalo “upaṭṭhitassati”.

79. And what person has established mindfulness? Herein, what is mindfulness? That which is mindfulness, recollection, repeated recollection, mindfulness, the state of remembering, the state of bearing, non-superficiality, non-forgetfulness, mindfulness, the faculty of mindfulness, the power of mindfulness, right mindfulness—this is called mindfulness. A person endowed with this mindfulness is called 'one with established mindfulness'.

80. Katamo ca puggalo sampajāno? Tattha katamaṃ sampajaññaṃ? Yā paññā pajānanā vicayo pavicayo dhammavicayo sallakkhaṇā upalakkhaṇā paccupalakkhaṇā paṇḍiccaṃ kosallaṃ nepuññaṃ vebhabyā cintā upaparikkhā bhūrī medhā pariṇāyikā vipassanā sampajaññaṃ [T 0.0160] patodo paññā paññindriyaṃ paññābalaṃ paññāsatthaṃ paññāpāsādo paññāāloko paññāobhāso paññāpajjoto paññāratanaṃ amoho dhammavicayo sammādiṭṭhi – idaṃ vuccati sampajaññaṃ. Iminā sampajaññena samannāgato puggalo “sampajāno”.

80. And what person is clearly comprehending? Herein, what is clear comprehension? That which is wisdom, understanding, investigation, thorough investigation, investigation of phenomena, discrimination, differentiation, detailed differentiation, erudition, skillfulness, subtlety, sagacity, reflection, scrutiny, breadth, intelligence, guidance, insight, clear comprehension, a goad; wisdom, the faculty of wisdom, the power of wisdom, the sword of wisdom, the mansion of wisdom, the light of wisdom, the radiance of wisdom, the lamp of wisdom, the jewel of wisdom; non-delusion, investigation of phenomena, right view—this is called clear comprehension. A person endowed with this clear comprehension is called 'one who is clearly comprehending'.

81. Katamo ca puggalo sīlasampanno? Tattha katamā sīlasampadā? Kāyiko avītikkamo vācasiko avītikkamo kāyikavācasiko avītikkamo – ayaṃ vuccati sīlasampadā. Sabbopi sīlasaṃvaro sīlasampadā. Imāya sīlasampadāya samannāgato puggalo “sīlasampanno”.

81. And what person is accomplished in virtue? Herein, what is accomplishment in virtue? Non-transgression by body, non-transgression by speech, non-transgression by body and speech—this is called accomplishment in virtue. All restraint in regard to virtue is accomplishment in virtue. A person endowed with this accomplishment in virtue is called 'one accomplished in virtue'.

82. Katamo ca puggalo diṭṭhisampanno? Tattha [P 0.0026] katamā diṭṭhisampadā? “Atthi dinnaṃ, atthi [V 0.0132] yiṭṭhaṃ, atthi hutaṃ, atthi sukatadukkaṭānaṃ kammānaṃ phalaṃ vipāko, atthi ayaṃ loko, atthi paro loko, atthi mātā, atthi pitā, atthi sattā opapātikā, atthi loke samaṇabrāhmaṇā sammaggatā sammāpaṭipannā ye imañca lokaṃ parañca lokaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā pavedentī”ti. Yā evarūpā paññā pajānanā…pe… amoho dhammavicayo sammādiṭṭhi – ayaṃ vuccati diṭṭhisampadā. Sabbāpi sammādiṭṭhi diṭṭhisampadā. Imāya diṭṭhisampadāya samannāgato puggalo “diṭṭhisampanno”.

82. And what person is accomplished in view? Therein, what is the accomplishment in view? 'There is a result from what is given, what is offered, and what is sacrificed; there is fruit and result of good and bad actions; there is this world and the other world; there is the result of conduct towards one's mother and father; there are beings of spontaneous birth; there are in the world ascetics and brahmins who have gone the right way and are practicing the right way, who declare this world and the other world after having realized them for themselves by direct knowledge.' Such wisdom, understanding... non-delusion, investigation of phenomena, right view—this is called the accomplishment in view. All right view is accomplishment in view. A person endowed with this accomplishment in view is called 'accomplished in view.'

83. Katame dve puggalā dullabhā lokasmiṃ? Yo ca pubbakārī, yo ca kataññū katavedī – ime dve puggalā dullabhā lokasmiṃ.

83. What two persons are rare in the world? One who does a kindness first, and one who is grateful and acknowledges what has been done—these two persons are rare in the world.

84. Katame [M 0.0129] dve puggalā duttappayā? Yo ca laddhaṃ laddhaṃ nikkhipati, yo ca laddhaṃ laddhaṃ vissajjeti – ime dve puggalā “duttappayā”.

84. What two persons are hard to satisfy? One who hoards whatever he gets, and one who gives away whatever he gets—these two persons are hard to satisfy.

85. Katame [T 0.0161] dve puggalā sutappayā? Yo ca laddhaṃ laddhaṃ na nikkhipati, yo ca laddhaṃ laddhaṃ na vissajjeti – ime dve puggalā “sutappayā”.

85. What two persons are easy to satisfy? One who does not hoard whatever he gets, and one who does not give away whatever he gets—these two persons are easy to satisfy.

86. Katamesaṃ dvinnaṃ puggalānaṃ āsavā vaḍḍhanti? Yo ca na kukkuccāyitabbaṃ kukkuccāyati, yo ca kukkuccāyitabbaṃ na kukkuccāyati – imesaṃ dvinnaṃ puggalānaṃ āsavā vaḍḍhanti.

86. For which two persons do the taints increase? For one who feels remorse over what is not a matter for remorse, and for one who does not feel remorse over what is a matter for remorse—for these two persons the taints increase.

87. Katamesaṃ dvinnaṃ puggalānaṃ āsavā na vaḍḍhanti? Yo ca na kukkuccāyitabbaṃ na kukkuccāyati, yo ca kukkuccāyitabbaṃ kukkuccāyati – imesaṃ dvinnaṃ puggalānaṃ āsavā na vaḍḍhanti.

87. For which two persons do the taints not increase? For one who does not feel remorse over what is not a matter for remorse, and for one who feels remorse over what is a matter for remorse—for these two persons the taints do not increase.

88. Katamo ca puggalo hīnādhimutto? Idhekacco puggalo dussīlo hoti pāpadhammo, so aññaṃ dussīlaṃ pāpadhammaṃ sevati bhajati payirupāsati – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “hīnādhimutto”.

88. And what person is of inferior inclination? Here, a certain person is immoral and of evil character; he associates with, resorts to, and attends on another who is immoral and of evil character. This person is called 'one of inferior inclination.'

89. Katamo [V 0.0133] ca puggalo paṇītādhimutto? Idhekacco puggalo sīlavā hoti kalyāṇadhammo, so aññaṃ sīlavantaṃ kalyāṇadhammaṃ sevati bhajati payirupāsati – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “paṇītādhimutto”.

89. And what person is of sublime inclination? Here, a certain person is virtuous and of good character; he associates with, resorts to, and attends on another who is virtuous and of good character. This person is called 'one of sublime inclination.'

90. Katamo [P 0.0027] ca puggalo titto? Paccekasambuddhā paccekabuddho (sī.) ye ca tathāgatassa sāvakā arahanto tittā. Sammāsambuddho titto ca tappetā ca tappetā ca, ayaṃ vuccati puggalo titto (sī.).

90. And what person is satisfied? Paccekasambuddhas and those disciples of the Tathāgata who are arahants are satisfied. A Sammāsambuddha is both satisfied and one who satisfies others.

Dukaniddeso.

The Exposition of the Twos.

3. Tikapuggalapaññatti

3. Designation of Persons in Triads

91. Katamo [T 0.0162] ca puggalo nirāso? Idhekacco puggalo dussīlo hoti pāpadhammo asuci saṅkassarasamācāro paṭicchannakammanto assamaṇo [M 0.0130] samaṇapaṭiñño abrahmacārī brahmacāripaṭiñño antopūti avassuto kasambujāto. So suṇāti – “itthannāmo kira bhikkhu āsavānaṃ khayā anāsavaṃ cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ diṭṭheva dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharatī”ti. Tassa na evaṃ hoti – “kudāssu nāmāhampi āsavānaṃ khayā anāsavaṃ cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ diṭṭheva dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharissāmī”ti. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “nirāso”.

91. And what person is without hope? Here, a certain person is immoral, of evil nature, impure, of suspect behavior, secretive in his actions, not a recluse though claiming to be one, not practicing the holy life though claiming to do so, rotten within, full of defilements, a refuse heap. He hears: “It seems that the monk of such and such a name, by the destruction of the taints, has realized for himself with direct knowledge, in this very life, the taintless liberation of mind, liberation by wisdom, and having entered upon it, abides in it.” But it does not occur to him: “When will I too, by the destruction of the taints, realize for myself with direct knowledge, in this very life, the taintless liberation of mind, liberation by wisdom, and having entered upon it, abide in it?” This is called a person “without hope.”

92. Katamo ca puggalo āsaṃso? Idhekacco puggalo sīlavā hoti kalyāṇadhammo. So suṇāti – “itthannāmo kira bhikkhu āsavānaṃ khayā anāsavaṃ cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ diṭṭheva dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharatī”ti. Tassa evaṃ hoti – “kudāssu nāmāhampi āsavānaṃ khayā anāsavaṃ cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ diṭṭheva dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharissāmī”ti. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “āsaṃso”.

92. And what person is hopeful? Here, a certain person is virtuous and of good character. He hears: “It seems that the monk of such and such a name, by the destruction of the taints, has realized for himself with direct knowledge, in this very life, the taintless liberation of mind, liberation by wisdom, and having entered upon it, abides in it.” It occurs to him: “When, indeed, will I too, by the destruction of the taints, realize for myself with direct knowledge, in this very life, the taintless liberation of mind, liberation by wisdom, and having entered upon it, abide in it?” This is called a “hopeful” person.

93. Katamo ca puggalo vigatāso? Idhekacco puggalo āsavānaṃ khayā anāsavaṃ cetovimuttiṃ [V 0.0134] paññāvimuttiṃ diṭṭheva dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharati. So suṇāti – “itthannāmo kira bhikkhu āsavānaṃ khayā anāsavaṃ cetovimuttiṃ [T 0.0163] paññāvimuttiṃ diṭṭheva dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharatī”ti. Tassa na evaṃ hoti – “kudāssu nāmāhampi āsavānaṃ khayā anāsavaṃ cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ diṭṭheva dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharissāmī”ti. Taṃ kissa hetu? Yā hissa pubbe avimuttassa vimuttāsā, sā paṭippassaddhā. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “vigatāso”.

93. And what person is hope-freed? Here, a certain person, by the destruction of the taints, has realized for himself with direct knowledge, in this very life, the taintless liberation of mind, liberation by wisdom, and having entered upon it, abides in it. He hears: “It seems that the monk of such and such a name, by the destruction of the taints, has realized for himself with direct knowledge, in this very life, the taintless liberation of mind, liberation by wisdom, and having entered upon it, abides in it.” It does not occur to him: “When, indeed, will I too, by the destruction of the taints, realize for myself with direct knowledge, in this very life, the taintless liberation of mind, liberation by wisdom, and having entered upon it, abide in it?” For what reason? Because his former hope for liberation, which he had when he was not yet liberated, has subsided. This is called a person who is 'hope-freed'.

94. Tattha katame tayo gilānūpamā puggalā? Tayo gilānā – idhekacco gilāno labhanto vā sappāyāni bhojanāni [P 0.0028] alabhanto vā sappāyāni bhojanāni, labhanto vā sappāyāni bhesajjāni alabhanto vā sappāyāni bhesajjāni, labhanto vā patirūpaṃ upaṭṭhākaṃ alabhanto vā patirūpaṃ upaṭṭhākaṃ, neva vuṭṭhāti tamhā ābādhā. (1)

94. Therein, what are the three persons comparable to a sick person? There are three kinds of sick persons. Here, a certain sick person, whether or not he obtains suitable food, whether or not he obtains suitable medicine, whether or not he obtains a suitable attendant, will not recover from that illness.

Idha panekacco gilāno labhanto vā sappāyāni bhojanāni alabhanto vā sappāyāni bhojanāni, labhanto vā sappāyāni bhesajjāni [M 0.0131] alabhanto vā sappāyāni bhesajjāni, labhanto vā patirūpaṃ upaṭṭhākaṃ alabhanto vā patirūpaṃ upaṭṭhākaṃ, vuṭṭhāti tamhā ābādhā. (2)

Again, here, a certain sick person, whether or not he obtains suitable food, whether or not he obtains suitable medicine, whether or not he obtains a suitable attendant, recovers from that illness.

Idha panekacco gilāno labhanto sappāyāni bhojanāni no alabhanto, labhanto sappāyāni bhesajjāni no alabhanto, labhanto patirūpaṃ upaṭṭhākaṃ no alabhanto, vuṭṭhāti tamhā ābādhā. (3)

Here, a certain sick person recovers from that illness upon obtaining suitable food, but not upon failing to obtain it; upon obtaining suitable medicine, but not upon failing to obtain it; and upon obtaining a suitable attendant, but not upon failing to obtain one.

Tatra yvāyaṃ gilāno labhanto sappāyāni bhojanāni no alabhanto, labhanto sappāyāni bhesajjāni no alabhanto, labhanto patirūpaṃ upaṭṭhākaṃ [T 0.0164] no alabhanto, vuṭṭhāti tamhā ābādhā, imaṃ gilānaṃ paṭicca bhagavatā gilānabhattaṃ anuññātaṃ gilānabhesajjaṃ anuññātaṃ gilānupaṭṭhāko anuññāto. Imañca pana gilānaṃ paṭicca aññepi gilānā upaṭṭhātabbā.

It is with reference to this sick person—the one who recovers from that illness upon obtaining suitable food but not upon failing to obtain it, upon obtaining suitable medicine but not upon failing to obtain it, and upon obtaining a suitable attendant but not upon failing to obtain one—that the Blessed One has allowed food for the sick, medicine for the sick, and an attendant for the sick. And it is on account of this sick person that other sick persons should also be attended to.

Evamevaṃ evameva (sī.) tayo gilānūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ. Katame tayo? Idhekacco puggalo labhanto vā tathāgataṃ dassanāya alabhanto vā tathāgataṃ dassanāya, labhanto vā [V 0.0135] tathāgatappaveditaṃ dhammavinayaṃ savaṇāya alabhanto vā tathāgatappaveditaṃ dhammavinayaṃ savaṇāya, neva okkamati niyāmaṃ kusalesu dhammesu sammattaṃ. (1)

In the same way, there are three persons comparable to a sick person found existing in the world. What three? Here, a certain person, whether or not he gets to see the Tathāgata, whether or not he gets to hear the Dhamma and Discipline proclaimed by the Tathāgata, does not enter upon the fixed course of rightness in wholesome qualities.

Idha panekacco puggalo labhanto vā tathāgataṃ dassanāya alabhanto vā tathāgataṃ dassanāya, labhanto vā tathāgatappaveditaṃ dhammavinayaṃ savaṇāya alabhanto vā tathāgatappaveditaṃ dhammavinayaṃ savaṇāya, okkamati niyāmaṃ kusalesu dhammesu sammattaṃ. (2)

Here, a certain person, whether or not he gets to see the Tathāgata, whether or not he gets to hear the Dhamma and Discipline proclaimed by the Tathāgata, enters upon the fixed course of rightness in wholesome qualities.

Idha panekacco puggalo labhanto tathāgataṃ dassanāya no alabhanto, labhanto tathāgatappaveditaṃ dhammavinayaṃ savaṇāya no alabhanto, okkamati niyāmaṃ kusalesu dhammesu sammattaṃ. (3)

Here, a certain person enters upon the fixed course of rightness in wholesome qualities upon getting to see the Tathāgata, but not upon failing to see him; and upon getting to hear the Dhamma and Discipline proclaimed by the Tathāgata, but not upon failing to hear it.

Tatra yvāyaṃ puggalo labhanto tathāgataṃ dassanāya no alabhanto, labhanto tathāgatappaveditaṃ dhammavinayaṃ savaṇāya no alabhanto[M 0.0132], okkamati niyāmaṃ kusalesu dhammesu sammattaṃ, imaṃ puggalaṃ paṭicca bhagavatā dhammadesanā anuññātā, imañca puggalaṃ paṭicca aññesampi [P 0.0029] dhammo desetabbo. Ime tayo gilānūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ.

It is with reference to the person who enters the fixed course of rightness in wholesome qualities upon getting to see the Tathāgata, but not upon failing to see him, and upon getting to hear the Dhamma and Discipline proclaimed by the Tathāgata, but not upon failing to hear it, that the Blessed One has permitted the teaching of the Dhamma. And with reference to this person, the Dhamma should be taught to others also. These three persons comparable to the sick exist and are found in the world.

95. Katamo [T 0.0165] ca puggalo kāyasakkhī? Idhekacco puggalo aṭṭha vimokkhe kāyena phusitvā viharati, paññāya cassa disvā ekacce āsavā parikkhīṇā honti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “kāyasakkhī”.

95. And what person is a body-witness? Here, a certain person dwells having touched the eight liberations with the body, and having seen with wisdom, some of his taints are destroyed. This person is called a 'body-witness.'

96. Katamo ca puggalo diṭṭhippatto? Idhekacco puggalo “idaṃ dukkha”nti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhasamudayo”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhanirodho”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, tathāgatappaveditā cassa dhammā paññāya vodiṭṭhā honti vocaritā, paññāya cassa disvā ekacce āsavā parikkhīṇā honti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “diṭṭhippatto”.

96. And what person is one attained-to-view? Here, a certain person understands as it really is: ‘This is suffering’; ‘This is the origin of suffering’; ‘This is the cessation of suffering’; ‘This is the path leading to the cessation of suffering.’ The teachings proclaimed by the Tathāgata have been well seen and well practiced by him with wisdom, and having seen with wisdom, some of his taints are destroyed. This person is called one ‘attained-to-view.’

97. Katamo ca puggalo saddhāvimutto? Idhekacco puggalo “idaṃ dukkha”nti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti…pe… tathāgatappaveditā cassa dhammā paññāya vodiṭṭhā honti vocaritā, paññāya cassa [V 0.0136] disvā ekacce āsavā parikkhīṇā honti, no ca kho yathādiṭṭhippattassa – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “saddhāvimutto”.

97. And what person is one liberated by faith? Here, a certain person understands as it really is: ‘This is suffering’… and so on… The teachings proclaimed by the Tathāgata have been well seen and well practiced by him with wisdom, and having seen with wisdom, some of his taints are destroyed, but not indeed as for one attained-to-view. This person is called one ‘liberated by faith.’

98. Katamo ca puggalo gūthabhāṇī? Idhekacco puggalo musāvādī hoti sabhaggato vā parisaggato vā ñātimajjhagato vā pūgamajjhagato vā rājakulamajjhagato vā abhinīto sakkhipuṭṭho – “ehambho ehi bho (syā. ka.) ma. ni. 1.440; a. ni. 3.28, purisa, yaṃ jānāsi taṃ vadehī”ti, so ajānaṃ vā āha – “jānāmī”ti, jānaṃ vā āha – “na jānāmī”ti, apassaṃ vā āha – “passāmī”ti, passaṃ vā āha – “na passāmī”ti. Iti attahetu vā parahetu vā āmisakiñcikkhahetu vā [T 0.0166] sampajānamusā bhāsitā hoti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “gūthabhāṇī”.

98. And what person is a filth-speaker? Here, a certain person is a liar. When brought to a council, an assembly, a gathering of relatives, a guild, or the royal court, and questioned as a witness: “Come, good man, speak what you know,” not knowing, he says, “I know”; or knowing, he says, “I do not know”; not seeing, he says, “I see”; or seeing, he says, “I do not see.” Thus, for his own sake, for another’s sake, or for the sake of some trifling material gain, he is one who speaks falsehood knowingly. This person is called a ‘filth-speaker.’”

99. Katamo [M 0.0133] ca puggalo pupphabhāṇī? Idhekacco puggalo musāvādaṃ pahāya musāvādā paṭivirato hoti sabhaggato vā parisaggato vā ñātimajjhagato vā pūgamajjhagato vā rājakulamajjhagato vā abhinīto sakkhipuṭṭho – “ehambho, purisa, yaṃ jānāsi taṃ vadehī”ti, so ajānaṃ vā āha – “na jānāmī”ti, jānaṃ vā āha – “jānāmī”ti, apassaṃ vā āha – “na passāmī”ti, passaṃ vā āha – “passāmī”ti. Iti attahetu vā parahetu vā āmisakiñcikkhahetu vā na sampajānamusā bhāsitā hoti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “pupphabhāṇī”.

99. And what person is a flower-speaker? Here, a certain person, having abandoned false speech, abstains from false speech. When brought to a council, an assembly, a gathering of relatives, a guild, or the royal court, and questioned as a witness: “Come, good man, speak what you know,” not knowing, he says, “I do not know”; or knowing, he says, “I know”; not seeing, he says, “I do not see”; or seeing, he says, “I see.” Thus, for his own sake, for another’s sake, or for the sake of some trifling material gain, he is not one who speaks falsehood knowingly. This person is called a ‘flower-speaker.’”

100. Katamo ca puggalo madhubhāṇī? Idhekacco puggalo yā sā vācā nelā kaṇṇasukhā pemaniyā [P 0.0030] hadayaṅgamā porī bahujanakantā bahujanamanāpā, tathārūpiṃ vācaṃ bhāsitā hoti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “madhubhāṇī”.

100. And what person is a honey-speaker? Here, a certain person utters speech that is faultless, pleasing to the ear, affectionate, goes to the heart, urbane, liked by many people, and agreeable to many people. This person is called a ‘honey-speaker.’”

101. Katamo ca puggalo arukūpamacitto? Idhekacco puggalo kodhano hoti upāyāsabahulo, appampi vutto samāno abhisajjati kuppati byāpajjati patitthīyati patiṭṭhīyati (syā. ka.) a. ni. 3.25, kopañca dosañca appaccayañca pātukaroti. Seyyathāpi nāma duṭṭhāruko kaṭṭhena vā kaṭhalāya kathalāya (ka.), kathalena (aṭṭhakathā) a. ni. 3.25 vā ghaṭṭito bhiyyoso mattāya āsavaṃ deti assavanoti (sī.), evamevaṃ idhekacco puggalo kodhano hoti upāyāsabahulo, appampi vutto samāno abhisajjati kuppati byāpajjati patitthīyati, kopañca dosañca appaccayañca pātukaroti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “arukūpamacitto”.

101. And what person has a mind like a sore? Here, a certain person is irritable and prone to vexation. Even when spoken to just a little, he gets offended, becomes angry, is hostile, and becomes stubborn; he makes manifest his anger, ill will, and displeasure. Just as a festering sore, when struck by a stick or a potsherd, gives forth discharge all the more, so too, here a certain person is irritable and prone to vexation. Even when spoken to just a little, he gets offended, becomes angry, is hostile, and becomes stubborn; he makes manifest his anger, ill will, and displeasure. This person is called one with a ‘mind like a sore.’”

102. Katamo [V 0.0137] ca puggalo vijjūpamacitto? Idhekacco puggalo “idaṃ dukkha”nti yathābhūtaṃ [T 0.0167] pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhasamudayo”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhanirodho”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti. Seyyathāpi nāma cakkhumā puriso rattandhakāratimisāya vijjantarikāya rūpāni passeyya, evamevaṃ idhekacco puggalo “idaṃ dukkha”nti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhasamudayo”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhanirodho”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti[M 0.0134], “ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “vijjūpamacitto”.

102. And what person has a mind like lightning? Here, a certain person understands as it really is: ‘This is suffering,’ ‘This is the origin of suffering,’ ‘This is the cessation of suffering,’ ‘This is the path leading to the cessation of suffering.’ Just as a man with sight might see forms in the pitch-black darkness of night by a flash of lightning, so too a certain person understands as it really is: ‘This is suffering,’ ‘This is the origin of suffering,’ ‘This is the cessation of suffering,’ ‘This is the path leading to the cessation of suffering.’ This person is called one with a ‘mind like lightning.’”

103. Katamo ca puggalo vajirūpamacitto? Idhekacco puggalo āsavānaṃ khayā anāsavaṃ cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ diṭṭheva dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharati. Seyyathāpi nāma vajirassa natthi kiñci abhejjaṃ maṇi vā pāsāṇo vā, evamevaṃ idhekacco puggalo āsavānaṃ khayā anāsavaṃ cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ diṭṭheva dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharati – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “vajirūpamacitto”.

103. And what person has a mind like a diamond? Here, a certain person, through the destruction of the taints, in this very life, having realized for himself with higher knowledge, attains and abides in the taintless liberation of mind, liberation by wisdom. Just as for a diamond there is nothing that cannot be broken, be it a gem or a stone, so too, here a certain person, through the destruction of the taints, in this very life, having realized for himself with higher knowledge, attains and abides in the taintless liberation of mind, liberation by wisdom. This person is called one with a ‘mind like a diamond.’”

104. Katamo ca puggalo andho? Idhekaccassa puggalassa tathārūpaṃ cakkhu na hoti yathārūpena cakkhunā anadhigataṃ vā bhogaṃ adhigaccheyya, adhigataṃ vā bhogaṃ phātiṃ kareyya; tathārūpampissa cakkhu na hoti yathārūpena cakkhunā kusalākusale dhamme jāneyya, sāvajjānavajje dhamme jāneyya, hīnappaṇīte dhamme jāneyya, kaṇhasukkasappaṭibhāge dhamme jāneyya – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “andho”.

104. And what person is blind? Here, a certain person does not have that kind of eye by which one might acquire unacquired wealth or make acquired wealth increase; and he does not have that kind of eye by which one might know wholesome and unwholesome dhammas, blameworthy and blameless dhammas, inferior and superior dhammas, and dark and bright dhammas with their counterparts. This person is called ‘blind.’”

105. Katamo ca puggalo ekacakkhu? Idhekaccassa puggalassa tathārūpaṃ cakkhu hoti[T 0.0168], yathārūpena cakkhunā anadhigataṃ vā bhogaṃ adhigaccheyya, adhigataṃ [P 0.0031] vā bhogaṃ phātiṃ kareyya; tathārūpampissa cakkhu na hoti yathārūpena cakkhunā kusalākusale dhamme jāneyya, sāvajjānavajje dhamme jāneyya, hīnappaṇīte dhamme jāneyya, kaṇhasukkasappaṭibhāge dhamme jāneyya – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “ekacakkhu”.

105. And what person has one eye? Here, a certain person has that kind of eye by which he might acquire unacquired wealth or cause acquired wealth to increase; but he does not have that kind of eye by which he might know wholesome and unwholesome dhammas, blameworthy and blameless dhammas, inferior and superior dhammas, and dark and bright dhammas with their counterparts. This person is called ‘one-eyed’.

106. Katamo ca puggalo dvicakkhu? Idhekaccassa puggalassa tathārūpaṃ cakkhu hoti yathārūpena cakkhunā anadhigataṃ vā bhogaṃ adhigaccheyya, adhigataṃ vā bhogaṃ phātiṃ kareyya; tathārūpampissa [V 0.0138] cakkhu hoti yathārūpena cakkhunā kusalākusale dhamme jāneyya, sāvajjānavajje dhamme jāneyya, hīnappaṇīte dhamme jāneyya, kaṇhasukkasappaṭibhāge dhamme jāneyya – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “dvicakkhu”.

106. And what person has two eyes? Here, a certain person has that kind of eye by which he might acquire unacquired wealth or cause acquired wealth to increase; and he also has that kind of eye by which he might know wholesome and unwholesome dhammas, blameworthy and blameless dhammas, inferior and superior dhammas, and dark and bright dhammas with their counterparts. This person is called ‘two-eyed’.

107. Katamo [M 0.0135] ca puggalo avakujjapañño? Idhekacco puggalo ārāmaṃ gantā gato (sī.), gantvā (syā.) hoti abhikkhaṇaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ santike dhammassavaṇāya dhammassavanāya (syā.). Tassa bhikkhū dhammaṃ desenti ādikalyāṇaṃ majjhekalyāṇaṃ pariyosānakalyāṇaṃ sātthaṃ sabyañjanaṃ kevalaparipuṇṇaṃ parisuddhaṃ brahmacariyaṃ pakāsenti. So tasmiṃ āsane nisinno tassā kathāya neva ādiṃ manasi karoti, na majjhaṃ manasi karoti, na pariyosānaṃ manasi karoti. Vuṭṭhitopi tamhā āsanā tassā kathāya neva ādiṃ manasi karoti, na majjhaṃ manasi karoti, na pariyosānaṃ manasi karoti. Seyyathāpi nāma kumbho nikkujjo nikujjo (syā.) a. ni. 3.30 tatra udakaṃ āsittaṃ vivaṭṭati, no saṇṭhāti; evamevaṃ idhekacco puggalo ārāmaṃ gantā hoti abhikkhaṇaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ santike dhammassavaṇāya[T 0.0169]. Tassa bhikkhū dhammaṃ desenti ādikalyāṇaṃ majjhekalyāṇaṃ pariyosānakalyāṇaṃ sātthaṃ sabyañjanaṃ kevalaparipuṇṇaṃ parisuddhaṃ brahmacariyaṃ pakāsenti. So tasmiṃ āsane nisinno tassā kathāya neva ādiṃ manasi karoti, na majjhaṃ manasi karoti, na pariyosānaṃ manasi karoti. Vuṭṭhitopi tamhā āsanā tassā kathāya neva ādiṃ manasi karoti, na majjhaṃ manasi karoti, na pariyosānaṃ manasi karoti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “avakujjapañño”.

107. And what person has overturned wisdom? Here, a certain person frequently goes to the monastery to listen to the Dhamma in the presence of the bhikkhus. The bhikkhus teach him the Dhamma that is good in the beginning, good in the middle, and good in the end, with its meaning and phrasing; they proclaim the holy life, perfectly complete and pure. While sitting in that seat, he does not attend to the beginning, the middle, or the end of that talk. Even after rising from that seat, he does not attend to the beginning, the middle, or the end of that talk. Just as water poured into an overturned pot flows away and does not remain, so too, here a certain person frequently goes to the monastery to listen to the Dhamma in the presence of the bhikkhus. The bhikkhus teach him the Dhamma that is good in the beginning, good in the middle, and good in the end, with its meaning and phrasing; they proclaim the holy life, perfectly complete and pure. While sitting in that seat, he does not attend to the beginning, the middle, or the end of that talk. Even after rising from that seat, he does not attend to the beginning, the middle, or the end of that talk. This person is called one with ‘overturned wisdom’.

108. Katamo ca puggalo ucchaṅgapañño? Idhekacco puggalo ārāmaṃ gantā hoti abhikkhaṇaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ santike dhammassavaṇāya. Tassa bhikkhū dhammaṃ desenti ādikalyāṇaṃ majjhekalyāṇaṃ pariyosānakalyāṇaṃ sātthaṃ sabyañjanaṃ kevalaparipuṇṇaṃ parisuddhaṃ brahmacariyaṃ pakāsenti. So tasmiṃ āsane nisinno tassā kathāya ādimpi manasi karoti, majjhampi manasi karoti, pariyosānampi manasi karoti. Vuṭṭhito ca kho tamhā āsanā tassā kathāya neva ādiṃ manasi karoti, na majjhaṃ manasi karoti, na pariyosānaṃ manasi karoti. Seyyathāpi nāma purisassa ucchaṅge nānākhajjakāni ākiṇṇāni – tilā [P 0.0032] taṇḍulā tilataṇḍulā (ka.) a. ni. 3.30 modakā badarā. So tamhā āsanā vuṭṭhahanto satisammosā pakireyya. Evamevaṃ idhekacco puggalo ārāmaṃ gantā hoti abhikkhaṇaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ santike dhammassavaṇāya. Tassa bhikkhū dhammaṃ desenti ādikalyāṇaṃ majjhekalyāṇaṃ pariyosānakalyāṇaṃ [M 0.0136] sātthaṃ sabyañjanaṃ kevalaparipuṇṇaṃ [V 0.0139] parisuddhaṃ brahmacariyaṃ pakāsenti. So tasmiṃ āsane nisinno tassā kathāya ādimpi [T 0.0170] manasi karoti, majjhampi manasi karoti, pariyosānampi manasi karoti. Vuṭṭhito ca kho tamhā āsanā tassā kathāya neva ādimpi manasi karoti, na majjhampi manasi karoti, na pariyosānampi manasi karoti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “ucchaṅgapañño”.

108. And what is the person with lap-wisdom? Here, a certain person frequently goes to the monastery to listen to the Dhamma in the presence of the bhikkhus. The bhikkhus teach him the Dhamma that is good in the beginning, good in the middle, and good in the end, with its meaning and phrasing; they proclaim the holy life, perfectly complete and pure. While sitting in that seat, he attends to the beginning, the middle, and the end of that talk. But having risen from that seat, he does not attend to the beginning, the middle, or the end of that talk. Just as a person’s lap might be filled with various snacks—sesame seeds, rice grains, sweets, and jujubes—and when rising from that seat, due to a lapse of mindfulness, he might scatter them; so too, here a certain person frequently goes to the monastery to listen to the Dhamma in the presence of the bhikkhus. The bhikkhus teach him the Dhamma that is good in the beginning, good in the middle, and good in the end, with its meaning and phrasing; they proclaim the holy life, perfectly complete and pure. While sitting in that seat, he attends to the beginning, the middle, and the end of that talk. But having risen from that seat, he does not attend to the beginning, the middle, or the end of that talk. This person is called one with lap-wisdom.

109. Katamo ca puggalo puthupañño? Idhekacco puggalo ārāmaṃ gantā hoti abhikkhaṇaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ santike dhammassavaṇāya. Tassa bhikkhū dhammaṃ desenti ādikalyāṇaṃ majjhekalyāṇaṃ pariyosānakalyāṇaṃ sātthaṃ sabyañjanaṃ kevalaparipuṇṇaṃ parisuddhaṃ brahmacariyaṃ pakāsenti. So tasmiṃ āsane nisinno tassā kathāya ādimpi manasi karoti, majjhampi manasi karoti, pariyosānampi manasi karoti. Vuṭṭhitopi tamhā āsanā tassā kathāya ādimpi manasi karoti, majjhampi manasi karoti, pariyosānampi manasi karoti. Seyyathāpi nāma kumbho ukkujjo tatra udakaṃ āsittaṃ saṇṭhāti, no vivaṭṭati; evamevaṃ idhekacco puggalo ārāmaṃ gantā honti abhikkhaṇaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ santike dhammassavaṇāya. Tassa bhikkhū dhammaṃ desenti ādikalyāṇaṃ majjhekalyāṇaṃ pariyosānakalyāṇaṃ sātthaṃ sabyañjanaṃ kevalaparipuṇṇaṃ parisuddhaṃ brahmacariyaṃ pakāsenti. So tasmiṃ āsane nisinno tassā kathāya ādimpi manasi karoti, majjhampi manasi karoti, pariyosānampi manasi karoti. Vuṭṭhitopi tamhā āsanā tassā kathāya ādimpi manasi karoti, majjhampi manasi karoti, pariyosānampi manasi karoti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “puthupañño”.

109. And what kind of person is one with broad wisdom? Here, a certain person is one who frequently goes to a monastery to listen to the Dhamma in the presence of the bhikkhus. The bhikkhus teach him the Dhamma that is good in the beginning, good in the middle, and good in the end, with its meaning and phrasing; they make clear the holy life, which is utterly complete and pure. While sitting in that seat, he attends to the beginning of that talk, he attends to the middle, and he attends to the end. Even after having risen from that seat, he attends to the beginning of that talk, he attends to the middle, and he attends to the end. Just as a water pot placed upright, when water is poured into it, retains it and does not let it run off; in the same way, here a certain person is one who frequently goes to a monastery to listen to the Dhamma in the presence of the bhikkhus. The bhikkhus teach him the Dhamma that is good in the beginning, good in the middle, and good in the end, with its meaning and phrasing; they make clear the holy life, which is utterly complete and pure. While sitting in that seat, he attends to the beginning of that talk, he attends to the middle, and he attends to the end. Even after having risen from that seat, he attends to the beginning of that talk, he attends to the middle, and he attends to the end. This is called a person with broad wisdom.

110. Katamo ca puggalo kāmesu ca bhavesu ca avītarāgo? Sotāpannasakadāgāmino [T 0.0171] – ime vuccanti puggalā “kāmesu ca bhavesu ca avītarāgā”.

110. And what kind of person is not free from passion for sensual pleasures and for states of existence? The stream-enterer and the once-returner. These persons are called 'those not free from passion for sensual pleasures and for states of existence'.

111. Katamo ca puggalo kāmesu vītarāgo, bhavesu avītarāgo? Anāgāmī – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “kāmesu vītarāgo, bhavesu avītarāgo”.

111. And what kind of person is free from passion for sensual pleasures but not free from passion for states of existence? The non-returner. This person is called 'one who is free from passion for sensual pleasures but not free from passion for states of existence'.

112. Katamo ca puggalo kāmesu ca bhavesu ca vītarāgo? Arahā – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “kāmesu ca bhavesu ca vītarāgo”.

112. And what kind of person is free from passion for sensual pleasures and for states of existence? The Arahant. This person is called 'one who is free from passion for sensual pleasures and for states of existence'.

113. Katamo [M 0.0137][V 0.0140] ca puggalo pāsāṇalekhūpamo? Idhekacco puggalo abhiṇhaṃ kujjhati. So ca khvassa kodho ciraṃ dīgharattaṃ anuseti. Seyyathāpi nāma pāsāṇe lekhā na khippaṃ lujjati vātena vā udakena vā, ciraṭṭhitikā hoti; evamevaṃ idhekacco puggalo abhiṇhaṃ kujjhati. So ca khvassa kodho ciraṃ dīgharattaṃ anuseti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “pāsāṇalekhūpamo”.

113. And what kind of person is comparable to a line on a rock? Here, a certain person is frequently angry. And that anger of his lies dormant for a long, long time. Just as a line on a rock is not quickly effaced by wind or by water but is long-lasting; in the same way, here a certain person is frequently angry, and that anger of his lies dormant for a long, long time. This is called a person comparable to a line on a rock.

114. Katamo ca puggalo pathavilekhūpamo? Idhekacco puggalo abhiṇhaṃ kujjhati. So ca khvassa kodho na ciraṃ dīgharattaṃ anuseti. Seyyathāpi nāma pathaviyā paṭhaviyā (sī. syā.) lekhā khippaṃ lujjati vātena vā udakena vā, na ciraṭṭhitikā hoti; evamevaṃ idhekacco puggalo abhiṇhaṃ kujjhati. So ca khvassa kodho na ciraṃ dīgharattaṃ anuseti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “pathavilekhūpamo”.

114. And what kind of person is comparable to a line on the earth? Here, a certain person is frequently angry. But that anger of his does not lie dormant for a long, long time. Just as a line on the earth is quickly effaced by wind or by water and is not long-lasting; in the same way, here a certain person is frequently angry, but that anger of his does not lie dormant for a long, long time. This is called a person comparable to a line on the earth.

115. Katamo ca puggalo udakalekhūpamo? Idhekacco puggalo āgāḷhenapi vuccamāno pharusenapi vuccamāno amanāpenapi vuccamāno saṃsandatimeva … ceva (syā.) a. ni. 3.133 sandhiyatimeva … ceva (syā.) a. ni. 3.133 sammodatimeva … ceva (syā.) a. ni. 3.133. Seyyathāpi nāma udake [P 0.0033] lekhā khippaṃ lujjati, na ciraṭṭhitikā hoti; evamevaṃ idhekacco puggalo āgāḷhenapi vuccamāno [T 0.0172] pharusenapi vuccamāno amanāpenapi vuccamāno saṃsandatimeva sandhiyatimeva sammodatimeva – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “udakalekhūpamo”.

115. And what kind of person is comparable to a line on water? Here, a certain person, even when spoken to strongly, even when spoken to harshly, even when spoken to disagreeably, just reconciles, just unites, just gets along. Just as a line on water is quickly effaced and is not long-lasting; in the same way, here a certain person, even when spoken to strongly, even when spoken to harshly, even when spoken to disagreeably, just reconciles, just unites, just gets along. This is called a person comparable to a line on water.

116. Tattha katame tayo potthakūpamā puggalā? Tayo potthakā – navopi potthako dubbaṇṇo ceva hoti dukkhasamphasso ca appaggho ca, majjhimopi potthako dubbaṇṇo ceva hoti dukkhasamphasso ca appaggho ca, jiṇṇopi potthako dubbaṇṇo ceva hoti dukkhasamphasso ca appaggho ca. Jiṇṇampi potthakaṃ ukkhaliparimajjanaṃ vā karonti saṅkārakūṭe vā naṃ chaḍḍenti. Evamevaṃ tayome potthakūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā bhikkhūsu. Katame tayo? Navo cepi bhikkhu hoti dussīlo pāpadhammo, idamassa dubbaṇṇatāya. Seyyathāpi so potthako dubbaṇṇo, tathūpamo [M 0.0138] ayaṃ puggalo. Ye kho panassa sevanti bhajanti payirupāsanti diṭṭhānugatiṃ āpajjanti, tesaṃ taṃ hoti dīgharattaṃ ahitāya dukkhāya. Idamassa dukkhasamphassatāya. Seyyathāpi so potthako dukkhasamphasso, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo. Yesaṃ kho pana so paṭiggaṇhāti cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhāraṃ[V 0.0141], tesaṃ taṃ na mahapphalaṃ hoti na mahānisaṃsaṃ. Idamassa appagghatāya. Seyyathāpi so potthako appaggho, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

116. In this context, what are the three persons comparable to a hempen cloth? There are three kinds of hempen cloths: a new hempen cloth is of poor color, rough to the touch, and of little value; a used hempen cloth is of poor color, rough to the touch, and of little value; and a worn-out hempen cloth is of poor color, rough to the touch, and of little value. A worn-out hempen cloth is used for scouring a pot or is thrown on a rubbish heap. In the same way, these three persons comparable to a hempen cloth exist and are found among the bhikkhus. What are the three? If a bhikkhu is new, he may be immoral and of evil character; this is his poor color. Just as that hempen cloth is of poor color, this person is comparable to that. Whoever associates with, attends on, and resorts to him, and falls into following his views, for them that leads to their harm and suffering for a long time. This is his roughness to the touch. Just as that hempen cloth is rough to the touch, this person is comparable to that. For those from whom he accepts the requisites—robes, almsfood, lodging, and medicine and provisions for the sick—that gift of theirs is not of great fruit, not of great benefit. This is his little value. Just as that hempen cloth is of little value, this person is comparable to that.

Majjhimo cepi bhikkhu hoti…pe… thero cepi bhikkhu hoti dussīlo pāpadhammo, idamassa dubbaṇṇatāya. Seyyathāpi so potthako dubbaṇṇo, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo. Ye [T 0.0173] kho panassa sevanti bhajanti payirupāsanti diṭṭhānugatiṃ āpajjanti, tesaṃ taṃ hoti dīgharattaṃ ahitāya dukkhāya. Idamassa dukkhasamphassatāya. Seyyathāpi so potthako dukkhasamphasso, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo. Yesaṃ kho pana so paṭiggaṇhāti cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhāraṃ, tesaṃ taṃ na mahapphalaṃ hoti na mahānisaṃsaṃ. Idamassa appagghatāya. Seyyathāpi so potthako appaggho, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

If a bhikkhu is of middle standing... and so on... and if an elder bhikkhu is immoral and of evil character, this is his poor color. Just as that hempen cloth is of poor color, this person is comparable to that. Whoever associates with, attends on, and resorts to him, and falls into following his views, for them that leads to their harm and suffering for a long time. This is his roughness to the touch. Just as that hempen cloth is rough to the touch, this person is comparable to that. For those from whom he accepts the requisites—robes, almsfood, lodging, and medicine and provisions for the sick—that gift of theirs is not of great fruit, not of great benefit. This is his little value. Just as that hempen cloth is of little value, this person is comparable to that.

Evarūpo ce thero bhikkhu saṅghamajjhe bhaṇati. Tamenaṃ bhikkhū evamāhaṃsu – “kiṃ nu kho tuyhaṃ bālassa abyattassa bhaṇitena, tvampi nāma bhaṇitabbaṃ maññasī”ti! So kupito anattamano tathārūpiṃ vācaṃ nicchāreti yathārūpāya vācāya saṅgho taṃ ukkhipati, saṅkārakūṭeva naṃ potthakaṃ. Ime [P 0.0034] tayo potthakūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā bhikkhūsu.

If such an elder bhikkhu speaks in the midst of the Sangha, the bhikkhus say this to him: ‘What is the use of your speaking, you fool, you incompetent one? Do you indeed think you should speak?’ Angered and displeased, he utters such speech by which the Sangha suspends him, just as one would toss that hempen cloth onto a rubbish heap. These three persons comparable to a hempen cloth exist and are found among the bhikkhus.

117. Tattha katame tayo kāsikavatthūpamā puggalā? Tīṇi kāsikavatthāni – navampi kāsikavatthaṃ vaṇṇavantañceva hoti sukhasamphassañca mahagghañca, majjhimampi kāsikavatthaṃ vaṇṇavantañceva hoti sukhasamphassañca mahagghañca, jiṇṇampi kāsikavatthaṃ vaṇṇavantañceva hoti sukhasamphassañca mahagghañca. Jiṇṇampi kāsikavatthaṃ ratanapaliveṭhanaṃ vā karonti gandhakaraṇḍake vā naṃ nikkhipanti.

117. In this context, what are the three persons comparable to Benares cloth? There are three kinds of Benares cloth. A new Benares cloth is beautiful, pleasant to the touch, and of great value. A used Benares cloth is beautiful, pleasant to the touch, and of great value. An old Benares cloth is also beautiful, pleasant to the touch, and of great value. An old Benares cloth they use to wrap a jewel, or they place it in a perfume casket.

Evamevaṃ [M 0.0139] tayome kāsikavatthūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā bhikkhūsu. Katame tayo? Navo cepi bhikkhu hoti sīlavā kalyāṇadhammo[T 0.0174], idamassa suvaṇṇatāya. Seyyathāpi taṃ kāsikavatthaṃ vaṇṇavantaṃ, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo. Ye kho panassa sevanti bhajanti payirupāsanti diṭṭhānugatiṃ āpajjanti, tesaṃ taṃ hoti dīgharattaṃ hitāya sukhāya. Idamassa sukhasamphassatāya. Seyyathāpi taṃ kāsikavatthaṃ sukhasamphassaṃ, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo. Yesaṃ kho pana so yesaṃ kho pana (sabbattha) a. ni. 3.100 paṭiggaṇhāti patigaṇhāti (sī.) rūpasiddhiṭīkāya pana sameti cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhāraṃ, tesaṃ taṃ mahapphalaṃ [V 0.0142] hoti mahānisaṃsaṃ. Idamassa mahagghatāya. Seyyathāpi taṃ kāsikavatthaṃ mahagghaṃ, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

In the same way, these three persons comparable to Benares cloth exist and are found among the bhikkhus. What are the three? If a newly ordained bhikkhu is virtuous and of good conduct, this is his beauty. Just as that Benares cloth is beautiful, this person is comparable to that. Whoever associates with, attends on, and resorts to him, and falls into following his views, for them that leads to their welfare and happiness for a long time. This is his pleasant touch. Just as that Benares cloth is pleasant to the touch, this person is comparable to that. For those from whom he accepts the requisites—robes, almsfood, lodging, and medicine and provisions for the sick—that gift of theirs is of great fruit and great benefit. This is his great value. Just as that Benares cloth is of great value, this person is comparable to that.

Majjhimo cepi bhikkhu…pe… thero cepi bhikkhu hoti sīlavā kalyāṇadhammo, idamassa suvaṇṇatāya. Seyyathāpi taṃ kāsikavatthaṃ vaṇṇavantaṃ, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo. Ye kho panassa sevanti bhajanti payirupāsanti diṭṭhānugatiṃ āpajjanti, tesaṃ taṃ hoti dīgharattaṃ hitāya sukhāya. Idamassa sukhasamphassatāya. Seyyathāpi taṃ kāsikavatthaṃ sukhasamphassaṃ, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo. Yesaṃ kho pana so paṭiggaṇhāti cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhāraṃ, tesaṃ taṃ mahapphalaṃ hoti mahānisaṃsaṃ. Idamassa mahagghatāya. Seyyathāpi taṃ kāsikavatthaṃ mahagghaṃ, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

If a bhikkhu is of middle standing... and so on... and if an elder bhikkhu is virtuous and of good conduct, this is his beauty. Just as that Benares cloth is beautiful, this person is comparable to that. Whoever associates with, attends on, and resorts to him, and falls into following his views, for them that leads to their welfare and happiness for a long time. This is his pleasant touch. Just as that Benares cloth is pleasant to the touch, this person is comparable to that. For those from whom he accepts the requisites—robes, almsfood, lodging, and medicine and provisions for the sick—that gift of theirs is of great fruit and great benefit. This is his great value. Just as that Benares cloth is of great value, this person is comparable to that.

Evarūpo ce thero bhikkhu saṅghamajjhe bhaṇati, tamenaṃ bhikkhū evamāhaṃsu – “appasaddā āyasmanto hotha, thero bhikkhu dhammañca vinayañca bhaṇatī”ti. Tassa taṃ vacanaṃ ādheyyaṃ gacchati, gandhakaraṇḍakeva [P 0.0035] naṃ kāsikavatthaṃ. Ime tayo kāsikavatthūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā bhikkhūsu.

If such an elder bhikkhu speaks in the midst of the Sangha, the bhikkhus say: ‘Be quiet, venerable sirs, the elder bhikkhu is speaking on the Dhamma and the Vinaya.’ His speech is held as authoritative, just as that Benares cloth is placed in a perfume casket. These three persons comparable to Benares cloth exist and are found among the bhikkhus.

118. Katamo [T 0.0175] ca puggalo suppameyyo? Idhekacco puggalo uddhato hoti unnaḷo capalo mukharo vikiṇṇavāco muṭṭhassati asampajāno asamāhito vibbhantacitto pākaṭindriyo – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “suppameyyo”.

118. And what kind of person is easily measured? Here, a certain person is agitated, arrogant, fickle, harsh-mouthed, of scattered speech, of lapsed mindfulness, without clear comprehension, unconcentrated, with a distracted mind, and with faculties unrestrained. This person is called ‘easily measured.’

119. Katamo ca puggalo duppameyyo? Idhekacco puggalo anuddhato hoti anunnaḷo acapalo amukharo avikiṇṇavāco upaṭṭhitassati [M 0.0140] sampajāno samāhito ekaggacitto saṃvutindriyo – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “duppameyyo”.

119. And what kind of person is hard to measure? Here, a certain person is unagitated, not arrogant, not fickle, not harsh-mouthed, not of scattered speech, with mindfulness established, clearly comprehending, concentrated, with a unified mind, and with faculties restrained. This person is called ‘hard to measure.’

120. Katamo ca puggalo appameyyo? Idhekacco puggalo āsavānaṃ khayā anāsavaṃ cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ diṭṭheva dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharati – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “appameyyo”.

120. And what kind of person is immeasurable? Here, through the destruction of the taints, a certain person has realized for himself with direct knowledge, in this very life, the taintless liberation of mind, liberation by wisdom, and having attained it, he dwells therein. This person is called ‘immeasurable.’

121. Katamo [V 0.0143] ca puggalo na sevitabbo na bhajitabbo na payirupāsitabbo? Idhekacco puggalo hīno hoti sīlena samādhinā paññāya. Evarūpo puggalo na sevitabbo na bhajitabbo na payirupāsitabbo, aññatra anuddayā aññatra anukampā.

121. And what kind of person should not be associated with, not frequented, and not served? Here, a certain person is deficient in virtue, concentration, and wisdom. Such a person should not be associated with, not frequented, and not served, except out of pity and compassion.

122. Katamo ca puggalo sevitabbo bhajitabbo payirupāsitabbo? Idhekacco puggalo sadiso hoti sīlena samādhinā paññāya. Evarūpo puggalo sevitabbo bhajitabbo payirupāsitabbo. Taṃ kissa hetu? “Sīlasāmaññagatānaṃ sataṃ sīlakathā ca no bhavissati, sā ca no phāsu bhavissati, sā ca no pavattinī bhavissati; samādhisāmaññagatānaṃ sataṃ samādhikathā ca no bhavissati, sā ca no phāsu bhavissati, sā ca no pavattinī pavattanī (sī.) a. ni. 3.26 bhavissati; paññāsāmaññagatānaṃ [T 0.0176] sataṃ paññākathā ca no bhavissati, sā ca no phāsu bhavissati, sā ca no pavattinī bhavissatī”ti. Tasmā evarūpo puggalo sevitabbo bhajitabbo payirupāsitabbo.

122. And what kind of person should be associated with, frequented, and served? Here, a certain person is similar in virtue, concentration, and wisdom. Such a person should be associated with, frequented, and served. For what reason? ‘When we are with those who are similar in virtue, we will have talk on virtue, and that will be pleasant for us and will be for our growth. When we are with those who are similar in concentration, we will have talk on concentration, and that will be pleasant for us and will be for our growth. When we are with those who are similar in wisdom, we will have talk on wisdom, and that will be pleasant for us and will be for our growth.’ Therefore, such a person should be associated with, frequented, and served.

123. Katamo ca puggalo sakkatvā garuṃ katvā sevitabbo bhajitabbo payirupāsitabbo? Idhekacco puggalo adhiko hoti sīlena samādhinā paññāya. Evarūpo puggalo sakkatvā garuṃ katvā sevitabbo bhajitabbo payirupāsitabbo. Taṃ kissa hetu? “Aparipūraṃ vā sīlakkhandhaṃ paripūressāmi, paripūraṃ vā sīlakkhandhaṃ tattha [P 0.0036] tattha paññāya anuggahessāmi; aparipūraṃ vā samādhikkhandhaṃ paripūressāmi, paripūraṃ vā samādhikkhandhaṃ tattha tattha paññāya anuggahessāmi; aparipūraṃ vā paññākkhandhaṃ paripūressāmi, paripūraṃ vā paññākkhandhaṃ tattha tattha paññāya anuggahessāmī”ti. Tasmā evarūpo puggalo sakkatvā garuṃ katvā sevitabbo bhajitabbo payirupāsitabbo.

123. And what kind of person should be associated with, frequented, and served after having honored and respected him? Here, a certain person is superior in virtue, concentration, and wisdom. Such a person should be associated with, frequented, and served after having honored and respected him. For what reason? ‘I will fulfill the unfulfilled aggregate of virtue, or I will assist with wisdom the fulfilled aggregate of virtue in various respects. I will fulfill the unfulfilled aggregate of concentration, or I will assist with wisdom the fulfilled aggregate of concentration in various respects. I will fulfill the unfulfilled aggregate of wisdom, or I will assist with wisdom the fulfilled aggregate of wisdom in various respects.’ Therefore, such a person should be associated with, frequented, and served after having honored and respected him.

124. Katamo [M 0.0141] ca puggalo jigucchitabbo na sevitabbo na bhajitabbo na payirupāsitabbo? Idhekacco puggalo dussīlo hoti pāpadhammo asuci saṅkassarasamācāro paṭicchannakammanto assamaṇo samaṇapaṭiñño abrahmacārī brahmacāripaṭiñño antopūti avassuto kasambujāto. Evarūpo puggalo jigucchitabbo na sevitabbo na bhajitabbo na payirupāsitabbo. Taṃ kissa hetu? Kiñcāpi evarūpassa puggalassa na diṭṭhānugatiṃ āpajjati, atha kho naṃ pāpako kittisaddo abbhuggacchati [T 0.0177] – “pāpamitto purisapuggalo pāpasahāyo pāpasampavaṅko”ti. Seyyathāpi nāma ahi gūthagato kiñcāpi na ḍaṃsati, atha kho [V 0.0144] naṃ makkheti; evamevaṃ kiñcāpi evarūpassa puggalassa na diṭṭhānugatiṃ āpajjati, atha kho naṃ pāpako kittisaddo abbhuggacchati – “pāpamitto purisapuggalo pāpasahāyo pāpasampavaṅko”ti! Tasmā evarūpo puggalo jigucchitabbo na sevitabbo na bhajitabbo na payirupāsitabbo.

124. And what kind of person should be loathed, not associated with, not frequented, and not served? Here, a certain person is immoral, of evil character, impure, of suspect conduct, of secretive actions, not an ascetic though claiming to be one, not a celibate though claiming to be one, inwardly rotten, oozing, and like rubbish. Such a person should be loathed, not associated with, not frequented, and not served. For what reason? Even if one does not follow his example, a bad report still spreads about him: ‘This person has bad friends, bad companions, bad associates.’ Just as a snake that has gotten into excrement, though it does not bite, still besmears one, so too, even if one does not follow that person’s example, a bad report still spreads about him: ‘This person has bad friends, bad companions, bad associates.’ Therefore, such a person should be loathed, not associated with, not frequented, and not served.

125. Katamo ca puggalo ajjhupekkhitabbo na sevitabbo na bhajitabbo na payirupāsitabbo? Idhekacco puggalo kodhano hoti upāyāsabahulo, appampi vutto samāno abhisajjati kuppati byāpajjati patitthīyati, kopañca dosañca appaccayañca pātukaroti. Seyyathāpi nāma duṭṭhāruko kaṭṭhena vā kaṭhalāya vā ghaṭṭito bhiyyoso mattāya āsavaṃ deti, evamevaṃ idhekacco puggalo kodhano hoti upāyāsabahulo, appampi vutto samāno abhisajjati kuppati byāpajjati patitthīyati, kopañca dosañca appaccayañca pātukaroti. Seyyathāpi nāma tindukālātaṃ kaṭṭhena vā kaṭhalāya vā ghaṭṭitaṃ bhiyyoso mattāya cicciṭāyati ciṭiciṭāyati, evamevaṃ idhekacco puggalo kodhano hoti upāyāsabahulo, appampi vutto samāno abhisajjati kuppati byāpajjati patitthīyati, kopañca dosañca appaccayañca pātukaroti. Seyyathāpi nāma gūthakūpo kaṭṭhena vā kaṭhalāya vā ghaṭṭito bhiyyoso mattāya duggandho hoti, evamevaṃ idhekacco puggalo kodhano hoti upāyāsabahulo, appampi [T 0.0178] vutto samāno abhisajjati kuppati byāpajjati patitthīyati, kopañca dosañca appaccayañca [P 0.0037] pātukaroti; evarūpo puggalo ajjhupekkhitabbo na sevitabbo na bhajitabbo na payirupāsitabbo. Taṃ kissa hetu? “Akkoseyyapi maṃ paribhāseyyapi maṃ anatthampi [M 0.0142] me kareyyā”ti! Tasmā evarūpo puggalo ajjhupekkhitabbo na sevitabbo na bhajitabbo na payirupāsitabbo.

125. And what kind of person should be regarded with equanimity, not associated with, not frequented, and not served? Here, some person is prone to anger and full of vexation. When spoken to even a little, he is offended, becomes hostile, malevolent, and stubborn, and he manifests anger, hatred, and resentment. Just as a festering sore, when struck with a stick or a potsherd, discharges even more matter, so too some person here is prone to anger and full of vexation; when spoken to even a little, he is offended, becomes hostile, malevolent, and stubborn, and he manifests anger, hatred, and resentment. Just as a firebrand of tinduka wood, when struck with a stick or a potsherd, sputters and crackles all the more, so too some person here is prone to anger and full of vexation; when spoken to even a little, he is offended, becomes hostile, malevolent, and stubborn, and he manifests anger, hatred, and resentment. Just as a cesspool, when struck with a stick or a potsherd, becomes even more foul-smelling, so too some person here is prone to anger and full of vexation; when spoken to even a little, he is offended, becomes hostile, malevolent, and stubborn, and he manifests anger, hatred, and resentment. Such a person should be regarded with equanimity, not associated with, not frequented, and not served. For what reason? With the thought: ‘He might abuse me, revile me, and do me harm.’ Therefore, such a person should be regarded with equanimity, not associated with, not frequented, and not served.

126. Katamo ca puggalo sevitabbo bhajitabbo payirupāsitabbo? Idhekacco puggalo sīlavā hoti kalyāṇadhammo – evarūpo puggalo sevitabbo bhajitabbo payirupāsitabbo. Taṃ kissa hetu? Kiñcāpi evarūpassa puggalassa na diṭṭhānugatiṃ āpajjati, atha kho naṃ kalyāṇo kittisaddo abbhuggacchati – “kalyāṇamitto purisapuggalo kalyāṇasahāyo kalyāṇasampavaṅko”ti! Tasmā evarūpo puggalo sevitabbo bhajitabbo payirupāsitabbo.

126. And what kind of person should be associated with, frequented, and served? Here, a certain person is virtuous, of good character—such a person should be associated with, frequented, and served. For what reason? Even if one does not emulate his conduct, a good report about him circulates: ‘This person has good friends, good companions, and good associates.’ Therefore, such a person should be associated with, frequented, and served.

127. Katamo ca puggalo sīlesu paripūrakārī, samādhismiṃ mattaso [V 0.0145] kārī, paññāya mattaso kārī? Sotāpannasakadāgāmino – ime vuccanti puggalā sīlesu paripūrakārino, samādhismiṃ mattaso kārino, paññāya mattaso kārino.

127. And what kind of person is fully accomplished in virtue, but to a moderate degree in concentration and wisdom? The stream-enterer and the once-returner—these are called persons fully accomplished in virtue, but to a moderate degree in concentration and wisdom.

128. Katamo ca puggalo sīlesu ca paripūrakārī, samādhismiñca paripūrakārī, paññāya mattaso kārī? Anāgāmī – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo sīlesu ca paripūrakārī, samādhismiñca paripūrakārī, paññāya mattaso kārī.

128. And what kind of person is fully accomplished in virtue and concentration, but to a moderate degree in wisdom? The non-returner. This person is called one who is fully accomplished in virtue and concentration, but to a moderate degree in wisdom.

129. Katamo ca puggalo sīlesu ca paripūrakārī, samādhismiñca paripūrakārī, paññāya ca paripūrakārī? Arahā – ayaṃ vuccati [T 0.0179] puggalo sīlesu ca paripūrakārī, samādhismiñca paripūrakārī, paññāya ca paripūrakārī.

129. And what kind of person is fully accomplished in virtue, concentration, and wisdom? The Arahant. This person is called one who is fully accomplished in virtue, concentration, and wisdom.

130. Tattha katame tayo satthāro? Idhekacco satthā kāmānaṃ pariññaṃ paññapeti paññāpeti (sī. syā.), na rūpānaṃ pariññaṃ paññapeti, na vedanānaṃ pariññaṃ paññapeti. Idha panekacco satthā kāmānañca pariññaṃ paññapeti, rūpānañca pariññaṃ paññapeti, na vedanānaṃ pariññaṃ paññapeti. Idha panekacco satthā kāmānañca pariññaṃ paññapeti, rūpānañca pariññaṃ paññapeti, vedanānañca pariññaṃ paññapeti.

130. Therein, what are the three teachers? Here, a certain teacher prescribes the full understanding of sensual pleasures, but does not prescribe the full understanding of forms, nor the full understanding of feelings. Here, another teacher prescribes the full understanding of sensual pleasures and also of forms, but does not prescribe the full understanding of feelings. Here, yet another teacher prescribes the full understanding of sensual pleasures, and also of forms, and also of feelings.

Tatra yvāyaṃ satthā kāmānaṃ pariññaṃ paññapeti, na rūpānaṃ pariññaṃ paññapeti, na vedanānaṃ pariññaṃ paññapeti, rūpāvacarasamāpattiyā lābhī satthā tena daṭṭhabbo. Tatra [P 0.0038] yvāyaṃ satthā kāmānañca pariññaṃ paññapeti, rūpānañca [M 0.0143] pariññaṃ paññapeti, na vedanānaṃ pariññaṃ paññapeti, arūpāvacarasamāpattiyā lābhī satthā tena daṭṭhabbo. Tatra yvāyaṃ satthā kāmānañca pariññaṃ paññapeti, rūpānañca pariññaṃ paññapeti, vedanānañca pariññaṃ paññapeti, sammāsambuddho satthā tena daṭṭhabbo. Ime tayo satthāro.

Therein, that teacher who prescribes the full understanding of sensual pleasures, but does not prescribe the full understanding of forms nor of feelings, should thereby be understood as a teacher who is an obtainer of the form-sphere attainment. Therein, that teacher who prescribes the full understanding of sensual pleasures and also of forms, but does not prescribe the full understanding of feelings, should thereby be understood as a teacher who is an obtainer of the formless-sphere attainment. Therein, that teacher who prescribes the full understanding of sensual pleasures, and also of forms, and also of feelings, should thereby be understood as a Perfectly Self-Enlightened One. These are the three teachers.

131. Tattha katame aparepi tayo satthāro? Idhekacco satthā diṭṭhe ceva dhamme attānaṃ saccato thetato paññapeti, abhisamparāyañca attānaṃ saccato thetato paññapeti. Idha panekacco [T 0.0180] satthā diṭṭhe ceva dhamme attānaṃ saccato thetato paññapeti, no ca kho abhisamparāyaṃ attānaṃ saccato [V 0.0146] thetato paññapeti. Idha panekacco satthā diṭṭhe ceva dhamme attānaṃ saccato thetato na paññapeti, abhisamparāyañca attānaṃ saccato thetato na paññapeti.

131. What are the other three teachers? Here, a certain teacher prescribes the self as true and stable in this very life, and also prescribes the self as true and stable in the future life. Here, another teacher prescribes the self as true and stable in this very life, but does not prescribe the self as true and stable in the future life. Here, yet another teacher does not prescribe the self as true and stable in this very life, nor does he prescribe the self as true and stable in the future life.

Tatra yvāyaṃ satthā diṭṭhe ceva dhamme attānaṃ saccato thetato paññapeti, abhisamparāyañca attānaṃ saccato thetato paññapeti, sassatavādo satthā tena daṭṭhabbo. Tatra yvāyaṃ satthā diṭṭhe ceva dhamme attānaṃ saccato thetato paññapeti, no ca kho abhisamparāyaṃ attānaṃ saccato thetato paññapeti, ucchedavādo satthā tena daṭṭhabbo. Tatra yvāyaṃ satthā diṭṭhe ceva dhamme attānaṃ saccato thetato na paññapeti, abhisamparāyañca attānaṃ saccato thetato na paññapeti, sammāsambuddho satthā tena daṭṭhabbo. Ime aparepi tayo satthāro.

Therein, that teacher who prescribes the self as true and stable in this very life, and also prescribes the self as true and stable in the future life, should thereby be understood as a teacher who is an eternalist. Therein, that teacher who prescribes the self as true and stable in this very life, but does not prescribe the self as true and stable in the future life, should thereby be understood as a teacher who is an annihilationist. Therein, that teacher who does not prescribe the self as true and stable in this very life, nor in the future life, should thereby be understood as a Perfectly Self-Enlightened One. These are the other three teachers.

Tikaniddeso.

The Exposition of the Triads.

4. Catukkapuggalapaññatti

4. The Designation of Persons by Fours

132. Katamo ca puggalo asappuriso? Idhekacco puggalo pāṇātipātī hoti, adinnādāyī hoti, kāmesumicchācārī hoti, musāvādī [T 0.0181] hoti, surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhāyī hoti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “asappuriso”.

132. And what kind of person is an unvirtuous person? Here, a certain person takes life, takes what is not given, engages in sexual misconduct, speaks falsehood, and partakes of spirits, liquor, and intoxicants which are the basis for heedlessness. This person is called an 'unvirtuous person'.

133. Katamo [P 0.0039] ca puggalo asappurisena asappurisataro? Idhekacco puggalo attanā ca pāṇātipātī hoti parañca pāṇātipāte samādapeti[M 0.0144], attanā ca adinnādāyī hoti parañca adinnādāne samādapeti, attanā ca kāmesumicchācārī hoti parañca kāmesumicchācāre samādapeti, attanā ca musāvādī hoti parañca musāvāde samādapeti, attanā ca surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhāyī hoti parañca surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhāne samādapeti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “asappurisena asappurisataro”.

133. And what kind of person is more unvirtuous than an unvirtuous person? Here, a certain person, himself, takes life and encourages others to take life; himself, takes what is not given and encourages others to take what is not given; himself, engages in sexual misconduct and encourages others to engage in sexual misconduct; himself, speaks falsehood and encourages others to speak falsehood; himself, partakes of spirits, liquor, and intoxicants which are the basis for heedlessness, and encourages others to do so. This person is called 'more unvirtuous than an unvirtuous person'.

134. Katamo ca puggalo sappuriso? Idhekacco puggalo pāṇātipātā paṭivirato hoti[V 0.0147], adinnādānā paṭivirato hoti, kāmesumicchācārā paṭivirato hoti, musāvādā paṭivirato hoti, surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhānā paṭivirato hoti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “sappuriso”.

134. And what kind of person is a virtuous person? Here, a certain person abstains from taking life, abstains from taking what is not given, abstains from sexual misconduct, abstains from false speech, and abstains from partaking of spirits, liquor, and intoxicants which are the basis for heedlessness. This person is called a 'virtuous person'.

135. Katamo ca puggalo sappurisena sappurisataro? Idhekacco puggalo attanā ca pāṇātipātā paṭivirato hoti parañca pāṇātipātā veramaṇiyā samādapeti, attanā ca adinnādānā paṭivirato hoti parañca adinnādānā veramaṇiyā samādapeti, attanā ca kāmesumicchācārā paṭivirato hoti parañca kāmesumicchācārā veramaṇiyā samādapeti, attanā ca musāvādā paṭivirato hoti parañca musāvādā veramaṇiyā samādapeti, attanā ca surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhānā paṭivirato hoti parañca surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhānā veramaṇiyā samādapeti [T 0.0182] – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “sappurisena sappurisataro”.

135. And what kind of person is more virtuous than a virtuous person? Here, a certain person, he himself abstains from taking life and establishes others in abstinence from taking life; he himself abstains from taking what is not given and establishes others in abstinence from taking what is not given; he himself abstains from sexual misconduct and establishes others in abstinence from sexual misconduct; he himself abstains from false speech and establishes others in abstinence from false speech; he himself abstains from spirits, liquor, and intoxicants which are the basis for heedlessness, and establishes others in abstinence from spirits, liquor, and intoxicants which are the basis for heedlessness. This person is called 'more virtuous than a virtuous person'.

136. Katamo ca puggalo pāpo? Idhekacco puggalo pāṇātipātī hoti, adinnādāyī hoti, kāmesumicchācārī hoti, musāvādī hoti, pisuṇavāco pisuṇāvāco hoti, pharusavāco pharusāvāco (sī.) dī. ni. 3.115 hoti, samphappalāpī hoti, abhijjhālu hoti, byāpannacitto hoti, micchādiṭṭhi micchādiṭṭhī (ka.) hoti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “pāpo”.

136. And what kind of person is an evil person? Here, a certain person takes life, takes what is not given, engages in sexual misconduct, speaks falsehood, speaks divisive speech, speaks harsh speech, engages in idle chatter, is covetous, has a mind of ill will, and holds wrong view. This person is called an 'evil person'.

137. Katamo ca puggalo pāpena pāpataro? Idhekacco puggalo attanā ca pāṇātipātī hoti parañca pāṇātipāte samādapeti, attanā [M 0.0145] ca adinnādāyī hoti parañca adinnādāne samādapeti, attanā ca kāmesumicchācārī hoti parañca kāmesumicchācāre samādapeti, attanā ca musāvādī hoti parañca musāvāde samādapeti, attanā ca pisuṇavāco hoti parañca pisuṇāya vācāya samādapeti, attanā ca pharusavāco [P 0.0040] hoti parañca pharusāya vācāya samādapeti, attanā ca samphappalāpī hoti parañca samphappalāpe samādapeti, attanā ca abhijjhālu hoti parañca abhijjhāya samādapeti, attanā ca byāpannacitto hoti parañca byāpāde samādapeti, attanā ca micchādiṭṭhi hoti parañca micchādiṭṭhiyā samādapeti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “pāpena pāpataro”.

137. And what kind of person is more evil than an evil person? Here, a certain person, he himself takes life and establishes others in taking life; he himself takes what is not given and establishes others in taking what is not given; he himself engages in sexual misconduct and establishes others in sexual misconduct; he himself speaks falsehood and establishes others in false speech; he himself speaks divisive speech and establishes others in divisive speech; he himself speaks harsh speech and establishes others in harsh speech; he himself engages in idle chatter and establishes others in idle chatter; he himself is covetous and establishes others in covetousness; he himself has a mind of ill will and establishes others in ill will; he himself holds wrong view and establishes others in wrong view. This person is called 'more evil than an evil person'.

138. Katamo ca puggalo kalyāṇo? Idhekacco puggalo pāṇātipātā paṭivirato hoti, adinnādānā paṭivirato hoti[V 0.0148], kāmesumicchācārā paṭivirato hoti, musāvādā paṭivirato hoti, pisuṇāya vācāya paṭivirato hoti, pharusāya vācāya paṭivirato hoti, samphappalāpā [T 0.0183] paṭivirato hoti, anabhijjhālu hoti, abyāpannacitto hoti, sammādiṭṭhi sammādiṭṭhī (ka.) hoti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “kalyāṇo”.

138. And what kind of person is a virtuous person? Here, a certain person abstains from taking life, abstains from taking what is not given, abstains from sexual misconduct, abstains from false speech, abstains from divisive speech, abstains from harsh speech, abstains from idle chatter, is uncovetous, has a mind without ill will, and holds right view. This person is called a 'virtuous person'.

139. Katamo ca puggalo kalyāṇena kalyāṇataro? Idhekacco puggalo attanā ca pāṇātipātā paṭivirato hoti parañca pāṇātipātā veramaṇiyā samādapeti, attanā ca adinnādānā paṭivirato hoti parañca adinnādānā veramaṇiyā samādapeti, attanā ca kāmesumicchācārā paṭivirato hoti parañca kāmesumicchācārā veramaṇiyā samādapeti, attanā ca musāvādā paṭivirato hoti parañca musāvādā veramaṇiyā samādapeti, attanā ca pisuṇāya vācāya paṭivirato hoti parañca pisuṇāya vācāya veramaṇiyā samādapeti, attanā ca pharusāya vācāya paṭivirato hoti parañca pharusāya vācāya veramaṇiyā samādapeti, attanā ca samphappalāpā paṭivirato hoti parañca samphappalāpā veramaṇiyā samādapeti, attanā ca anabhijjhālu hoti parañca anabhijjhāya samādapeti, attanā ca abyāpannacitto hoti parañca abyāpāde samādapeti, attanā ca sammādiṭṭhi hoti parañca [M 0.0146] sammādiṭṭhiyā samādapeti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “kalyāṇena kalyāṇataro”.

139. And what kind of person is more virtuous than a virtuous person?
