Here, a certain person, he himself abstains from taking life and establishes others in abstinence from taking life; he himself abstains from taking what is not given and establishes others in abstinence from taking what is not given; he himself abstains from sexual misconduct and establishes others in abstinence from sexual misconduct; he himself abstains from false speech and establishes others in abstinence from false speech; he himself abstains from divisive speech and establishes others in abstinence from divisive speech; he himself abstains from harsh speech and establishes others in abstinence from harsh speech; he himself abstains from idle chatter and establishes others in abstinence from idle chatter; he himself is uncovetous and establishes others in non-covetousness; he himself has a mind without ill will and establishes others in non-ill will; he himself holds right view and establishes others in right view. This person is called 'more virtuous than a virtuous person'.

140. Katamo ca puggalo pāpadhammo? Idhekacco puggalo pāṇātipātī hoti, adinnādāyī hoti…pe… micchādiṭṭhi hoti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “pāpadhammo”.

140. And what kind of person is of evil nature? Here, a certain person takes life, takes what is not given…pe… holds wrong view. This person is called a person 'of evil nature'.

141. Katamo ca puggalo pāpadhammena pāpadhammataro? Idhekacco puggalo attanā ca pāṇātipātī hoti parañca [T 0.0184] pāṇātipāte samādapeti, attanā ca adinnādāyī hoti parañca adinnādāne samādapeti…pe… attanā ca micchādiṭṭhi hoti parañca micchādiṭṭhiyā samādapeti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “pāpadhammena pāpadhammataro”.

141. And what kind of person is of a more evil nature than one of evil nature? Here, a certain person, he himself takes life and establishes others in taking life; he himself takes what is not given and establishes others in taking what is not given…pe… he himself holds wrong view and establishes others in wrong view. This person is called 'one of a more evil nature than one of evil nature'.

142. Katamo ca puggalo kalyāṇadhammo? Idhekacco puggalo pāṇātipātā paṭivirato hoti, adinnādānā paṭivirato hoti…pe… samādiṭṭhi hoti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “kalyāṇadhammo”.

142. And what kind of person is of a virtuous nature? Here, a certain person abstains from taking life, abstains from taking what is not given…pe… holds right view. This person is called 'one of a virtuous nature'.

143. Katamo [V 0.0149] ca puggalo kalyāṇadhammena kalyāṇadhammataro? Idhekacco puggalo attanā ca pāṇātipātā paṭivirato hoti parañca pāṇātipātā veramaṇiyā samādapeti…pe… attanā ca sammādiṭṭhi hoti parañca sammādiṭṭhiyā samādapeti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “kalyāṇadhammena kalyāṇadhammataro”.

143. And what kind of person is of a more virtuous nature than one of a virtuous nature? Here, a certain person, he himself abstains from taking life and establishes others in abstinence from taking life…pe… he himself holds right view and establishes others in right view. This person is called 'one of a more virtuous nature than one of a virtuous nature'.

144. Katamo [P 0.0041] ca puggalo sāvajjo? Idhekacco puggalo sāvajjena kāyakammena samannāgato hoti, sāvajjena vacīkammena samannāgato hoti, sāvajjena manokammena samannāgato hoti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “sāvajjo”.

144. And what kind of person is blameworthy? Here, a certain person is endowed with blameworthy bodily action, blameworthy verbal action, and blameworthy mental action. This person is called 'blameworthy'.

145. Katamo ca puggalo vajjabahulo? Idhekacco puggalo sāvajjena bahulaṃ kāyakammena samannāgato hoti appaṃ anavajjena, sāvajjena bahulaṃ vacīkammena samannāgato hoti appaṃ anavajjena, sāvajjena bahulaṃ manokammena samannāgato hoti appaṃ anavajjena – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “vajjabahulo”.

145. And what kind of person is one abounding in faults? Here, a certain person is endowed with much blameworthy bodily action and little that is blameless; with much blameworthy verbal action and little that is blameless; with much blameworthy mental action and little that is blameless. This person is called 'one abounding in faults'.

146. Katamo [M 0.0147] ca puggalo appavajjo? Idhekacco puggalo anavajjena bahulaṃ kāyakammena samannāgato [T 0.0185] hoti appaṃ sāvajjena, anavajjena bahulaṃ vacīkammena samannāgato hoti appaṃ sāvajjena, anavajjena bahulaṃ manokammena samannāgato hoti appaṃ sāvajjena – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “appavajjo”.

146. And what kind of person is one with few faults? Here, a certain person is endowed with much blameless bodily action and little that is blameworthy; with much blameless verbal action and little that is blameworthy; with much blameless mental action and little that is blameworthy. This person is called 'one with few faults'.

147. Katamo ca puggalo anavajjo? Idhekacco puggalo anavajjena kāyakammena samannāgato hoti, anavajjena vacīkammena samannāgato hoti, anavajjena manokammena samannāgato hoti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “anavajjo”.

147. And what kind of person is one who is blameless? Here, a certain person is endowed with blameless bodily action, blameless verbal action, and blameless mental action. This person is called 'blameless'.

148. Katamo ca puggalo ugghaṭitaññū? Yassa puggalassa saha udāhaṭavelāya dhammābhisamayo hoti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “ugghaṭitaññū”.

148. And what kind of person is one of quick understanding? The person for whom there is a breakthrough to the Dhamma at the very time a topic is stated. This person is called 'one of quick understanding'.

149. Katamo ca puggalo vipañcitaññū? Yassa puggalassa saṃkhittena bhāsitassa vitthārena atthe vibhajiyamāne dhammābhisamayo hoti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “vipañcitaññū”.

149. And what kind of person is one who understands through detailed exposition? The person for whom there is a breakthrough to the Dhamma when the meaning of what was stated in brief is analyzed in detail. This person is called 'one who understands through detailed exposition'.

150. Katamo [V 0.0150] ca puggalo neyyo? Yassa puggalassa uddesato paripucchato yoniso manasikaroto kalyāṇamitte sevato bhajato payirupāsato evaṃ anupubbena dhammābhisamayo hoti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “neyyo”.

150. And what kind of person is one who is guidable? The person for whom there is a breakthrough to the Dhamma gradually through instruction, questioning, wise attention, and by associating with, resorting to, and attending on good friends. This person is called 'one who is guidable'.

151. Katamo ca puggalo padaparamo? Yassa puggalassa bahumpi suṇato bahumpi bhaṇato bahumpi dhārayato bahumpi vācayato na tāya jātiyā dhammābhisamayo hoti – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “padaparamo”.

151. And what kind of person is one whose highest attainment is the text? It is the person who, though they hear much, speak much, memorize much, and recite much, does not in that very life make a breakthrough to the Dhamma. This person is called 'one whose highest attainment is the text'.

152. Katamo [P 0.0042] ca puggalo yuttappaṭibhāno yuttapaṭibhāṇo (syā.) a. ni. 4.132 no muttappaṭibhāno? Idhekacco puggalo pañhaṃ puṭṭho samāno yuttaṃ vadati no sīghaṃ – ayaṃ [T 0.0186] vuccati puggalo “yuttappaṭibhāno no muttappaṭibhāno”.

152. And what kind of person is astute but not quick-witted? Here, a certain person, when asked a question, speaks pertinently but not quickly. This person is called 'one who is astute but not quick-witted'.

153. Katamo [M 0.0148] ca puggalo muttappaṭibhāno no yuttappaṭibhāno? Idhekacco puggalo pañhaṃ puṭṭho samāno sīghaṃ vadati no yuttaṃ – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “muttappaṭibhāno no yuttappaṭibhāno”.

153. And what kind of person is quick-witted but not astute? Here, a certain person, when asked a question, speaks quickly but not pertinently. This person is called 'one who is quick-witted but not astute'.

154. Katamo ca puggalo yuttappaṭibhāno ca muttappaṭibhāno ca? Idhekacco puggalo pañhaṃ puṭṭho samāno yuttañca vadati sīghañca – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “yuttappaṭibhāno ca muttappaṭibhāno ca”.

154. And what kind of person is both astute and quick-witted? Here, a certain person, when asked a question, speaks both pertinently and quickly. This person is called 'one who is both astute and quick-witted'.

155. Katamo ca puggalo neva yuttappaṭibhāno no muttappaṭibhāno? Idhekacco puggalo pañhaṃ puṭṭho samāno neva yuttaṃ vadati no sīghaṃ – ayaṃ vuccati, puggalo “neva yuttappaṭibhāno no muttappaṭibhāno”.

155. And what kind of person is neither astute nor quick-witted? Here, a certain person, when asked a question, speaks neither pertinently nor quickly. This person is called 'one who is neither astute nor quick-witted'.

156. Tattha katame cattāro dhammakathikā puggalā? Idhekacco dhammakathiko appañca bhāsati asahitañca, parisā cassa na kusalā hoti sahitāsahitassa. Evarūpo dhammakathiko evarūpāya parisāya dhammakathikotveva saṅkhaṃ gacchati.

156. Herein, what are the four kinds of persons who are Dhamma speakers? Here, a certain Dhamma speaker speaks little and what is unbeneficial, and his assembly is not skilled in regard to what is beneficial and unbeneficial. For such an assembly, such a Dhamma speaker is reckoned as a Dhamma speaker.

Idha [V 0.0151] panekacco dhammakathiko appañca bhāsati sahitañca, parisā cassa kusalā hoti sahitāsahitassa. Evarūpo dhammakathiko evarūpāya parisāya dhammakathikotveva saṅkhaṃ gacchati.

Again, here, a certain Dhamma speaker speaks little but what is beneficial, and his assembly is skilled in regard to what is beneficial and unbeneficial. For such an assembly, such a Dhamma speaker is reckoned as a Dhamma speaker.

Idha panekacco dhammakathiko bahuñca bhāsati asahitañca, parisā cassa na kusalā hoti sahitāsahitassa. Evarūpo dhammakathiko evarūpāya parisāya dhammakathikotveva saṅkhaṃ gacchati.

Again, here, a certain Dhamma speaker speaks much but what is unbeneficial, and his assembly is not skilled in regard to what is beneficial and unbeneficial. For such an assembly, such a Dhamma speaker is reckoned as a Dhamma speaker.

Idha panekacco dhammakathiko bahuñca bhāsati sahitañca, parisā cassa kusalā hoti sahitāsahitassa. Evarūpo [T 0.0187] dhammakathiko evarūpāya parisāya dhammakathikotveva saṅkhaṃ gacchati. Ime cattāro “dhammakathikā puggalā”.

Again, here, a certain Dhamma speaker speaks much and what is beneficial, and his assembly is skilled in regard to what is beneficial and unbeneficial. For such an assembly, such a Dhamma speaker is reckoned as a Dhamma speaker. These are the four persons who are Dhamma speakers.

157. Tattha katame cattāro valāhakūpamā puggalā? Cattāro valāhakā – gajjitā no vassitā, vassitā no gajjitā, gajjitā ca vassitā ca, neva gajjitā no vassitā. Evamevaṃ cattārome [P 0.0043] valāhakūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ. Katame cattāro[M 0.0149]? Gajjitā no vassitā, vassitā no gajjitā, gajjitā ca vassitā ca, neva gajjitā no vassitā.

157. Herein, what are the four persons comparable to clouds? There are four kinds of clouds: one that thunders but does not rain, one that rains but does not thunder, one that both thunders and rains, and one that neither thunders nor rains. In the very same way, these four persons comparable to clouds are present and exist in the world. What four? One who thunders but does not rain, one who rains but does not thunder, one who both thunders and rains, and one who neither thunders nor rains.

Kathañca puggalo gajjitā hoti no vassitā? Idhekacco puggalo bhāsitā hoti, no kattā. Evaṃ puggalo gajjitā hoti, no vassitā. Seyyathāpi so valāhako gajjitā no vassitā, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

And how is a person one who thunders but does not rain? Here, a certain person is a speaker but not a doer. In this way, a person is one who thunders but does not rain. Just as that cloud thunders but does not rain, so this person is of a similar nature.

Kathañca puggalo vassitā hoti no gajjitā? Idhekacco puggalo kattā hoti, no bhāsitā. Evaṃ puggalo vassitā hoti no gajjitā. Seyyathāpi so valāhako vassitā no gajjitā, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

And how is a person one who rains but does not thunder? Here, a certain person is a doer but not a speaker. In this way, a person is one who rains but does not thunder. Just as that cloud rains but does not thunder, so this person is of a similar nature.

Kathañca puggalo gajjitā ca hoti vassitā ca? Idhekacco puggalo bhāsitā ca hoti, kattā ca. Evaṃ puggalo gajjitā ca hoti vassitā ca. Seyyathāpi so valāhako gajjitā ca vassitā ca, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

And how is a person one who both thunders and rains? Here, a certain person is both a speaker and a doer. In this way, a person is one who both thunders and rains. Just as that cloud both thunders and rains, so this person is of a similar nature.

Kathañca [V 0.0152] puggalo neva gajjitā hoti no vassitā? Idhekacco puggalo neva bhāsitā hoti no kattā. Evaṃ puggalo neva gajjitā hoti no vassitā. Seyyathāpi so valāhako neva [T 0.0188] gajjitā no vassitā, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

And how is a person one who neither thunders nor rains? Here, a certain person is neither a speaker nor a doer. In this way, a person is one who neither thunders nor rains. Just as that cloud neither thunders nor rains, this person is to be understood as comparable to that.

Ime cattāro valāhakūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ.

These four persons comparable to clouds are found to exist in the world.

158. Tattha katame cattāro mūsikūpamā puggalā? Catasso mūsikā – gādhaṃ kattā no vasitā, vasitā no gādhaṃ kattā, gādhaṃ kattā ca vasitā ca, neva gādhaṃ kattā no vasitā. Evamevaṃ cattārome mūsikūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ. Katame cattāro? Gādhaṃ kattā no vasitā, vasitā no gādhaṃ kattā, gādhaṃ kattā ca vasitā ca, neva gādhaṃ kattā no vasitā.

158. Herein, what are the four persons comparable to mice? There are four kinds of mice: one that makes a burrow but does not dwell in it, one that dwells in a burrow but does not make it, one that both makes a burrow and dwells in it, and one that neither makes a burrow nor dwells in it. In the very same way, these four persons comparable to mice are found to exist in the world. What four? One who makes a burrow but does not dwell in it, one who dwells in a burrow but does not make it, one who both makes a burrow and dwells in it, and one who neither makes a burrow nor dwells in it.

Kathañca puggalo gādhaṃ kattā hoti no vasitā? Idhekacco puggalo dhammaṃ pariyāpuṇāti – suttaṃ geyyaṃ veyyākaraṇaṃ gāthaṃ udānaṃ itivuttakaṃ jātakaṃ abbhutadhammaṃ vedallaṃ. So “idaṃ dukkha”nti yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhasamudayo”ti yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhanirodho”ti [M 0.0150] yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti. Evaṃ puggalo gādhaṃ kattā hoti no vasitā. Seyyathāpi sā mūsikā gādhaṃ kattā no vasitā, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

And how is a person one who makes a burrow but does not dwell in it? Here, a certain person learns the Dhamma—discourses, mixed prose and verse, expositions, verses, inspired utterances, 'thus-saids,' birth stories, amazing phenomena, and question-and-answer texts. He does not understand as it truly is: 'This is suffering.' He does not understand as it truly is: 'This is the origin of suffering.' He does not understand as it truly is: 'This is the cessation of suffering.' He does not understand as it truly is: 'This is the path leading to the cessation of suffering.' In this way, a person is one who makes a burrow but does not dwell in it. Just as that mouse makes a burrow but does not dwell in it, this person is to be understood as comparable to that.

Kathañca [P 0.0044] puggalo vasitā hoti no gādhaṃ kattā? Idhekacco puggalo dhammaṃ na pariyāpuṇāti – suttaṃ geyyaṃ veyyākaraṇaṃ gāthaṃ udānaṃ itivuttakaṃ jātakaṃ abbhutadhammaṃ vedallaṃ. So “idaṃ dukkha”nti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhasamudayo”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ [T 0.0189] dukkhanirodho”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti. Evaṃ puggalo vasitā hoti no gādhaṃ kattā. Seyyathāpi sā mūsikā vasitā no gādhaṃ kattā, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

And how is a person one who dwells in a burrow but does not make it? Here, a certain person does not learn the Dhamma—discourses, mixed prose and verse, expositions, verses, inspired utterances, 'thus-saids,' birth stories, amazing phenomena, and question-and-answer texts. He understands as it truly is: 'This is suffering.' He understands as it truly is: 'This is the origin of suffering.' He understands as it truly is: 'This is the cessation of suffering.' He understands as it truly is: 'This is the path leading to the cessation of suffering.' In this way, a person is one who dwells in a burrow but does not make it. Just as that mouse dwells in a burrow but does not make it, this person is to be understood as comparable to that.

Kathañca puggalo gādhaṃ kattā ca hoti vasitā ca? Idhekacco puggalo dhammaṃ pariyāpuṇāti – suttaṃ geyyaṃ veyyākaraṇaṃ gāthaṃ udānaṃ itivuttakaṃ jātakaṃ abbhutadhammaṃ vedallaṃ. So “idaṃ dukkha”nti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhasamudayo”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhanirodho”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti. Evaṃ puggalo gādhaṃ kattā ca hoti vasitā ca. Seyyathāpi sā mūsikā gādhaṃ kattā ca vasitā ca, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

And how is a person one who both makes a burrow and dwells in it? Here, a certain person learns the Dhamma—discourses, mixed prose and verse, expositions, verses, inspired utterances, 'thus-saids,' birth stories, amazing phenomena, and question-and-answer texts. He understands as it truly is: 'This is suffering.' He understands as it truly is: 'This is the origin of suffering.' He understands as it truly is: 'This is the cessation of suffering.' He understands as it truly is: 'This is the path leading to the cessation of suffering.' In this way, a person is one who both makes a burrow and dwells in it. Just as that mouse both makes a burrow and dwells in it, this person is to be understood as comparable to that.

Kathañca puggalo neva gādhaṃ kattā hoti no vasitā? Idhekacco puggalo dhammaṃ na pariyāpuṇāti – suttaṃ geyyaṃ veyyākaraṇaṃ gāthaṃ udānaṃ itivuttakaṃ jātakaṃ abbhutadhammaṃ vedallaṃ. So [V 0.0153] “idaṃ dukkha”nti yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhasamudayo”ti yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhanirodho”ti yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti. Evaṃ puggalo neva gādhaṃ kattā hoti no vasitā. Seyyathāpi sā mūsikā neva gādhaṃ kattā no vasitā, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

And how is a person one who neither makes a burrow nor dwells in it? Here, a certain person does not learn the Dhamma—discourses, mixed prose and verse, expositions, verses, inspired utterances, 'thus-saids,' birth stories, amazing phenomena, and question-and-answer texts. He does not understand as it truly is: 'This is suffering.' He does not understand as it truly is: 'This is the origin of suffering.' He does not understand as it truly is: 'This is the cessation of suffering.' He does not understand as it truly is: 'This is the path leading to the cessation of suffering.' In this way, a person is one who neither makes a burrow nor dwells in it. Just as that mouse neither makes a burrow nor dwells in it, this person is to be understood as comparable to that.

Ime cattāro mūsikūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ.

These four persons comparable to mice are found to exist in the world.

159. Tattha [T 0.0190][M 0.0151] katame cattāro ambūpamā puggalā? Cattāri ambāni – āmaṃ pakkavaṇṇi pakkavaṇṇī, pakkaṃ āmavaṇṇi āmavaṇṇī (syā. ka.) a. ni. 4.105, āmaṃ āmavaṇṇi, pakkaṃ pakkavaṇṇi. Evamevaṃ cattārome ambūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ. Katame cattāro? Āmo pakkavaṇṇī, pakko āmavaṇṇī, āmo āmavaṇṇī, pakko pakkavaṇṇī.

159. Herein, what are the four persons comparable to mangoes? There are four kinds of mangoes: one that is unripe but has the appearance of a ripe one, one that is ripe but has the appearance of an unripe one, one that is unripe and has the appearance of an unripe one, and one that is ripe and has the appearance of a ripe one. In the very same way, these four persons comparable to mangoes are found to exist in the world. What four? A person who is unripe but has the appearance of a ripe one, a person who is ripe but has the appearance of an unripe one, a person who is unripe and has the appearance of an unripe one, and a person who is ripe and has the appearance of a ripe one.

Kathañca puggalo āmo hoti pakkavaṇṇī? Idhekaccassa puggalassa pāsādikaṃ hoti abhikkantaṃ paṭikkantaṃ ālokitaṃ vilokitaṃ samiñjitaṃ sammiñjitaṃ (sī. syā.) pasāritaṃ saṅghāṭipattacīvaradhāraṇaṃ. So “idaṃ dukkha”nti yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhasamudayo”ti yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhanirodho”ti yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti. Evaṃ puggalo āmo hoti pakkavaṇṇī. Seyyathāpi taṃ ambaṃ āmaṃ pakkavaṇṇi, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

And how is a person unripe but has a ripe appearance? Here, for a certain person, the going forward and returning, the looking ahead and looking aside, the bending and stretching, and the bearing of the outer robe, bowl, and robes are inspiring. But he does not understand as it truly is: ‘This is suffering’; he does not understand as it truly is: ‘This is the origin of suffering’; he does not understand as it truly is: ‘This is the cessation of suffering’; he does not understand as it truly is: ‘This is the path leading to the cessation of suffering.’ In this way, a person is unripe but has a ripe appearance. Just as that mango is unripe but has a ripe appearance, this person is to be understood as comparable to that.

Kathañca puggalo pakko hoti āmavaṇṇī? Idhekaccassa [P 0.0045] puggalassa na pāsādikaṃ hoti abhikkantaṃ paṭikkantaṃ ālokitaṃ vilokitaṃ samiñjitaṃ pasāritaṃ saṅghāṭipattacīvaradhāraṇaṃ. So “idaṃ dukkha”nti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhasamudayo”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhanirodho”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti. Evaṃ puggalo pakko hoti āmavaṇṇī. Seyyathāpi taṃ ambaṃ pakkaṃ āmavaṇṇi, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

And how is a person ripe but has an unripe appearance? Here, for a certain person, the going forward and returning, the looking ahead and looking aside, the bending and stretching, and the bearing of the outer robe, bowl, and robes are not inspiring. But he understands as it truly is: ‘This is suffering’; he understands as it truly is: ‘This is the origin of suffering’; he understands as it truly is: ‘This is the cessation of suffering’; he understands as it truly is: ‘This is the path leading to the cessation of suffering.’ In this way, a person is ripe but has an unripe appearance. Just as that mango is ripe but has an unripe appearance, this person is to be understood as comparable to that.

Kathañca puggalo āmo hoti āmavaṇṇī? Idhekaccassa puggalassa [T 0.0191] na pāsādikaṃ hoti abhikkantaṃ paṭikkantaṃ ālokitaṃ vilokitaṃ samiñjitaṃ pasāritaṃ saṅghāṭipattacīvaradhāraṇaṃ. So “idaṃ dukkha”nti yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti…pe… “ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti. Evaṃ puggalo āmo hoti āmavaṇṇī. Seyyathāpi taṃ ambaṃ āmaṃ āmavaṇṇi, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

And how is a person unripe and has an unripe appearance? Here, for a certain person, the going forward and returning, the looking ahead and looking aside, the bending and stretching, and the bearing of the outer robe, bowl, and robe are not inspiring. And he does not understand as it really is: ‘This is suffering’ … he does not understand as it really is: ‘This is the path leading to the cessation of suffering.’ In this way, a person is unripe and has an unripe appearance. Just as that mango is unripe and has an unripe appearance, this person is to be understood as comparable to that.

Kathañca puggalo pakko hoti pakkavaṇṇī? Idhekaccassa puggalassa pāsādikaṃ hoti abhikkantaṃ paṭikkantaṃ ālokitaṃ vilokitaṃ samiñjitaṃ pasāritaṃ saṅghāṭipattacīvaradhāraṇaṃ[V 0.0154]. So “idaṃ dukkha”nti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti [M 0.0152] …pe… “ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti. Evaṃ puggalo pakko hoti pakkavaṇṇī. Seyyathāpi taṃ ambaṃ pakkaṃ pakkavaṇṇi, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

And how is a person ripe and has a ripe appearance? Here, for a certain person, the going forward and returning, the looking ahead and looking aside, the bending and stretching, and the bearing of the outer robe, bowl, and robe are inspiring. And he understands as it really is: ‘This is suffering’ … he understands as it really is: ‘This is the path leading to the cessation of suffering.’ In this way, a person is ripe and has a ripe appearance. Just as that mango is ripe and has a ripe appearance, this person is to be understood as comparable to that.

Ime cattāro ambūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ.

These four persons comparable to mangoes are found existing in the world.

160. Tattha katame cattāro kumbhūpamā puggalā? Cattāro kumbhā – tuccho pihito, pūro vivaṭo, tuccho vivaṭo, pūro pihito. Evamevaṃ cattārome kumbhūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ. Katame cattāro? Tuccho pihito, pūro vivaṭo, tuccho vivaṭo, pūro pihito.

160. Now, what are the four persons comparable to pots? There are four kinds of pots: empty and covered, full and open, empty and open, and full and covered. In the same way, these four persons comparable to pots are found existing in the world. What four? The empty and covered, the full and open, the empty and open, and the full and covered.

Kathañca puggalo tuccho hoti pihito? Idhekaccassa puggalassa pāsādikaṃ hoti abhikkantaṃ paṭikkantaṃ ālokitaṃ vilokitaṃ samiñjitaṃ pasāritaṃ saṅghāṭipattacīvaradhāraṇaṃ. So “idaṃ dukkha”nti yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti…pe… “ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī [T 0.0192] paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti. Evaṃ puggalo tuccho hoti pihito. Seyyathāpi so kumbho tuccho pihito, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

And how is a person empty and covered? Here, for a certain person, the going forward and returning, the looking ahead and looking aside, the bending and stretching, and the bearing of the outer robe, bowl, and robe are inspiring. But he does not understand as it really is: ‘This is suffering’ … he does not understand as it really is: ‘This is the path leading to the cessation of suffering.’ In this way, a person is empty and covered. Just as that pot is empty and covered, this person is to be understood as comparable to that.

Kathañca puggalo pūro hoti vivaṭo? Idhekaccassa puggalassa na pāsādikaṃ hoti abhikkantaṃ paṭikkantaṃ ālokitaṃ vilokitaṃ samiñjitaṃ pasāritaṃ saṅghāṭipattacīvaradhāraṇaṃ. So “idaṃ dukkha”nti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti…pe… “ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti. Evaṃ puggalo pūro hoti vivaṭo. Seyyathāpi so kumbho pūro vivaṭo, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

And how is a person full and open? Here, for a certain person, the going forward and returning, the looking ahead and looking aside, the bending and stretching, and the bearing of the outer robe, bowl, and robe are not inspiring. But he understands as it really is: ‘This is suffering’ … he understands as it really is: ‘This is the path leading to the cessation of suffering.’ In this way, a person is full and open. Just as that pot is full and open, this person is to be understood as comparable to that.

Kathañca [P 0.0046] puggalo tuccho hoti vivaṭo? Idhekaccassa puggalassa na pāsādikaṃ hoti abhikkantaṃ paṭikkantaṃ ālokitaṃ vilokitaṃ samiñjitaṃ pasāritaṃ saṅghāṭipattacīvaradhāraṇaṃ. So “idaṃ dukkha”nti yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti…pe… “ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ [V 0.0155] nappajānāti. Evaṃ puggalo tuccho hoti vivaṭo. Seyyathāpi so kumbho tuccho vivaṭo, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

So “idaṃ dukkha”nti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti…pe… “ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti. Evaṃ puggalo pūro hoti pihito. Seyyathāpi so kumbho pūro pihito, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo. Ime cattāro kumbhūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ.

Just as that pot is full and covered, this person is to be understood as comparable to that. These four persons comparable to pots are found existing in the world.

161. Tattha [T 0.0193] katame cattāro udakarahadūpamā puggalā? Cattāro udakarahadā – uttāno gambhīrobhāso, gambhīro uttānobhāso, uttāno uttānobhāso, gambhīro gambhīrobhāso. Evamevaṃ cattārome udakarahadūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ. Katame cattāro? Uttāno gambhīrobhāso, gambhīro uttānobhāso, uttāno uttānobhāso, gambhīro gambhīrobhāso.

161. Now, what are the four persons comparable to lakes of water? There are four kinds of lakes of water: shallow but appearing deep, deep but appearing shallow, shallow and appearing shallow, and deep and appearing deep. In the same way, these four persons comparable to lakes of water are found existing in the world. What four? The shallow but appearing deep, the deep but appearing shallow, the shallow and appearing shallow, and the deep and appearing deep.

Kathañca puggalo uttāno hoti gambhīrobhāso? Idhekaccassa puggalassa pāsādikaṃ hoti abhikkantaṃ paṭikkantaṃ ālokitaṃ vilokitaṃ samiñjitaṃ pasāritaṃ saṅghāṭipattacīvaradhāraṇaṃ. So [V 0.0156] “idaṃ dukkha”nti yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti…pe… “ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti. Evaṃ puggalo uttāno hoti gambhīrobhāso. Seyyathāpi so udakarahado uttāno gambhīrobhāso, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

And how is a person shallow but appearing deep? Here, for a certain person, the going forward and returning, the looking ahead and looking aside, the bending and stretching, and the bearing of the outer robe, bowl, and robe are inspiring. But he does not understand as it really is: ‘This is suffering’ … he does not understand as it really is: ‘This is the path leading to the cessation of suffering.’ In this way, a person is shallow but appearing deep. Just as that pool of water is shallow but appears deep, this person is to be understood as comparable to that.

Kathañca puggalo gambhīro hoti uttānobhāso? Idhekaccassa puggalassa na pāsādikaṃ hoti abhikkantaṃ paṭikkantaṃ ālokitaṃ vilokitaṃ samiñjitaṃ pasāritaṃ saṅghāṭipattacīvaradhāraṇaṃ. So “idaṃ dukkha”nti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti…pe… “ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti. Evaṃ puggalo gambhīro hoti uttānobhāso. Seyyathāpi so udakarahado gambhīro uttānobhāso, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

And how is a person deep but appearing shallow? Here, for a certain person, the going forward and returning, the looking ahead and looking aside, the bending and stretching, and the bearing of the outer robe, bowl, and robe are not inspiring. But he understands as it really is: ‘This is suffering’ … he understands as it really is: ‘This is the path leading to the cessation of suffering.’ In this way, a person is deep but appearing shallow. Just as that pool of water is deep but appears shallow, this person is to be understood as comparable to that.

Kathañca puggalo uttāno hoti uttānobhāso? Idhekaccassa [P 0.0047] puggalassa na pāsādikaṃ hoti abhikkantaṃ paṭikkantaṃ ālokitaṃ vilokitaṃ [T 0.0194] samiñjitaṃ pasāritaṃ saṅghāṭipattacīvaradhāraṇaṃ. So “idaṃ dukkha”nti yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti…pe… “ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ [M 0.0154] nappajānāti. Evaṃ puggalo uttāno hoti uttānobhāso. Seyyathāpi so udakarahado uttāno uttānobhāso, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

And how is a person shallow and appearing shallow? Here, a certain person’s going forward and returning, looking ahead and looking aside, bending and stretching, and wearing of the double-layered robe, bowl, and robe is not inspiring. He does not understand as it really is: ‘This is suffering’ … he does not understand as it really is: ‘This is the path of practice leading to the cessation of suffering.’ In this way, a person is shallow and appearing shallow. Just as that pool of water is shallow and appears shallow, so this person is to be understood as comparable to it.

Kathañca puggalo gambhīro hoti gambhīrobhāso? Idhekaccassa puggalassa pāsādikaṃ hoti abhikkantaṃ paṭikkantaṃ ālokitaṃ vilokitaṃ samiñjitaṃ pasāritaṃ saṅghāṭipattacīvaradhāraṇaṃ. So “idaṃ dukkha”nti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti…pe… “ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti. Evaṃ puggalo gambhīro hoti gambhīrobhāso. Seyyathāpi so udakarahado gambhīro gambhīrobhāso, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo. Ime cattāro udakarahadūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ.

And how is a person deep and appearing deep? Here, a certain person’s going forward and returning, looking ahead and looking aside, bending and stretching, and wearing of the double-layered robe, bowl, and robe is inspiring. He understands as it really is: ‘This is suffering’ … he understands as it really is: ‘This is the path of practice leading to the cessation of suffering.’ In this way, a person is deep and appearing deep. Just as that pool of water is deep and appears deep, so this person is to be understood as comparable to it. These four persons comparable to pools of water are found existing in the world.

162. Tattha katame cattāro balībaddūpamā puggalā? Cattāro balībaddā balibaddhā (syā.) – sakagavacaṇḍo sagavacaṇḍo (ka. sī.) a. ni. 4.108 no paragavacaṇḍo, paragavacaṇḍo no sakagavacaṇḍo, sakagavacaṇḍo ca paragavacaṇḍo ca, neva sakagavacaṇḍo no paragavacaṇḍo. Evamevaṃ cattārome balībaddūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ. Katame cattāro? Sakagavacaṇḍo no paragavacaṇḍo, paragavacaṇḍo no sakagavacaṇḍo, sakagavacaṇḍo ca paragavacaṇḍo ca, neva sakagavacaṇḍo no paragavacaṇḍo.

162. Among them, what are the four persons comparable to bulls? There are four kinds of bulls: one that is fierce toward its own herd but not toward another’s herd; one that is fierce toward another’s herd but not toward its own herd; one that is fierce toward both its own herd and another’s herd; and one that is fierce toward neither its own herd nor another’s herd. In the same way, these four persons comparable to bulls are found existing in the world. What four? One who is like a bull that is fierce toward its own herd but not toward another’s herd; one who is like a bull that is fierce toward another’s herd but not toward its own herd; one who is like a bull that is fierce toward both its own herd and another’s herd; and one who is like a bull that is fierce toward neither its own herd nor another’s herd.

Kathañca [V 0.0157] puggalo sakagavacaṇḍo hoti no paragavacaṇḍo? Idhekacco puggalo sakaparisaṃ ubbejitā [T 0.0195] hoti, no paraparisaṃ. Evaṃ puggalo sakagavacaṇḍo hoti no paragavacaṇḍo. Seyyathāpi so balībaddo sakagavacaṇḍo no paragavacaṇḍo, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

And how is a person like a bull that is fierce toward its own herd but not toward another’s herd? Here, a certain person intimidates his own assembly, but not another’s assembly. In this way, a person is like a bull that is fierce toward its own herd but not toward another’s herd. Just as that bull is fierce toward its own herd but not toward another’s, so this person is to be understood as comparable to it.

Kathañca puggalo paragavacaṇḍo hoti no sakagavacaṇḍo? Idhekacco puggalo paraparisaṃ ubbejitā hoti, no sakaparisaṃ. Evaṃ puggalo paragavacaṇḍo hoti no sakagavacaṇḍo. Seyyathāpi so balībaddo paragavacaṇḍo no sakagavacaṇḍo, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

And how is a person like a bull that is fierce toward another’s herd but not toward his own herd? Here, a certain person intimidates another’s assembly, but not his own assembly. In this way, a person is like a bull that is fierce toward another’s herd but not toward his own herd. Just as that bull is fierce toward another’s herd but not toward its own, so this person is to be understood as comparable to it.

Kathañca puggalo sakagavacaṇḍo ca hoti paragavacaṇḍo ca? Idhekacco puggalo sakaparisañca ubbejitā hoti, paraparisañca. Evaṃ puggalo [M 0.0155] sakagavacaṇḍo ca hoti paragavacaṇḍo ca. Seyyathāpi so balībaddo sakagavacaṇḍo ca paragavacaṇḍo ca, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

And how is a person like a bull that is fierce toward his own herd and also toward another’s herd? Here, a certain person intimidates his own assembly and also another’s assembly. In this way, a person is like a bull that is fierce toward his own herd and also toward another’s herd. Just as that bull is fierce toward its own herd and also toward another’s herd, so this person is to be understood as comparable to it.

Kathañca puggalo neva sakagavacaṇḍo hoti no paragavacaṇḍo? Idhekacco puggalo neva sakaparisaṃ ubbejitā hoti no paraparisaṃ. Evaṃ puggalo neva sakagavacaṇḍo hoti no paragavacaṇḍo. Seyyathāpi so balībaddo neva sakagavacaṇḍo no paragavacaṇḍo, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo. Ime cattāro balībaddūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ.

And how is a person like a bull that is fierce toward neither his own herd nor another’s herd? Here, a certain person intimidates neither his own assembly nor another’s assembly. In this way, a person is like a bull that is fierce toward neither his own herd nor another’s herd. Just as that bull is fierce toward neither its own herd nor another’s herd, so this person is to be understood as comparable to it. These four persons comparable to bulls are found existing in the world.

163. Tattha [P 0.0048] katame cattāro āsīvisūpamā puggalā? Cattāro āsīvisā āsivisā (syā.) – āgataviso no ghoraviso, ghoraviso no āgataviso, āgataviso ca ghoraviso ca, neva āgataviso no ghoraviso. Evamevaṃ [T 0.0196] cattārome āsīvisūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ. Katame cattāro? Āgataviso no ghoraviso, ghoraviso no āgataviso, āgataviso ca ghoraviso ca, neva āgataviso no ghoraviso.

163. Among them, what are the four persons comparable to venomous snakes? There are four kinds of venomous snakes: one with swift-acting venom but not virulent venom; one with virulent venom but not swift-acting venom; one with venom that is both swift-acting and virulent; and one with venom that is neither swift-acting nor virulent. In the same way, these four persons comparable to venomous snakes are found existing in the world. What four? One who is like a snake with swift-acting venom but not virulent venom; one who is like a snake with virulent venom but not swift-acting venom; one who is like a snake with venom that is both swift-acting and virulent; and one who is like a snake with venom that is neither swift-acting nor virulent.

Kathañca puggalo āgataviso hoti no ghoraviso? Idhekacco puggalo abhiṇhaṃ kujjhati. So ca khvassa kodho na ciraṃ dīgharattaṃ anuseti. Evaṃ puggalo āgataviso hoti, no ghoraviso. Seyyathāpi so āsīviso āgataviso no ghoraviso, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

And how is a person like a snake with swift-acting venom but not virulent venom? Here, a certain person frequently gets angry, but that anger of his does not lie latent for a long time. In this way, a person is like a snake with swift-acting venom but not virulent venom. Just as that venomous snake has swift-acting venom but not virulent venom, so this person is to be understood as comparable to it.

Kathañca [V 0.0158] puggalo ghoraviso hoti no āgataviso? Idhekacco puggalo naheva kho neva kho (sī.) a. ni. 4.110 abhiṇhaṃ kujjhati. So ca khvassa kodho ciraṃ dīgharattaṃ anuseti. Evaṃ puggalo ghoraviso hoti, no āgataviso. Seyyathāpi so āsīviso ghoraviso no āgataviso, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

And how is a person like a snake with virulent venom but not swift-acting venom? Here, a certain person does not frequently get angry, but that anger of his lies latent for a long time. In this way, a person is like a snake with virulent venom but not swift-acting venom. Just as that venomous snake has virulent venom but not swift-acting venom, so this person is to be understood as comparable to it.

Kathañca puggalo āgataviso ca hoti ghoraviso ca? Idhekacco puggalo abhiṇhaṃ kujjhati. So ca khvassa kodho ciraṃ dīgharattaṃ anuseti. Evaṃ puggalo āgataviso ca hoti ghoraviso ca. Seyyathāpi [M 0.0156] so āsīviso āgataviso ca ghoraviso ca, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

And how is a person like a snake with venom that is both swift-acting and virulent? Here, a certain person frequently gets angry, and that anger of his lies latent for a long time. In this way, a person is like a snake with venom that is both swift-acting and virulent. Just as that venomous snake has venom that is both swift-acting and virulent, so this person is to be understood as comparable to it.

Kathañca puggalo neva āgataviso hoti no ghoraviso? Idhekacco puggalo naheva kho abhiṇhaṃ kujjhati. So ca khvassa kodho na ciraṃ dīgharattaṃ anuseti. Evaṃ puggalo neva āgataviso hoti no ghoraviso. Seyyathāpi so āsīviso neva āgataviso no ghoraviso, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo. Ime cattāro āsīvisūpamā puggalā santo [T 0.0197] saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ.

And how is a person like a snake with venom that is neither swift-acting nor virulent? Here, a certain person does not frequently get angry, and that anger of his does not lie latent for a long time. In this way, a person is like a snake with venom that is neither swift-acting nor virulent. Just as that venomous snake has venom that is neither swift-acting nor virulent, so this person is to be understood as comparable to it. These four persons comparable to venomous snakes are found existing in the world.

164. Kathañca puggalo ananuvicca apariyogāhetvā avaṇṇārahassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti? Idhekacco puggalo duppaṭipannānaṃ micchāpaṭipannānaṃ titthiyānaṃ titthiyasāvakānaṃ vaṇṇaṃ bhāsati – “suppaṭipannā” itipi, “sammāpaṭipannā” itipīti. Evaṃ puggalo ananuvicca apariyogāhetvā avaṇṇārahassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti.

164. And how is a person one who, without reflecting and without comprehending, speaks praise of one who is unworthy of praise? Here, a certain person speaks praise of sectarians and their disciples who are of bad and wrong practice, saying: ‘They are of good practice,’ and ‘They are of right practice.’ In this way, a person is one who, without reflecting and without comprehending, speaks praise of one who is unworthy of praise.

Kathañca puggalo ananuvicca apariyogāhetvā vaṇṇārahassa avaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti? Idhekacco [P 0.0049] puggalo suppaṭipannānaṃ sammāpaṭipannānaṃ buddhānaṃ buddhasāvakānaṃ avaṇṇaṃ bhāsati – “duppaṭipannā” itipi, “micchāpaṭipannā” itipīti. Evaṃ puggalo ananuvicca apariyogāhetvā vaṇṇārahassa avaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti.

And how is a person one who, without reflecting and without comprehending, speaks dispraise of one who is worthy of praise? Here, a certain person speaks dispraise of the Buddhas and the disciples of the Buddhas who are of good and right practice, saying: ‘They are of bad practice,’ and ‘They are of wrong practice.’ In this way, a person is one who, without reflecting and without comprehending, speaks dispraise of one who is worthy of praise.

Kathañca puggalo ananuvicca apariyogāhetvā appasādanīye ṭhāne pasādaṃ upadaṃsitā hoti? Idhekacco puggalo duppaṭipadāya micchāpaṭipadāya pasādaṃ janeti – “suppaṭipadā” itipi[V 0.0159], “sammāpaṭipadā” itipīti. Evaṃ puggalo ananuvicca apariyogāhetvā appasādanīye ṭhāne pasādaṃ upadaṃsitā hoti.

And how is a person one who, without reflecting and without comprehending, shows confidence in a matter that is not inspiring? Here, a certain person generates confidence in bad practice and wrong practice, saying: ‘This is good practice,’ and ‘This is right practice.’ In this way, a person is one who, without reflecting and without comprehending, shows confidence in a matter that is not inspiring.

Kathañca puggalo ananuvicca apariyogāhetvā pasādanīye ṭhāne appasādaṃ upadaṃsitā hoti? Idhekacco puggalo suppaṭipadāya sammāpaṭipadāya appasādaṃ janeti – “duppaṭipadā” itipi, “micchāpaṭipadā” itipīti. Evaṃ puggalo ananuvicca apariyogāhetvā pasādanīye ṭhāne appasādaṃ upadaṃsitā hoti.

And how is a person one who, without reflecting and without comprehending, shows a lack of confidence in a matter that is inspiring? Here, a certain person generates a lack of confidence in good practice and right practice, saying: ‘This is bad practice,’ and ‘This is wrong practice.’ In this way, a person is one who, without reflecting and without comprehending, shows a lack of confidence in a matter that is inspiring.

165. Kathañca [M 0.0157] puggalo anuvicca pariyogāhetvā avaṇṇārahassa avaṇṇaṃ [T 0.0198] bhāsitā hoti? Idhekacco puggalo duppaṭipannānaṃ micchāpaṭipannānaṃ titthiyānaṃ titthiyasāvakānaṃ avaṇṇaṃ bhāsati – “duppaṭipannā” itipi, “micchāpaṭipannā” itipīti. Evaṃ puggalo anuvicca pariyogāhetvā avaṇṇārahassa avaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti.

165. And how is a person one who, having reflected and comprehended, speaks dispraise of one who is unworthy of praise? Here, a certain person speaks dispraise of sectarians and their disciples who are of bad and wrong practice, saying: ‘They are of bad practice,’ and ‘They are of wrong practice.’ In this way, a person is one who, having reflected and comprehended, speaks dispraise of one who is unworthy of praise.

Kathañca puggalo anuvicca pariyogāhetvā vaṇṇārahassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti? Idhekacco puggalo suppaṭipannānaṃ sammāpaṭipannānaṃ buddhānaṃ buddhasāvakānaṃ vaṇṇaṃ bhāsati – “suppaṭipannā” itipi, “sammāpaṭipannā” itipīti. Evaṃ puggalo anuvicca pariyogāhetvā vaṇṇārahassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti.

And how is a person one who, having reflected and comprehended, speaks praise of one who is worthy of praise? Here, a certain person speaks praise of the Buddhas and the disciples of the Buddhas who are of good and right practice, saying: ‘They are of good practice,’ and ‘They are of right practice.’ In this way, a person is one who, having reflected and comprehended, speaks praise of one who is worthy of praise.

Kathañca puggalo anuvicca pariyogāhetvā appasādanīye ṭhāne appasādaṃ upadaṃsitā hoti? Idhekacco puggalo duppaṭipadāya micchāpaṭipadāya appasādaṃ janeti – “duppaṭipadā” itipi, “micchāpaṭipadā” itipīti. Evaṃ puggalo anuvicca pariyogāhetvā appasādanīye ṭhāne appasādaṃ upadaṃsitā hoti.

And how is a person one who, having reflected and comprehended, shows a lack of confidence in a matter that is not inspiring? Here, a certain person generates a lack of confidence in bad practice and wrong practice, saying: ‘This is bad practice,’ and ‘This is wrong practice.’ In this way, a person is one who, having reflected and comprehended, shows a lack of confidence in a matter that is not inspiring.

Kathañca puggalo anuvicca pariyogāhetvā pasādanīye ṭhāne pasādaṃ upadaṃsitā hoti? Idhekacco [P 0.0050] puggalo suppaṭipadāya sammāpaṭipadāya pasādaṃ janeti – “suppaṭipadā” itipi, “sammāpaṭipadā” itipīti. Evaṃ puggalo anuvicca pariyogāhetvā pasādanīye ṭhāne pasādaṃ upadaṃsitā hoti.

And how is a person one who, having reflected and comprehended, shows confidence in a matter that is inspiring? Here, a certain person generates confidence in good practice and right practice, saying: ‘This is good practice,’ and ‘This is right practice.’ In this way, a person is one who, having reflected and comprehended, shows confidence in a matter that is inspiring.

166. Kathañca [V 0.0160] puggalo avaṇṇārahassa avaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena, no ca kho vaṇṇārahassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena? Idhekacco puggalo vaṇṇopi saṃvijjati avaṇṇopi saṃvijjati[T 0.0199]. Yo tattha avaṇṇo taṃ bhaṇati bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena, yo tattha vaṇṇo taṃ na bhaṇati bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena. Evaṃ puggalo avaṇṇārahassa avaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena, no ca kho vaṇṇārahassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena.

166. And how is a person one who speaks dispraise of one unworthy of praise factually, truthfully, and at the proper time, but is not one who speaks praise of one worthy of praise factually, truthfully, and at the proper time? Here, a certain person has both praiseworthy and blameworthy qualities. As to the blameworthy qualities that are there, he speaks of them factually, truthfully, and at the proper time. As to the praiseworthy qualities that are there, he does not speak of them factually, truthfully, and at the proper time. In this way, a person is one who speaks dispraise of one unworthy of praise factually, truthfully, and at the proper time, but is not one who speaks praise of one worthy of praise factually, truthfully, and at the proper time.

Kathañca puggalo vaṇṇārahassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena, no ca kho avaṇṇārahassa avaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena? Idhekacco puggalo vaṇṇopi saṃvijjati avaṇṇopi saṃvijjati[M 0.0158]. Yo tattha vaṇṇo taṃ bhaṇati bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena, yo tattha avaṇṇo taṃ na bhaṇati bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena. Evaṃ puggalo vaṇṇārahassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena, no ca kho avaṇṇārahassa avaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena.

And how is a person one who speaks praise of one worthy of praise factually, truthfully, and at the proper time, but is not one who speaks dispraise of one unworthy of praise factually, truthfully, and at the proper time? Here, regarding a certain person, there are both grounds for praise and for dispraise. He speaks of the grounds for praise factually, truthfully, and at the proper time, but he does not speak of the grounds for dispraise factually, truthfully, and at the proper time. In this way, a person is one who speaks praise of one worthy of praise factually, truthfully, and at the proper time, but is not one who speaks dispraise of one unworthy of praise factually, truthfully, and at the proper time.

Kathañca puggalo avaṇṇārahassa ca avaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena, vaṇṇārahassa ca vaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena? Idhekacco puggalo vaṇṇopi saṃvijjati avaṇṇopi saṃvijjati. Yo tattha avaṇṇo taṃ bhaṇati bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena, yopi tattha vaṇṇo tampi bhaṇati bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena. Tatra kālaññū hoti tassa pañhassa veyyākaraṇāya. Evaṃ puggalo avaṇṇārahassa ca avaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena, vaṇṇārahassa ca vaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena.

And how is a person one who speaks dispraise of one unworthy of praise factually, truthfully, and at the proper time, and also speaks praise of one worthy of praise factually, truthfully, and at the proper time? Here, a certain person has both blameworthy and praiseworthy qualities. He speaks of the blameworthy qualities factually, truthfully, and at the proper time; and he also speaks of the praiseworthy qualities factually, truthfully, and at the proper time. In that matter, he knows the right time for answering that question. In this way, a person is one who speaks dispraise of one unworthy of praise factually, truthfully, and at the proper time, and also speaks praise of one worthy of praise factually, truthfully, and at the proper time.

Kathañca puggalo neva avaṇṇārahassa avaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena, nopi vaṇṇārahassa vaṇṇaṃ [T 0.0200] bhāsitā hoti bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena? Idhekacco puggalo vaṇṇopi saṃvijjati avaṇṇopi saṃvijjati. Yo tattha avaṇṇo taṃ na bhaṇati bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena, yopi tattha vaṇṇo tampi na bhaṇati bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ [P 0.0051] kālena. Upekkhako viharati sato sampajāno. Evaṃ puggalo neva avaṇṇārahassa avaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena, nopi vaṇṇārahassa vaṇṇaṃ bhāsitā hoti bhūtaṃ tacchaṃ kālena.

And how is it that a person speaks neither dispraise of one deserving dispraise truthfully, factually, and at the right time, nor praise of one deserving praise truthfully, factually, and at the right time? Here, a certain person has both praiseworthy and blameworthy qualities. They do not speak of the blameworthy qualities truthfully, factually, and at the right time; nor do they speak of the praiseworthy qualities truthfully, factually, and at the right time. They dwell equanimously, mindful and clearly comprehending. In this way, a person speaks neither dispraise of one deserving dispraise truthfully, factually, and at the right time, nor praise of one deserving praise truthfully, factually, and at the right time.

167. Katamo ca puggalo uṭṭhānaphalūpajīvī no puññaphalūpajīvī? Yassa puggalassa uṭṭhahato [V 0.0161] ghaṭato vāyamato ājīvo abhinibbattati, no puññato – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “uṭṭhānaphalūpajīvī, no puññaphalūpajīvī”.

167. And what kind of person lives on the fruit of exertion, not on the fruit of merit? It is the person whose livelihood is produced by exertion, effort, and striving, not by merit. This person is called one who 'lives on the fruit of exertion, not on the fruit of merit.'

Katamo ca puggalo puññaphalūpajīvī no uṭṭhānaphalūpajīvī? Paranimmitavasavattī deve paranimmitavasavattideve (sī. syā.) upādāya tatūpari devā puññaphalūpajīvino na uṭṭhānaphalūpajīvino.

And what kind of person lives on the fruit of merit, not on the fruit of exertion? Beginning with the devas who wield power over the creations of others, the devas above them live on the fruit of merit, not on the fruit of exertion.

Katamo [M 0.0159] ca puggalo uṭṭhānaphalūpajīvī ca puññaphalūpajīvī ca? Yassa puggalassa uṭṭhahato ghaṭato vāyamato ājīvo abhinibbattati puññato ca – ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “uṭṭhānaphalūpajīvī ca puññaphalūpajīvī ca”.

And what kind of person lives on both the fruit of exertion and the fruit of merit? It is the person whose livelihood is produced by exertion, effort, and striving, and also by merit. This person is called one who 'lives on both the fruit of exertion and the fruit of merit.'

Katamo ca puggalo neva uṭṭhānaphalūpajīvī no puññaphalūpajīvī? Nerayikā neva uṭṭhānaphalūpajīvino no puññaphalūpajīvino.

And what kind of person lives on neither the fruit of exertion nor the fruit of merit? Beings in hell live on neither the fruit of exertion nor the fruit of merit.

168. Kathañca puggalo tamo hoti tamaparāyano? Idhekacco puggalo nīce kule paccājāto hoti – caṇḍālakule vā nesādakule vā venakule veṇakule (sī. syā.) vā rathakārakule vā pukkusakule vā dalidde daḷidde (sī.) passa aṅguttaranikāye appannapānabhojane [T 0.0201] kasiravuttike, yattha kasirena ghāsacchādo labbhati. So ca hoti dubbaṇṇo duddasiko okoṭimako bahvābādho kāṇo vā kuṇī vā khañjo vā pakkhahato vā, na lābhī annassa pānassa vatthassa yānassa mālāgandhavilepanassa seyyāvasathapadīpeyyassa. So kāyena duccaritaṃ carati, vācāya duccaritaṃ carati, manasā duccaritaṃ carati. So kāyena duccaritaṃ caritvā vācāya duccaritaṃ caritvā manasā duccaritaṃ caritvā kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā apāyaṃ duggatiṃ vinipātaṃ nirayaṃ upapajjati. Evaṃ puggalo tamo hoti tamaparāyano.

168. And how is a person one who comes from darkness and is destined for darkness? Here, a certain person is reborn in a low family—a family of outcastes, hunters, bamboo-workers, cartwrights, or scavengers; a poor family with little food and drink and a difficult livelihood, where food and clothing are obtained with difficulty. And they are ugly, unsightly, dwarfish, and very sickly: one-eyed, or with a crippled hand, or lame, or paralyzed. They do not obtain food, drink, clothing, vehicles, garlands, scents, and unguents, a bed, a dwelling, and a lamp. They engage in misconduct by body, speech, and mind. Having engaged in misconduct by body, speech, and mind, on the breakup of the body, after death, they are reborn in the plane of misery, a bad destination, the lower world, in hell. In this way, a person is one who comes from darkness and is destined for darkness.

Kathañca puggalo tamo hoti jotiparāyano? Idhekacco puggalo nīce kule paccājāto hoti – caṇḍālakule vā nesādakule vā venakule vā rathakārakule vā pukkusakule vā dalidde appannapānabhojane kasiravuttike, yattha kasirena ghāsacchādo labbhati. So ca hoti dubbaṇṇo duddasiko okoṭimako bahvābādho kāṇo vā kuṇī vā khañjo vā pakkhahato vā, na lābhī annassa pānassa vatthassa yānassa mālāgandhavilepanassa seyyāvasathapadīpeyyassa. So kāyena [V 0.0162] sucaritaṃ carati, vācāya sucaritaṃ carati[P 0.0052], manasā sucaritaṃ carati. So kāyena sucaritaṃ caritvā vācāya sucaritaṃ caritvā manasā sucaritaṃ caritvā kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā sugatiṃ saggaṃ lokaṃ upapajjati. Evaṃ puggalo tamo hoti jotiparāyano.

And how is a person one who comes from darkness and is destined for light? Here, a certain person is reborn in a low family—a family of outcastes, hunters, bamboo-workers, cartwrights, or scavengers; a poor family with little food and drink and a difficult livelihood, where food and clothing are obtained with difficulty. And they are ugly, unsightly, dwarfish, and very sickly: one-eyed, or with a crippled hand, or lame, or paralyzed. They do not obtain food, drink, clothing, vehicles, garlands, scents, and unguents, a bed, a dwelling, and a lamp. They engage in good conduct by body, speech, and mind. Having engaged in good conduct by body, speech, and mind, on the breakup of the body, after death, they are reborn in a good destination, a heavenly world. In this way, a person is one who comes from darkness and is destined for light.

Kathañca puggalo joti hoti tamaparāyano? Idhekacco puggalo ucce kule paccājāto hoti – khattiyamahāsālakule vā brāhmaṇamahāsālakule [M 0.0160] vā [T 0.0202] gahapatimahāsālakule vā aḍḍhe mahaddhane mahābhoge pahūtajātarūparajate pahūtavittūpakaraṇe pahūtadhanadhaññe. So ca hoti abhirūpo dassanīyo pāsādiko paramāya vaṇṇapokkharatāya samannāgato, lābhī annassa pānassa vatthassa yānassa mālāgandhavilepanassa seyyāvasathapadīpeyyassa. So kāyena duccaritaṃ carati, vācāya duccaritaṃ carati, manasā duccaritaṃ carati. So kāyena duccaritaṃ caritvā vācāya duccaritaṃ caritvā manasā duccaritaṃ caritvā kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā apāyaṃ duggatiṃ vinipātaṃ nirayaṃ upapajjati. Evaṃ puggalo joti hoti tamaparāyano.

And how is a person one who comes from light and is destined for darkness? Here, a certain person is reborn in a high family—a family of wealthy khattiyas, wealthy brahmins, or wealthy householders—affluent, with great wealth and great possessions, with abundant gold and silver, abundant property and equipment, abundant wealth and grain. And they are handsome, good-looking, and inspiring, endowed with the supreme beauty of complexion. They are one who obtains food, drink, clothing, vehicles, garlands, scents, and unguents, a bed, a dwelling, and a lamp. They engage in misconduct by body, speech, and mind. Having engaged in misconduct by body, speech, and mind, on the breakup of the body, after death, they are reborn in the plane of misery, a bad destination, the lower world, in hell. In this way, a person is one who comes from light and is destined for darkness.

Kathañca puggalo joti hoti jotiparāyano? Idhekacco puggalo ucce kule paccājāto hoti – khattiyamahāsālakule vā brāhmaṇamahāsālakule vā gahapatimahāsālakule vā aḍḍhe mahaddhane mahābhoge pahūtajātarūparajate pahūtavittūpakaraṇe pahūtadhanadhaññe. So ca hoti abhirūpo dassanīyo pāsādiko paramāya vaṇṇapokkharatāya samannāgato, lābhī annassa pānassa vatthassa yānassa mālāgandhavilepanassa seyyāvasathapadīpeyyassa. So kāyena sucaritaṃ carati, vācāya sucaritaṃ carati, manasā sucaritaṃ carati. So kāyena sucaritaṃ caritvā vācāya sucaritaṃ caritvā manasā sucaritaṃ caritvā kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā sugatiṃ saggaṃ lokaṃ upapajjati. Evaṃ puggalo joti hoti jotiparāyano.

And how is a person one who comes from light and is destined for light? Here, a certain person is reborn in a high family—a family of wealthy khattiyas, wealthy brahmins, or wealthy householders—affluent, with great wealth and great possessions, with abundant gold and silver, abundant property and equipment, abundant wealth and grain. And they are handsome, good-looking, and inspiring, endowed with the supreme beauty of complexion. They are one who obtains food, drink, clothing, vehicles, garlands, scents, and unguents, a bed, a dwelling, and a lamp. They engage in good conduct by body, speech, and mind. Having engaged in good conduct by body, speech, and mind, on the breakup of the body, after death, they are reborn in a good destination, a heavenly world. In this way, a person is one who comes from light and is destined for light.

169. Kathañca puggalo oṇatoṇato hoti…pe… evaṃ puggalo oṇatoṇato [T 0.0203] hoti.

169. And how is a person one who comes from a low state and is inclined to a low state? … In this way, a person is one who comes from a low state and is inclined to a low state.

Kathañca puggalo oṇatuṇṇato hoti…pe… evaṃ puggalo oṇatuṇṇato hoti.

And how is a person one who, having come from a low state, is inclined to a high state? … Thus, a person is one who, having come from a low state, is inclined to a high state.

Kathañca puggalo uṇṇatoṇato hoti…pe… evaṃ puggalo uṇṇatoṇato hoti.

And how is a person one who, having come from a high state, is inclined to a low state? … Thus, a person is one who, having come from a high state, is inclined to a low state.

Kathañca puggalo uṇṇatuṇṇato hoti…pe… evaṃ puggalo uṇṇatuṇṇato hoti.

And how is a person one who, having come from a high state, is inclined to a high state? … Thus, a person is one who, having come from a high state, is inclined to a high state.

170. Tattha [M 0.0161][V 0.0163] katame cattāro rukkhūpamā puggalā? Cattāro rukkhā – pheggu sāraparivāro, sāro phegguparivāro, pheggu phegguparivāro, sāro sāraparivāro. Evamevaṃ cattārome rukkhūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ. Katame cattāro? Pheggu sāraparivāro, sāro phegguparivāro, pheggu phegguparivāro, sāro sāraparivāro.

170. Therein, what are the four persons comparable to trees? There are four kinds of trees: sapwood with a retinue of heartwood; heartwood with a retinue of sapwood; sapwood with a retinue of sapwood; and heartwood with a retinue of heartwood. In the same way, these four persons comparable to trees are found existing in the world. What four? The person who is like sapwood with a retinue of heartwood; the person who is like heartwood with a retinue of sapwood; the person who is like sapwood with a retinue of sapwood; and the person who is like heartwood with a retinue of heartwood.

Kathañca [P 0.0053] puggalo pheggu hoti sāraparivāro? Idhekacco puggalo dussīlo hoti pāpadhammo, parisā ca khvassa hoti sīlavatī kalyāṇadhammā. Evaṃ puggalo pheggu hoti sāraparivāro. Seyyathāpi so rukkho pheggu sāraparivāro, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

And how is a person like sapwood with a retinue of heartwood? Here, a certain person is immoral, of evil character, but their assembly is indeed virtuous, of good character. Thus, a person is like sapwood with a retinue of heartwood. This person is comparable to that tree which is sapwood with a retinue of heartwood.

Kathañca puggalo sāro hoti phegguparivāro? Idhekacco puggalo sīlavā hoti kalyāṇadhammo, parisā ca khvassa hoti dussīlā pāpadhammā. Evaṃ puggalo sāro hoti phegguparivāro. Seyyathāpi so rukkho sāro phegguparivāro, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

And how is a person like heartwood with a retinue of sapwood? Here, a certain person is virtuous, of good character, but their assembly is indeed immoral, of evil character. Thus, a person is like heartwood with a retinue of sapwood. This person is comparable to that tree which is heartwood with a retinue of sapwood.

Kathañca puggalo pheggu hoti phegguparivāro? Idhekacco puggalo dussīlo hoti pāpadhammo, parisāpissa hoti dussīlā pāpadhammā. Evaṃ puggalo pheggu hoti phegguparivāro. Seyyathāpi so rukkho pheggu [T 0.0204] phegguparivāro, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo.

And how is a person like sapwood with a retinue of sapwood? Here, a certain person is immoral, of evil character, and their assembly is also immoral, of evil character. Thus, a person is like sapwood with a retinue of sapwood. This person is comparable to that tree which is sapwood with a retinue of sapwood.

Kathañca puggalo sāro hoti sāraparivāro? Idhekacco puggalo sīlavā hoti kalyāṇadhammo, parisāpissa hoti sīlavatī kalyāṇadhammā. Evaṃ puggalo sāro hoti sāraparivāro. Seyyathāpi so rukkho sāro sāraparivāro, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo. Ime cattāro rukkhūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ.

And how is a person like heartwood with a retinue of heartwood? Here, a certain person is virtuous, of good character, and their assembly is also virtuous, of good character. Thus, a person is like heartwood with a retinue of heartwood. This person is comparable to that tree which is heartwood with a retinue of heartwood. These four persons comparable to trees are found existing in the world.

171. Katamo ca puggalo rūpappamāṇo rūpappasanno? Idhekacco puggalo ārohaṃ vā passitvā pariṇāhaṃ vā passitvā saṇṭhānaṃ vā passitvā pāripūriṃ vā passitvā tattha pamāṇaṃ gahetvā pasādaṃ janeti. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo rūpappamāṇo rūpappasanno.

171. And which person is one who measures by form and is pleased by form? Here, a certain person, having seen another's height, or girth, or build, or completeness of features, takes that as a measure and generates confidence. This is called a person who measures by form and is pleased by form.

Katamo [M 0.0162] ca puggalo ghosappamāṇo ghosappasanno? Idhekacco puggalo paravaṇṇanāya parathomanāya [V 0.0164] parapasaṃsanāya paravaṇṇahārikāya paravaṇṇahāriyā (sī.) tattha pamāṇaṃ gahetvā pasādaṃ janeti. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo ghosappamāṇo ghosappasanno.

And which person is one who measures by report and is pleased by report? Here, a certain person, by means of others' praise, extolling, commendation, or reports of good qualities, takes that as a measure and generates confidence. This is called a person who measures by report and is pleased by report.

172. Katamo ca puggalo lūkhappamāṇo lūkhappasanno? Idhekacco puggalo cīvaralūkhaṃ vā passitvā pattalūkhaṃ vā passitvā senāsanalūkhaṃ vā passitvā vividhaṃ vā dukkarakārikaṃ passitvā tattha pamāṇaṃ gahetvā pasādaṃ janeti. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo lūkhappamāṇo lūkhappasanno.

172. And which person is one who measures by austerity and is pleased by austerity? Here, a certain person, having seen coarse robes, or a coarse bowl, or coarse lodgings, or the performance of various difficult deeds, takes that as a measure and generates confidence. This is called a person who measures by austerity and is pleased by austerity.

Katamo ca puggalo dhammappamāṇo dhammappasanno? Idhekacco puggalo sīlaṃ vā passitvā samādhiṃ vā passitvā paññaṃ [P 0.0054] vā passitvā tattha pamāṇaṃ gahetvā pasādaṃ janeti. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo [T 0.0205] dhammappamāṇo dhammappasanno.

And which person is one who measures by the Dhamma and is pleased by the Dhamma? Here, a certain person, having seen another's virtue, or concentration, or wisdom, takes that as a measure and generates confidence. This is called a person who measures by the Dhamma and is pleased by the Dhamma.

173. Kathañca puggalo attahitāya paṭipanno hoti no parahitāya? Idhekacco puggalo attanā sīlasampanno hoti, no paraṃ sīlasampadāya samādapeti; attanā samādhisampanno hoti, no paraṃ samādhisampadāya samādapeti; attanā paññāsampanno hoti, no paraṃ paññāsampadāya samādapeti; attanā vimuttisampanno hoti, no paraṃ vimuttisampadāya samādapeti; attanā vimuttiñāṇadassanasampanno hoti, no paraṃ vimuttiñāṇadassanasampadāya samādapeti. Evaṃ puggalo attahitāya paṭipanno hoti no parahitāya.

173. And how is a person one who is practicing for their own welfare but not for the welfare of others? Here, a certain person is themselves accomplished in virtue but does not encourage others in the accomplishment of virtue; is themselves accomplished in concentration but does not encourage others in the accomplishment of concentration; is themselves accomplished in wisdom but does not encourage others in the accomplishment of wisdom; is themselves accomplished in liberation but does not encourage others in the accomplishment of liberation; is themselves accomplished in the knowledge and vision of liberation but does not encourage others in the accomplishment of the knowledge and vision of liberation. In this way, a person is one who is practicing for their own welfare but not for the welfare of others.

Kathañca puggalo parahitāya paṭipanno hoti no attahitāya? Idhekacco puggalo attanā na sīlasampanno hoti, paraṃ sīlasampadāya samādapeti; attanā na samādhisampanno hoti, paraṃ samādhisampadāya samādapeti; attanā na paññāsampanno hoti, paraṃ paññāsampadāya samādapeti; attanā na vimuttisampanno hoti, paraṃ vimuttisampadāya samādapeti; attanā na vimuttiñāṇadassanasampanno hoti, paraṃ vimuttiñāṇadassanasampadāya samādapeti. Evaṃ puggalo parahitāya paṭipanno hoti no attahitāya.

And how is a person one who is practicing for the welfare of others but not for their own welfare? Here, a certain person is not themselves accomplished in virtue but encourages others in the accomplishment of virtue; is not themselves accomplished in concentration but encourages others in the accomplishment of concentration; is not themselves accomplished in wisdom but encourages others in the accomplishment of wisdom; is not themselves accomplished in liberation but encourages others in the accomplishment of liberation; is not themselves accomplished in the knowledge and vision of liberation but encourages others in the accomplishment of the knowledge and vision of liberation. In this way, a person is one who is practicing for the welfare of others but not for their own welfare.

Kathañca [M 0.0163] puggalo attahitāya ceva paṭipanno hoti parahitāya ca? Idhekacco puggalo attanā ca sīlasampanno hoti, parañca sīlasampadāya samādapeti; attanā ca samādhisampanno hoti, parañca samādhisampadāya samādapeti; attanā ca paññāsampanno hoti, parañca paññāsampadāya [T 0.0206][V 0.0165] samādapeti; attanā ca vimuttisampanno hoti, parañca vimuttisampadāya samādapeti; attanā ca vimuttiñāṇadassanasampanno hoti, parañca vimuttiñāṇadassanasampadāya samādapeti. Evaṃ puggalo attahitāya ceva paṭipanno hoti parahitāya ca.

And how is a person one who is practicing for their own welfare and for the welfare of others? Here, a certain person is themselves accomplished in virtue and also encourages others in the accomplishment of virtue; is themselves accomplished in concentration and also encourages others in the accomplishment of concentration; is themselves accomplished in wisdom and also encourages others in the accomplishment of wisdom; is themselves accomplished in liberation and also encourages others in the accomplishment of liberation; is themselves accomplished in the knowledge and vision of liberation and also encourages others in the accomplishment of the knowledge and vision of liberation. In this way, a person is one who is practicing for their own welfare and for the welfare of others.

Kathañca puggalo neva attahitāya paṭipanno hoti no parahitāya? Idhekacco puggalo attanā na sīlasampanno hoti, no paraṃ sīlasampadāya samādapeti; attanā na samādhisampanno hoti, no paraṃ samādhisampadāya samādapeti; attanā na paññāsampanno hoti, no paraṃ paññāsampadāya samādapeti; attanā na [P 0.0055] vimuttisampanno hoti, no paraṃ vimuttisampadāya samādapeti; attanā na vimuttiñāṇadassanasampanno hoti, no paraṃ vimuttiñāṇadassanasampadāya samādapeti. Evaṃ puggalo neva attahitāya paṭipanno hoti no parahitāya.

And how is a person one who is practicing neither for their own welfare nor for the welfare of others? Here, a certain person is not themselves accomplished in virtue and does not encourage others in the accomplishment of virtue; is not themselves accomplished in concentration and does not encourage others in the accomplishment of concentration; is not themselves accomplished in wisdom and does not encourage others in the accomplishment of wisdom; is not themselves accomplished in liberation and does not encourage others in the accomplishment of liberation; is not themselves accomplished in the knowledge and vision of liberation and does not encourage others in the accomplishment of the knowledge and vision of liberation. In this way, a person is one who is practicing neither for their own welfare nor for the welfare of others.

174. Kathañca puggalo attantapo hoti attaparitāpanānuyogamanuyutto? Idhekacco puggalo acelako hoti muttācāro hatthāpalekhano hatthāvalekhano (syā.), naehibhaddantiko natiṭṭhabhaddantiko nābhihaṭaṃ na uddissakataṃ na nimantanaṃ sādiyati, so na kumbhimukhā paṭiggaṇhāti na kaḷopimukhā kalopimukhā (sī. syā.) ma. ni. 2.7 paṭiggaṇhāti, na eḷakamantaraṃ na daṇḍamantaraṃ na musalamantaraṃ na dvinnaṃ bhuñjamānānaṃ na gabbhiniyā na pāyamānāya na purisantaragatāya, na saṅkittīsu na yattha sā upaṭṭhito hoti na yattha makkhikā saṇḍasaṇḍacārinī, na macchaṃ na [T 0.0207] maṃsaṃ na suraṃ na merayaṃ na thusodakaṃ pivati. So ekāgāriko vā hoti ekālopiko, dvāgāriko vā hoti dvālopiko…pe… sattāgāriko vā hoti sattālopiko; ekissāpi dattiyā yāpeti, dvīhipi dattīhi yāpeti…pe… sattahipi dattīhi yāpeti; ekāhikampi āhāraṃ āhāreti, dvīhikampi dvāhikampi (sī.) āhāraṃ āhāreti…pe… sattāhikampi āhāraṃ āhāreti. Iti evarūpaṃ [M 0.0164] aḍḍhamāsikampi pariyāyabhattabhojanānuyogamanuyutto viharati. So sākabhakkho vā hoti sāmākabhakkho vā hoti nīvārabhakkho vā hoti daddulabhakkho vā hoti haṭabhakkho vā hoti kaṇabhakkho vā hoti ācāmabhakkho vā hoti piññākabhakkho vā hoti tiṇabhakkho vā hoti gomayabhakkho vā hoti, vanamūlaphalāhāro yāpeti pavattaphalabhojī. So sāṇānipi dhāreti masāṇānipi dhāreti chavadussānipi dhāreti paṃsukūlānipi dhāreti tirīṭānipi dhāreti ajinampi dhāreti ajinakkhipampi dhāreti kusacīrampi dhāreti vākacīrampi dhāreti phalakacīrampi dhāreti kesakambalampi dhāreti vāḷakambalampi dhāreti ulūkapakkhampi [V 0.0166] ulukapakkhampi (sī. syā.) dhāreti, kesamassulocakopi hoti kesamassulocanānuyogamanuyutto, ubbhaṭṭhakopi hoti āsanapaṭikkhitto, ukkuṭikopi hoti ukkuṭikappadhānamanuyutto, kaṇṭakāpassayikopi hoti kaṇṭakāpassaye seyyaṃ kappeti, sāyatatiyakampi sāyaṃtatiyakampi (syā. ka.) ma. ni. 2.7 udakorohanānuyogamanuyutto viharati. Iti evarūpaṃ anekavihitaṃ kāyassa ātāpanaparitāpanānuyogamanuyutto viharati[P 0.0056]. Evaṃ puggalo attantapo hoti attaparitāpanānuyogamanuyutto.

174. And how is a person a self-tormentor, devoted to the practice of self-torment? Here, a certain person goes naked, is of unrestrained conduct, and licks their hands clean; they do not come when told, 'Venerable sir, please come,' nor stop when told, 'Venerable sir, please stop;' they do not accept food brought to them, food prepared for them, or an invitation to a meal. They do not accept food from the mouth of a pot or a pan. They do not accept it across a threshold, a stick, or a pestle; nor from two people eating together; nor from a pregnant woman, a nursing woman, or a woman consorting with a man; nor from where food is distributed; nor where a dog is waiting or flies are swarming. They do not eat fish or meat; they do not drink liquor, spirits, or fermented brew. They are a one-houser, a one-morsel person; a two-houser, a two-morsel person… a seven-houser, a seven-morsel person. They subsist on one small bowlful a day, on two… on seven small bowlfuls a day. They take food once a day, once every two days… once every seven days. Thus they dwell committed to the practice of eating by turns, up to half a month. They are one who feeds on vegetables, millet, wild rice, daddula-scum, moss, broken rice, rice scum, sesame cake, grass, or cow dung. They subsist on forest roots and fruits; they feed on fallen fruit. They wear garments of hemp, of coarse cloth, of charnel-ground cloths, of refuse rags, of tree bark; they wear antelope hide, antelope hide with the hoofs intact, a garment of kusa grass, a garment of bark-strips, a garment of wood-shavings; they wear a blanket of human hair, a blanket of horsehair, a garment of owl’s feathers. They are a plucker-out of hair and beard, devoted to the practice of plucking out hair and beard. They are one who stands upright, rejecting seats. They are a squatter, devoted to the exertion of squatting. They are a thorn-bed ascetic; they make their bed on a mattress of thorns. They dwell committed to the practice of bathing in water three times a day, including the evening. Thus they dwell committed to the practice of heating and tormenting the body in many ways. In this way, a person is a self-tormentor, devoted to the practice of self-torment.

175. Kathañca puggalo parantapo hoti paraparitāpanānuyogamanuyutto? Idhekacco puggalo orabbhiko hoti sūkariko sākuṇiko māgaviko luddo macchaghātako coro coraghātako goghātako bandhanāgāriko, ye vā panaññepi keci kurūrakammantā. Evaṃ puggalo parantapo hoti paraparitāpanānuyogamanuyutto.

175. And how is a person a tormentor of others, devoted to the practice of tormenting others? Here, a certain person is a butcher of sheep, a pig butcher, a fowler, a deer-hunter, a trapper, a fisherman, a thief, an executioner of thieves, a cattle slaughterer, a jailer, or one who follows any other such cruel occupation. In this way, a person is a tormentor of others, devoted to the practice of tormenting others.

176. Kathañca puggalo attantapo ca hoti attaparitāpanānuyogamanuyutto, parantapo ca paraparitāpanānuyogamanuyutto? Idhekacco puggalo rājā vā hoti khattiyo muddhāvasitto muddhābhisitto (syā. ka.) brāhmaṇo vā mahāsālo. So puratthimena narassa navaṃ sandhāgāraṃ santāgāraṃ (syā.), yaññāgāraṃ (sī.) kārāpetvā kesamassuṃ ohāretvā kharājinaṃ khurājinaṃ (syā. ka.) nivāsetvā sappitelena kāyaṃ abbhañjitvā [M 0.0165] migavisāṇena piṭṭhiṃ kaṇḍuvamāno kaṇḍūyamāno (sī.) sandhāgāraṃ pavisati saddhiṃ mahesiyā brāhmaṇena ca purohitena. So tattha anantarahitāya bhūmiyā haritupalittāya seyyaṃ kappeti. Ekissā gāviyā sarūpavacchāya yaṃ ekasmiṃ thane khīraṃ hoti tena rājā yāpeti, yaṃ dutiyasmiṃ thane khīraṃ hoti tena mahesī yāpeti, yaṃ tatiyasmiṃ thane khīraṃ hoti tena brāhmaṇo purohito yāpeti, yaṃ catutthasmiṃ thane khīraṃ hoti tena aggiṃ juhati, avasesena vacchako yāpeti. So evamāha – “ettakā usabhā haññantu yaññatthāya, ettakā vacchatarā haññantu yaññatthāya, ettakā vacchatariyo haññantu yaññatthāya, ettakā [T 0.0209] ajā haññantu yaññatthāya, ettakā urabbhā haññantu yaññatthāya, (ettakā assā haññantu yaññatthāya) ( ) natthi sīhaḷapotthake. majjhimanikāye kandarakasuttepi evameva ettakā rukkhā chijjantu yūpatthāya, ettakā dabbhā lūyantu barihisatthāyā”ti parihiṃsatthāyāti (sī. syā. ka.) ma. ni. 2.9. Yepissa te honti dāsāti vā pessāti vā kammakarāti vā, tepi daṇḍatajjitā bhayatajjitā assumukhā rudamānā parikammāni karonti. Evaṃ puggalo attantapo ca hoti attaparitāpanānuyogamanuyutto, parantapo ca paraparitāpanānuyogamanuyutto.

176. And how is a person one who torments themself and is devoted to the practice of self-torment, and who torments others and is devoted to the practice of tormenting others? Here, a certain person is a king, an anointed khattiya, or a wealthy brahmin. To the east of the city, they have a new sacrificial hall built. Having shaved off their hair and beard, dressed in a rough deerskin, smeared their body with ghee and oil, and scratching their back with a deer's horn, they enter the sacrificial hall together with their chief queen and the brahmin chaplain. There they lie down on the bare ground, which has been smeared with fresh cow-dung. The king subsists on the milk from one teat of a single cow with a calf of similar appearance; the chief queen subsists on the milk from the second teat; the brahmin chaplain subsists on the milk from the third teat; with the milk from the fourth teat they make an offering to the fire; and the calf subsists on what is left. They say: ‘Let so many bulls be slaughtered for the sake of the sacrifice, so many steers be slaughtered for the sake of the sacrifice, so many heifers be slaughtered for the sake of the sacrifice, so many goats be slaughtered for the sake of the sacrifice, so many rams be slaughtered for the sake of the sacrifice, so many horses be slaughtered for the sake of the sacrifice, so many trees be cut down for the sacrificial posts, and so much dabba grass be mown for the sacred ground.’ And those who are their slaves, messengers, or workers perform the preparatory rites, threatened by the rod and by fear, with tear-stained faces, weeping. In this way a person is one who torments themself and is devoted to the practice of self-torment, and who torments others and is devoted to the practice of tormenting others.

177. Kathañca puggalo neva attantapo ca hoti na attaparitāpanānuyogamanuyutto, na parantapo [V 0.0167] na paraparitāpanānuyogamanuyutto? So anattantapo aparantapo diṭṭheva dhamme nicchāto nibbuto sītībhūto sukhappaṭisaṃvedī brahmabhūtena attanā viharati.
