177. And how is a person one who neither torments himself nor is devoted to the practice of self-torment, neither torments others nor is devoted to the practice of tormenting others? Such a person, not tormenting himself and not tormenting others, in this very life is without craving, extinguished, cooled, experiencing happiness, and dwells with a self that has become sublime.

Idha tathāgato loke uppajjati arahaṃ sammāsambuddho vijjācaraṇasampanno [P 0.0057] sugato lokavidū anuttaro purisadammasārathi satthā devamanussānaṃ buddho bhagavā. So imaṃ lokaṃ sadevakaṃ samārakaṃ sabrahmakaṃ sassamaṇabrāhmaṇiṃ pajaṃ sadevamanussaṃ sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā pavedeti. So dhammaṃ deseti ādikalyāṇaṃ majjhekalyāṇaṃ pariyosānakalyāṇaṃ sātthaṃ sabyañjanaṃ, kevalaparipuṇṇaṃ parisuddhaṃ brahmacariyaṃ pakāseti. Taṃ dhammaṃ suṇāti gahapati vā gahapatiputto vā aññatarasmiṃ vā kule paccājāto[M 0.0166]. So taṃ dhammaṃ sutvā tathāgate saddhaṃ paṭilabhati. So tena saddhāpaṭilābhena [T 0.0210] samannāgato iti paṭisañcikkhati – “sambādho gharāvāso rajāpatho, abbhokāso pabbajjā. Nayidaṃ sukaraṃ agāraṃ ajjhāvasatā ekantaparipuṇṇaṃ ekantaparisuddhaṃ saṅkhalikhitaṃ brahmacariyaṃ carituṃ. Yaṃnūnāhaṃ kesamassuṃ ohāretvā kāsāyāni vatthāni acchādetvā agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajeyya”nti! So aparena samayena appaṃ vā bhogakkhandhaṃ pahāya mahantaṃ vā bhogakkhandhaṃ pahāya appaṃ vā ñātiparivaṭṭaṃ pahāya mahantaṃ vā ñātiparivaṭṭaṃ pahāya kesamassuṃ ohāretvā kāsāyāni vatthāni acchādetvā agārasmā anagāriyaṃ pabbajati.

Here, a Tathāgata arises in the world, an Arahant, a Perfectly Self-Enlightened One, accomplished in knowledge and conduct, a Fortunate One, a Knower of the world, an unsurpassed trainer of persons to be tamed, a Teacher of gods and humans, the Buddha, the Blessed One. He makes known this world with its devas, its Māra, and its Brahmā, this generation with its ascetics and brahmins, with its devas and humans, having realized it himself with direct knowledge. He teaches the Dhamma that is good in the beginning, good in the middle, and good in the end, with its meaning and phrasing; he reveals the holy life, perfectly complete and pure. A householder or a householder’s son, or one born in some other family, hears that Dhamma. Having heard the Dhamma, he gains faith in the Tathāgata. Possessed of that faith, he reflects: ‘The household life is confining, a path of dust; going forth is the open air. It is not easy for one living at home to lead the holy life, perfectly complete and pure, polished like a conch shell. Suppose I shave off my hair and beard, put on the saffron robes, and go forth from the household life into homelessness?’ Then, after some time, having abandoned a small or large fortune, having abandoned a small or large circle of relatives, he shaves off his hair and beard, puts on the saffron robes, and goes forth from the household life into homelessness.

178. So evaṃ pabbajito samāno bhikkhūnaṃ sikkhāsājīvasamāpanno pāṇātipātaṃ pahāya pāṇātipātā paṭivirato hoti nihitadaṇḍo nihitasattho lajjī dayāpanno sabbapāṇabhūtahitānukampī viharati.

178. Having thus gone forth, and possessing the training and livelihood of the bhikkhus, he abandons the destruction of life and abstains from the destruction of life. With rod and weapon laid aside, conscientious and merciful, he dwells compassionate for the welfare of all living beings.

Adinnādānaṃ pahāya adinnādānā paṭivirato hoti dinnādāyī dinnapāṭikaṅkhī, athenena sucibhūtena attanā viharati.

Abandoning the taking of what is not given, he abstains from taking what is not given. Taking only what is given, expecting only what is given, he dwells with a self that has become pure by being free from theft.

Abrahmacariyaṃ pahāya brahmacārī hoti ārācārī anācārī (ka.) paṭivirato methunā gāmadhammā.

Abandoning unchaste conduct, he is celibate, living aloof, and abstains from sexual intercourse, the practice of villagers.

Musāvādaṃ pahāya musāvādā paṭivirato hoti saccavādī saccasandho theto paccayiko avisaṃvādako lokassa.

Abandoning false speech, he abstains from false speech. He is a speaker of truth, joining truth to truth, steadfast, trustworthy, and not a deceiver of the world.

Pisuṇaṃ [V 0.0168] vācaṃ pahāya pisuṇāya vācāya paṭivirato hoti, ito sutvā na amutra akkhātā imesaṃ bhedāya, amutra vā sutvā na imesaṃ akkhātā amūsaṃ bhedāya. Iti bhinnānaṃ vā sandhātā sahitānaṃ vā anuppadātā samaggārāmo samaggarato samagganandī [T 0.0211] samaggakaraṇiṃ vācaṃ bhāsitā hoti.

Abandoning divisive speech, he abstains from divisive speech. He does not repeat there what he has heard here in order to cause division among them, nor does he repeat here what he has heard there in order to cause division among them. Thus he is a reconciler of those who are divided and an encourager of those who are united. He delights in concord, rejoices in concord, is fond of concord, and speaks words that promote concord.

Pharusaṃ vācaṃ pahāya pharusāya vācāya paṭivirato hoti. Yā sā vācā nelā kaṇṇasukhā pemanīyā hadayaṅgamā porī bahujanakantā bahujanamanāpā tathārūpiṃ vācaṃ bhāsitā hoti.

Abandoning harsh speech, he abstains from harsh speech. He speaks such words as are faultless, pleasing to the ear, lovable, going to the heart, courteous, agreeable to many, and pleasing to many.

Samphappalāpaṃ [P 0.0058][M 0.0167] pahāya samphappalāpā paṭivirato hoti, kālavādī bhūtavādī atthavādī dhammavādī vinayavādī nidhānavatiṃ vācaṃ bhāsitā kālena sāpadesaṃ pariyantavatiṃ atthasaṃhitaṃ.

Abandoning idle chatter, he abstains from idle chatter. He speaks at the proper time, speaks what is true, speaks what is beneficial, and speaks on the Dhamma and the Vinaya. He speaks words worth treasuring, which are timely, accompanied by reasons, circumscribed, and connected with the goal.

179. So bījagāmabhūtagāmasamārambhā paṭivirato hoti, ekabhattiko hoti rattūparato virato vikālabhojanā, naccagītavāditavisūkadassanā paṭivirato hoti, mālāgandhavilepanadhāraṇamaṇḍanavibhūsanaṭṭhānā paṭivirato hoti, uccāsayanamahāsayanā paṭivirato hoti, jātarūparajatapaṭiggahaṇā paṭivirato hoti.

179. He abstains from damaging seed and plant life. He eats only one meal a day, abstaining from eating at night and at the wrong time. He abstains from dancing, singing, instrumental music, and seeing shows. He abstains from wearing garlands, using scents, and applying unguents for the purpose of beautification and adornment. He abstains from high and luxurious beds. He abstains from accepting gold and silver.

Āmakadhaññapaṭiggahaṇā paṭivirato hoti, āmakamaṃsapaṭiggahaṇā paṭivirato hoti, itthikumārikāpaṭiggahaṇā paṭivirato hoti, dāsidāsapaṭiggahaṇā paṭivirato hoti, ajeḷakapaṭiggahaṇā paṭivirato hoti, kukkuṭasūkarapaṭiggahaṇā paṭivirato hoti, hatthigavāssavaḷavapaṭiggahaṇā paṭivirato hoti, khettavatthupaṭiggahaṇā paṭivirato hoti, dūteyyapahiṇagamanānuyogā paṭivirato hoti, kayavikkayā paṭivirato hoti, tulākūṭakaṃsakūṭamānakūṭā paṭivirato hoti, ukkoṭanavañcananikatisāciyogā … sāviyogā (syā. ka.) paṭivirato hoti, chedanavadhabandhanaviparāmosaālopasahasākārā paṭivirato hoti.

He abstains from accepting raw grain, raw meat, women and girls, male and female slaves, goats and sheep, fowl and pigs, elephants, cattle, horses, and mares, and fields and lands. He abstains from undertaking errands and messages. He abstains from cheating with scales, coins, and measures. He abstains from the crooked ways of bribery, deception, and fraud. He abstains from cutting, killing, binding, highway robbery, plundering, and violence.

180. So santuṭṭho hoti kāyaparihārikena cīvarena kucchiparihārikena piṇḍapātena. So yena yeneva [T 0.0212] pakkamati samādāyeva pakkamati, seyyathāpi nāma pakkhī sakuṇo yena yeneva ḍeti sapattabhārova ḍeti. Evamevaṃ bhikkhu santuṭṭho hoti kāyaparihārikena cīvarena kucchiparihārikena [V 0.0169] piṇḍapātena. So yena yeneva pakkamati samādāyeva pakkamati. So iminā ariyena sīlakkhandhena samannāgato ajjhattaṃ anavajjasukhaṃ paṭisaṃvedeti.

180. He is content with robes to protect his body and with almsfood to sustain his belly. Wherever he goes, he sets out taking only these with him. Just as a winged bird, wherever it flies, flies with its wings as its only burden, so too, the bhikkhu is content with robes to protect his body and with almsfood to sustain his belly. Wherever he goes, he sets out taking only these with him. Possessed of this noble aggregate of virtue, he experiences within himself a blameless happiness.

181. So cakkhunā rūpaṃ disvā na nimittaggāhī hoti nānubyañjanaggāhī. Yatvādhikaraṇamenaṃ cakkhundriyaṃ asaṃvutaṃ viharantaṃ abhijjhādomanassā pāpakā akusalā dhammā anvāssaveyyuṃ, tassa saṃvarāya paṭipajjati, rakkhati cakkhundriyaṃ, cakkhundriye saṃvaraṃ āpajjati; sotena saddaṃ sutvā…pe… ghānena [M 0.0168] gandhaṃ ghāyitvā…pe… jivhāya rasaṃ sāyitvā…pe… kāyena phoṭṭhabbaṃ phusitvā…pe… manasā dhammaṃ viññāya na nimittaggāhī hoti nānubyañjanaggāhī. Yatvādhikaraṇamenaṃ manindriyaṃ asaṃvutaṃ viharantaṃ abhijjhādomanassā pāpakā akusalā dhammā anvāssaveyyuṃ, tassa saṃvarāya paṭipajjati, rakkhati manindriyaṃ, manindriye [P 0.0059] saṃvaraṃ āpajjati. So iminā ariyena indriyasaṃvarena samannāgato ajjhattaṃ abyāsekasukhaṃ paṭisaṃvedeti.

181. On seeing a form with the eye, he does not grasp at its signs or its features. Inasmuch as, if he were to dwell with the eye faculty unrestrained, evil, unwholesome states of covetousness and displeasure might assail him, he practices for its restraint, he guards the eye faculty, he attains restraint over the eye faculty. On hearing a sound with the ear… on smelling an odor with the nose… on tasting a flavor with the tongue… on touching a tangible with the body… on cognizing a mental phenomenon with the mind, he does not grasp at its signs or its features. Inasmuch as, if he were to dwell with the mind faculty unrestrained, evil, unwholesome states of covetousness and displeasure might assail him, he practices for its restraint, he guards the mind faculty, he attains restraint over the mind faculty. Possessed of this noble restraint of the faculties, he experiences within himself an unblemished happiness.

182. So abhikkante paṭikkante sampajānakārī hoti, ālokite vilokite sampajānakārī hoti, samiñjite pasārite sampajānakārī hoti, saṅghāṭipattacīvaradhāraṇe sampajānakārī hoti, asite pīte khāyite sāyite sampajānakārī hoti, uccārapassāvakamme sampajānakārī hoti, gate ṭhite nisinne sutte [T 0.0213] jāgarite bhāsite tuṇhībhāve sampajānakārī hoti.

182. He is one who acts with clear comprehension when going forward and returning; when looking ahead and looking aside; when bending and extending his limbs; in bearing his outer robe, bowl, and other robes; when eating, drinking, chewing, and savoring; when defecating and urinating; when walking, standing, sitting, falling asleep, waking up, speaking, and keeping silent.

So iminā ca ariyena sīlakkhandhena samannāgato iminā ca ariyena indriyasaṃvarena samannāgato iminā ca ariyena satisampajaññena samannāgato imāya ca ariyāya santuṭṭhiyā samannāgato passa ma. ni. 2 kandarakasutte; a. ni. 1 attantapasutte ca vivittaṃ senāsanaṃ bhajati araññaṃ rukkhamūlaṃ pabbataṃ kandaraṃ giriguhaṃ susānaṃ vanapatthaṃ abbhokāsaṃ palālapuñjaṃ. So pacchābhattaṃ piṇḍapātapaṭikkanto nisīdati pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā ujuṃ kāyaṃ paṇidhāya parimukhaṃ satiṃ upaṭṭhapetvā. So abhijjhaṃ loke pahāya vigatābhijjhena cetasā viharati, abhijjhāya cittaṃ parisodheti; byāpādapadosaṃ pahāya abyāpannacitto viharati sabbapāṇabhūtahitānukampī, byāpādapadosā cittaṃ parisodheti; thinamiddhaṃ thīnamiddhaṃ (sī. syā.) pahāya vigatathinamiddho viharati ālokasaññī sato sampajāno, thinamiddhā cittaṃ parisodheti; uddhaccakukkuccaṃ pahāya anuddhato viharati ajjhattaṃ vūpasantacitto, uddhaccakukkuccā [V 0.0170] cittaṃ parisodheti; vicikicchaṃ pahāya tiṇṇavicikiccho viharati akathaṃkathī kusalesu dhammesu, vicikicchāya cittaṃ parisodheti.

Endowed with this noble aggregate of virtue, this noble restraint of the faculties, this noble mindfulness and clear comprehension, and this noble contentment, he resorts to a secluded dwelling: a forest, the root of a tree, a mountain, a ravine, a hillside cave, a charnel ground, a jungle thicket, an open space, or a heap of straw. After his meal, having returned from his alms round, he sits down, folds his legs crosswise, sets his body erect, and establishes mindfulness before him. Having abandoned covetousness for the world, he dwells with a mind free from covetousness; he purifies his mind of covetousness. Having abandoned ill will and malevolence, he dwells with a mind free from ill will, compassionate for the welfare of all living beings; he purifies his mind of ill will and malevolence. Having abandoned sloth and torpor, he dwells free from sloth and torpor, percipient of light, mindful, and clearly comprehending; he purifies his mind of sloth and torpor. Having abandoned restlessness and remorse, he dwells unagitated, with a mind inwardly peaceful; he purifies his mind of restlessness and remorse. Having abandoned doubt, he dwells having crossed over doubt, without perplexity regarding wholesome states; he purifies his mind of doubt.

183. So ime pañca nīvaraṇe pahāya cetaso upakkilese paññāya dubbalīkaraṇe vivicceva kāmehi vivicca akusalehi dhammehi savitakkaṃ [M 0.0169] savicāraṃ vivekajaṃ pītisukhaṃ paṭhamaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati; vitakkavicārānaṃ vūpasamā ajjhattaṃ sampasādanaṃ cetaso ekodibhāvaṃ avitakkaṃ avicāraṃ samādhijaṃ pītisukhaṃ dutiyaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati; pītiyā ca [T 0.0214] virāgā upekkhako ca viharati sato ca sampajāno sukhañca kāyena paṭisaṃvedeti, yaṃ taṃ ariyā ācikkhanti – “upekkhako satimā sukhavihārī”ti tatiyaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati; sukhassa ca pahānā dukkhassa ca pahānā pubbeva somanassadomanassānaṃ atthaṅgamā adukkhamasukhaṃ upekkhāsatipārisuddhiṃ catutthaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati.

183. Having abandoned these five hindrances, defilements of the mind that weaken wisdom, quite secluded from sensual pleasures, secluded from unwholesome states, he enters and abides in the first jhāna, which is accompanied by directed thought and evaluation, with rapture and happiness born of seclusion. With the stilling of directed thought and evaluation, he enters and abides in the second jhāna, which has internal tranquility and unification of mind, is without directed thought and evaluation, and has rapture and happiness born of concentration. With the fading away of rapture, he dwells equanimous, and mindful and clearly comprehending, he experiences happiness with the body; he enters and abides in the third jhāna, of which the noble ones declare: ‘Equanimous and mindful, one abides happily.’ With the abandoning of pleasure and pain, and with the previous disappearance of joy and grief, he enters and abides in the fourth jhāna, which is neither-painful-nor-pleasant and includes the purification of mindfulness by equanimity.

So [P 0.0060] evaṃ samāhite citte parisuddhe pariyodāte anaṅgaṇe vigatūpakkilese mudubhūte kammaniye ṭhite āneñjappatte pubbenivāsānussatiñāṇāya cittaṃ abhininnāmeti. So anekavihitaṃ pubbenivāsaṃ anussarati, seyyathidaṃ – ekampi jātiṃ dvepi jātiyo tissopi jātiyo catassopi jātiyo pañcapi jātiyo dasapi jātiyo vīsampi jātiyo tiṃsampi jātiyo cattālīsampi jātiyo paññāsampi jātiyo jātisatampi jātisahassampi jātisatasahassampi anekepi saṃvaṭṭakappe anekepi vivaṭṭakappe anekepi saṃvaṭṭavivaṭṭakappe – “amutrāsiṃ evaṃnāmo evaṃgotto evaṃvaṇṇo evamāhāro evaṃsukhadukkhapaṭisaṃvedī evamāyupariyanto, so tato cuto amutra udapādiṃ; tatrāpāsiṃ evaṃnāmo evaṃgotto evaṃvaṇṇo evamāhāro evaṃsukhadukkhapaṭisaṃvedī evamāyupariyanto, so tato cuto idhūpapanno”ti. Iti sākāraṃ sauddesaṃ anekavihitaṃ pubbenivāsaṃ anussarati.

With his mind thus concentrated, purified, bright, unblemished, free from defilements, malleable, workable, steady, and having attained the imperturbable, he inclines his mind to the knowledge of recollecting past lives. He recollects his manifold past lives, that is to say: one birth, two births, three births, four births, five births, ten births, twenty births, thirty births, forty births, fifty births, a hundred births, a thousand births, a hundred thousand births, many eons of cosmic contraction, many eons of cosmic expansion, many eons of cosmic contraction and expansion: ‘There I was with such a name, of such a clan, with such a complexion, eating such food, experiencing such pleasure and pain, with such a life span. Passing away from there, I arose elsewhere. There, too, I was with such a name, of such a clan, with such a complexion, eating such food, experiencing such pleasure and pain, with such a life span. Passing away from there, I have arisen here.’ Thus, with their aspects and details, he recollects his manifold past lives.

184. So evaṃ samāhite citte parisuddhe pariyodāte anaṅgaṇe vigatūpakkilese mudubhūte kammaniye ṭhite āneñjappatte sattānaṃ cutūpapātañāṇāya cittaṃ abhininnāmeti. So dibbena [T 0.0215] cakkhunā visuddhena atikkantamānusakena satte passati cavamāne upapajjamāne hīne paṇīte suvaṇṇe dubbaṇṇe, sugate duggate yathākammūpage satte pajānāti – “ime vata bhonto sattā [V 0.0171] kāyaduccaritena samannāgatā, vacīduccaritena samannāgatā, manoduccaritena samannāgatā, ariyānaṃ upavādakā, micchādiṭṭhikā micchādiṭṭhikammasamādānā. Te kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā apāyaṃ duggatiṃ vinipātaṃ nirayaṃ upapannā. Ime vā pana bhonto sattā kāyasucaritena [M 0.0170] samannāgatā vacīsucaritena samannāgatā manosucaritena samannāgatā ariyānaṃ anupavādakā sammādiṭṭhikā sammādiṭṭhikammasamādānā. Te kāyassa bhedā paraṃ maraṇā sugatiṃ saggaṃ lokaṃ upapannā”ti. So iti dibbena cakkhunā visuddhena atikkantamānusakena satte passati cavamāne upapajjamāne hīne paṇīte suvaṇṇe dubbaṇṇe, sugate duggate yathākammūpage satte pajānāti.

184. With his mind thus concentrated, purified, bright, unblemished, free from defilements, malleable, workable, steady, and having attained the imperturbable, he inclines his mind to the knowledge of the passing away and arising of beings. With the divine eye, which is purified and surpasses the human, he sees beings passing away and arising—inferior and superior, of good complexion and bad complexion, in good destinations and in bad destinations. He knows beings who fare according to their kamma: ‘Indeed, venerable sirs, these beings were endowed with misconduct of body, speech, and mind, were revilers of the noble ones, held wrong view, and undertook actions based on wrong view. They, with the breakup of the body, after death, have arisen in a state of deprivation, a bad destination, a place of ruin, in hell. But indeed, venerable sirs, these other beings were endowed with good conduct of body, speech, and mind, were not revilers of the noble ones, held right view, and undertook actions based on right view. They, with the breakup of the body, after death, have arisen in a good destination, in the heavenly world.’ Thus, with the divine eye, which is purified and surpasses the human, he sees beings passing away and arising—inferior and superior, of good complexion and bad complexion, in good destinations and in bad destinations. He knows beings who fare according to their kamma.

185. So evaṃ samāhite citte parisuddhe pariyodāte anaṅgaṇe vigatūpakkilese mudubhūte kammaniye ṭhite āneñjappatte āsavānaṃ khayañāṇāya cittaṃ abhininnāmeti. So “idaṃ dukkha”nti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhasamudayo”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhanirodho”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ime [P 0.0061] āsavā”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ āsavasamudayo”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ āsavanirodho”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ āsavanirodhagāminī paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ [T 0.0216] pajānāti. Tassa evaṃ jānato evaṃ passato kāmāsavāpi cittaṃ vimuccati, bhavāsavāpi cittaṃ vimuccati, avijjāsavāpi cittaṃ vimuccati. Vimuttasmiṃ vimuttamiti ñāṇaṃ hoti. “Khīṇā jāti, vusitaṃ brahmacariyaṃ, kataṃ karaṇīyaṃ, nāparaṃ itthattāyā”ti pajānāti. Evaṃ puggalo neva attantapo ca hoti na attaparitāpanānuyogamanuyutto, na parantapo na paraparitāpanānuyogamanuyutto. So anattantapo aparantapo diṭṭheva dhamme nicchāto nibbuto sītībhūto sukhappaṭisaṃvedī brahmabhūtena attanā viharati.

185. With his mind thus concentrated, purified, bright, unblemished, free from defilements, malleable, workable, steady, and having attained the imperturbable, he inclines his mind to the knowledge of the destruction of the taints. He knows as it really is: ‘This is suffering.’ He knows as it really is: ‘This is the origin of suffering.’ He knows as it really is: ‘This is the cessation of suffering.’ He knows as it really is: ‘This is the path leading to the cessation of suffering.’ He knows as it really is: ‘These are the taints.’ He knows as it really is: ‘This is the origin of the taints.’ He knows as it really is: ‘This is the cessation of the taints.’ He knows as it really is: ‘This is the path leading to the cessation of the taints.’ For him, thus knowing and thus seeing, the mind is liberated from the taint of sensual desire, the mind is liberated from the taint of becoming, and the mind is liberated from the taint of ignorance. When it is liberated, the knowledge arises: ‘It is liberated.’ He knows: ‘Birth is destroyed, the holy life has been lived, what had to be done has been done, there is no more of this state of being.’ Thus, such a person is neither a self-tormentor nor one engaged in the practice of self-torment, nor a tormentor of others nor one engaged in the practice of tormenting others. He, not tormenting himself and not tormenting others, in this very life, is free from craving, extinguished, cooled, experiencing bliss, and dwells with a self that has become sublime.

186. Katamo ca puggalo sarāgo? Yassa puggalassa rāgo appahīno, ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “sarāgo”.

186. And which person is one with passion? The person for whom passion is not abandoned, this person is called 'one with passion.'

Katamo ca puggalo sadoso? Yassa puggalassa doso appahīno, ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “sadoso”.

And which person is one with aversion? The person for whom aversion is not abandoned, this person is called 'one with aversion.'

Katamo [V 0.0172] ca puggalo samoho? Yassa puggalassa moho appahīno, ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “samoho”.

And which person is one with delusion? The person for whom delusion is not abandoned, this person is called 'one with delusion.'

Katamo ca puggalo samāno? Yassa puggalassa māno appahīno, ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “samāno”.

And which person is one with conceit? The person for whom conceit is not abandoned, this person is called 'one with conceit.'

187. Kathañca [M 0.0171] puggalo lābhī hoti ajjhattaṃ cetosamathassa, na lābhī adhipaññādhammavipassanāya? Idhekacco puggalo lābhī hoti rūpasahagatānaṃ vā arūpasahagatānaṃ vā samāpattīnaṃ, na lābhī lokuttaramaggassa vā phalassa vā. Evaṃ puggalo lābhī hoti ajjhattaṃ cetosamathassa, na lābhī adhipaññādhammavipassanāya.

187. And how is a person a gainer of internal serenity of mind, but not a gainer of the higher wisdom of insight into phenomena? Here, a certain person is a gainer of the form attainments or the formless attainments, but is not a gainer of the supramundane path or its fruit. Thus, a person is a gainer of internal serenity of mind, but not a gainer of the higher wisdom of insight into phenomena.

Kathañca puggalo lābhī hoti adhipaññādhammavipassanāya, na lābhī ajjhattaṃ cetosamathassa? Idhekacco puggalo [T 0.0217] lābhī hoti lokuttaramaggassa vā phalassa vā, na lābhī rūpasahagatānaṃ vā arūpasahagatānaṃ vā samāpattīnaṃ. Evaṃ puggalo lābhī hoti adhipaññādhammavipassanāya, na lābhī ajjhattaṃ cetosamathassa.

And how is a person a gainer of the higher wisdom of insight into phenomena, but not a gainer of internal serenity of mind? Here, a certain person is a gainer of the supramundane path or its fruit, but is not a gainer of the form attainments or the formless attainments. Thus, a person is a gainer of the higher wisdom of insight into phenomena, but not a gainer of internal serenity of mind.

Kathañca puggalo lābhī ceva hoti ajjhattaṃ cetosamathassa, lābhī ca adhipaññādhammavipassanāya? Idhekacco puggalo lābhī hoti rūpasahagatānaṃ vā arūpasahagatānaṃ vā samāpattīnaṃ, lābhī lokuttaramaggassa vā phalassa vā. Evaṃ puggalo lābhī ceva hoti ajjhattaṃ cetosamathassa, lābhī ca adhipaññādhammavipassanāya.

And how is a person a gainer of both internal serenity of mind and the higher wisdom of insight into phenomena? Here, a certain person is a gainer of the form attainments or the formless attainments, and is also a gainer of the supramundane path or its fruit. Thus, a person is a gainer of both internal serenity of mind and the higher wisdom of insight into phenomena.

Kathañca [P 0.0062] puggalo neva lābhī hoti ajjhattaṃ cetosamathassa, na lābhī adhipaññādhammavipassanāya? Idhekacco puggalo neva lābhī hoti rūpasahagatānaṃ vā arūpasahagatānaṃ vā samāpattīnaṃ, na lābhī lokuttaramaggassa vā phalassa vā. Evaṃ puggalo neva lābhī hoti ajjhattaṃ cetosamathassa, na lābhī adhipaññādhammavipassanāya.

And how is a person a gainer of neither internal serenity of mind nor the higher wisdom of insight into phenomena? Here, a certain person is neither a gainer of the form attainments or the formless attainments, nor a gainer of the supramundane path or its fruit. Thus, a person is a gainer of neither internal serenity of mind nor the higher wisdom of insight into phenomena.

188. Katamo ca puggalo anusotagāmī? Idhekacco puggalo kāme ca paṭisevati pāpañca kammaṃ karoti. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “anusotagāmī”.

188. And which person is one who goes along with the stream? Here, a certain person partakes of sensual pleasures and performs evil actions. This person is called 'one who goes along with the stream'.

Katamo [V 0.0173] ca puggalo paṭisotagāmī? Idhekacco puggalo kāme ca na paṭisevati pāpañca kammaṃ na karoti. So sahāpi dukkhena sahāpi domanassena assumukhenapi rudamāno paripuṇṇaṃ parisuddhaṃ brahmacariyaṃ carati. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “paṭisotagāmī”.

And which person is one who goes against the stream? Here, a certain person does not partake of sensual pleasures and does not perform evil actions. That person, with suffering and displeasure, weeping with a tear-stained face, practices the complete and pure holy life. This person is called 'one who goes against the stream'.

Katamo [M 0.0172] ca puggalo ṭhitatto? Idhekacco puggalo pañcannaṃ orambhāgiyānaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ [T 0.0218] parikkhayā opapātiko hoti tattha parinibbāyī anāvattidhammo tasmā lokā. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “ṭhitatto”.

And which person is one of steadfast nature? Here, a certain person, through the destruction of the five lower fetters, is of spontaneous rebirth, will attain Parinibbāna there, and is of a nature not to return from that world. This person is called one of 'steadfast nature'.

Katamo ca puggalo tiṇṇo pāraṅgato thale tiṭṭhati brāhmaṇo? Idhekacco puggalo āsavānaṃ khayā anāsavaṃ cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ diṭṭheva dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharati. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo tiṇṇo pāraṅgato thale tiṭṭhati brāhmaṇo.

And which person is the brahmin who has crossed over, reached the far shore, and stands on dry land? Here, a certain person, through the destruction of the taints, having realized for himself with higher knowledge the taintless liberation of mind and liberation by wisdom in this very life, abides having attained it. This person is called the brahmin 'who has crossed over, reached the far shore, and stands on dry land'.

189. Kathañca puggalo appassuto hoti sutena anupapanno? Idhekaccassa puggalassa appakaṃ sutaṃ hoti suttaṃ geyyaṃ veyyākaraṇaṃ gāthaṃ udānaṃ itivuttakaṃ jātakaṃ abbhutadhammaṃ vedallaṃ. So tassa appakassa sutassa na atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammappaṭipanno na dhammamaññāya na dhammānudhammapaṭipanno (syā.), na dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammappaṭipanno (ka.) [P 0.0063] hoti. Evaṃ puggalo appassuto hoti sutena anupapanno.

189. And how is a person of little learning and not endowed with that learning? Here, a certain person has learned little: discourses, prose-and-verse, expositions, verses, inspired utterances, quotations, birth stories, marvelous accounts, and question-and-answer discourses. Of that little learning, not having understood the meaning and not having understood the Dhamma, that person does not practice in accordance with the Dhamma. In this way, a person is of little learning and not endowed with that learning.

Kathañca puggalo appassuto hoti sutena upapanno? Idhekaccassa puggalassa appakaṃ sutaṃ hoti suttaṃ geyyaṃ veyyākaraṇaṃ gāthaṃ udānaṃ itivuttakaṃ jātakaṃ abbhutadhammaṃ vedallaṃ. So tassa appakassa sutassa atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammappaṭipanno hoti. Evaṃ puggalo appassuto hoti sutena upapanno.

And how is a person of little learning but endowed with that learning? Here, a certain person has learned little: discourses, prose-and-verse, expositions, verses, inspired utterances, quotations, birth stories, marvelous accounts, and question-and-answer discourses. Of that little learning, having understood the meaning and having understood the Dhamma, that person practices in accordance with the Dhamma. In this way, a person is of little learning but endowed with that learning.

Kathañca puggalo bahussuto hoti sutena anupapanno? Idhekaccassa puggalassa bahukaṃ sutaṃ hoti suttaṃ geyyaṃ veyyākaraṇaṃ gāthaṃ udānaṃ itivuttakaṃ jātakaṃ abbhutadhammaṃ vedallaṃ. So tassa bahukassa sutassa na atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammappaṭipanno hoti. Evaṃ puggalo bahussuto [T 0.0219] hoti sutena anupapanno.

And how is a person of much learning but not endowed with that learning? Here, a certain person has learned much: discourses, prose-and-verse, expositions, verses, inspired utterances, quotations, birth stories, marvelous accounts, and question-and-answer discourses. Of that much learning, not having understood the meaning and not having understood the Dhamma, that person does not practice in accordance with the Dhamma. In this way, a person is of much learning but not endowed with that learning.

Kathañca puggalo bahussuto hoti sutena upapanno? Idhekaccassa puggalassa bahukaṃ sutaṃ hoti [V 0.0174] suttaṃ geyyaṃ veyyākaraṇaṃ gāthaṃ udānaṃ itivuttakaṃ jātakaṃ abbhutadhammaṃ vedallaṃ. So tassa bahukassa sutassa atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammappaṭipanno hoti. Evaṃ puggalo bahussuto hoti sutena upapanno.

And how is a person of much learning and endowed with that learning? Here, a certain person has learned much: discourses, prose-and-verse, expositions, verses, inspired utterances, quotations, birth stories, marvelous accounts, and question-and-answer discourses. Of that much learning, having understood the meaning and having understood the Dhamma, that person practices in accordance with the Dhamma. In this way, a person is of much learning and endowed with that learning.

190. Katamo [M 0.0173] ca puggalo samaṇamacalo? Idhekacco puggalo tiṇṇaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ parikkhayā sotāpanno hoti avinipātadhammo niyato sambodhiparāyano. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “samaṇamacalo”.

190. And which person is the immovable ascetic? Here, a certain person, through the destruction of the three fetters, is a stream-enterer, of a nature not subject to perdition, certain, and destined for enlightenment. This person is called the 'immovable ascetic'.

Katamo ca puggalo samaṇapadumo? Idhekacco puggalo tiṇṇaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ parikkhayā rāgadosamohānaṃ tanuttā sakadāgāmī hoti, sakideva imaṃ lokaṃ āgantvā dukkhassantaṃ karoti. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “samaṇapadumo”.

And which person is the red-lotus ascetic? Here, a certain person, through the destruction of the three fetters and the attenuation of lust, hatred, and delusion, is a once-returner; having come to this world only once, he makes an end of suffering. This person is called the 'red-lotus ascetic'.

Katamo ca puggalo samaṇapuṇḍarīko? Idhekacco puggalo pañcannaṃ orambhāgiyānaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ parikkhayā opapātiko hoti tattha parinibbāyī anāvattidhammo tasmā lokā. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “samaṇapuṇḍarīko”.

And which person is the white-lotus ascetic? Here, a certain person, through the destruction of the five lower fetters, is of spontaneous rebirth, will attain Parinibbāna there, and is of a nature not to return from that world. This person is called the 'white-lotus ascetic'.

Katamo ca puggalo samaṇesu samaṇasukhumālo? Idhekacco puggalo āsavānaṃ khayā anāsavaṃ cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ diṭṭheva dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharati. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo “samaṇesu samaṇasukhumālo”ti.

And which person is the most refined ascetic among ascetics? Here, a certain person, through the destruction of the taints, having realized for himself with higher knowledge the taintless liberation of mind and liberation by wisdom in this very life, abides having attained it. This person is called 'the most refined ascetic among ascetics'.

Catukkaniddeso.

The Exposition of the Fours is concluded.

5. Pañcakapuggalapaññatti

5. Designation of Persons by Fives

191. Tatra [T 0.0220][P 0.0064] yvāyaṃ puggalo ārabhati ca vippaṭisārī ca hoti, tañca cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti, yatthassa te uppannā pāpakā akusalā dhammā aparisesā nirujjhanti[V 0.0175], so evamassa vacanīyo – “āyasmato kho ārambhajā ārabbhajā (ka.) a. ni. 5.142 āsavā saṃvijjanti, vippaṭisārajā āsavā pavaḍḍhanti. Sādhu vatāyasmā ārambhaje āsave pahāya vippaṭisāraje āsave paṭivinodetvā cittaṃ paññañca bhāvetu. Evamāyasmā amunā pañcamena puggalena samasamo bhavissatī”ti.

191. Herein, as to that person who commits an offense and is remorseful, and who does not understand as it truly is the liberation of mind and liberation by wisdom wherein his arisen evil, unwholesome states cease without remainder, he should be told thus: ‘Venerable one, the taints born of committing an offense are present in you, and the taints born of remorse are increasing. It would be good, venerable one, if, having abandoned the taints born of committing an offense and dispelled the taints born of remorse, you would develop the insight-mind and insight-wisdom. In this way, you will become the equal of that fifth person.’

Tatra yvāyaṃ puggalo ārabhati na vippaṭisārī hoti, tañca cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti, yatthassa te uppannā pāpakā [M 0.0174] akusalā dhammā aparisesā nirujjhanti, so evamassa vacanīyo – “āyasmato kho ārambhajā āsavā saṃvijjanti, vippaṭisārajā āsavā nappavaḍḍhanti. Sādhu vatāyasmā ārambhaje āsave pahāya cittaṃ paññañca bhāvetu. Evamāyasmā amunā pañcamena puggalena samasamo bhavissatī”ti.

Herein, as to that person who commits an offense but is not remorseful, and who does not understand as it truly is the liberation of mind and liberation by wisdom wherein his arisen evil, unwholesome states cease without remainder, he should be told thus: ‘Venerable one, the taints born of committing an offense are present in you, but the taints born of remorse are not increasing. It would be good, venerable one, if, having abandoned the taints born of committing an offense, you would develop the insight-mind and insight-wisdom. In this way, you will become the equal of that fifth person.’

Tatra yvāyaṃ puggalo na ārabhati vippaṭisārī hoti, tañca cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti, yatthassa te uppannā pāpakā akusalā dhammā aparisesā nirujjhanti, so evamassa vacanīyo – “āyasmato kho ārambhajā āsavā na saṃvijjanti, vippaṭisārajā [T 0.0221] āsavā pavaḍḍhanti. Sādhu vatāyasmā vippaṭisāraje āsave paṭivinodetvā cittaṃ paññañca bhāvetu. Evamāyasmā amunā pañcamena puggalena samasamo bhavissatī”ti.

Herein, as to that person who does not commit an offense but is remorseful, and who does not understand as it truly is the liberation of mind and liberation by wisdom wherein his arisen evil, unwholesome states cease without remainder, he should be told thus: ‘Venerable one, the taints born of committing an offense are not present in you, but the taints born of remorse are increasing. It would be good, venerable one, if, having dispelled the taints born of remorse, you would develop the insight-mind and insight-wisdom. In this way, you will become the equal of that fifth person.’

Tatra yvāyaṃ puggalo na ārabhati na vippaṭisārī hoti, tañca cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ yathābhūtaṃ nappajānāti, yatthassa te pāpakā akusalā dhammā aparisesā nirujjhanti, so evamassa vacanīyo – “āyasmato kho ārambhajā āsavā na saṃvijjanti, vippaṭisārajā āsavā nappavaḍḍhanti. Sādhu vatāyasmā cittaṃ paññañca bhāvetu. Evamāyasmā amunā pañcamena puggalena samasamo bhavissatī”ti. Ime cattāro puggalā amunā pañcamena puggalena evaṃ ovadiyamānā evaṃ anusāsiyamānā anupubbena āsavānaṃ khayaṃ pāpuṇanti.

Herein, as to that person who does not commit an offense and is not remorseful, and who does not understand as it truly is the liberation of mind and liberation by wisdom wherein his evil, unwholesome states cease without remainder, he should be told thus: ‘Venerable one, the taints born of committing an offense are not present in you, and the taints born of remorse are not increasing. It would be good, venerable one, if you would develop the insight-mind and insight-wisdom. In this way, you will become the equal of that fifth person.’ When these four persons are thus advised and instructed by that fifth person, they gradually attain the destruction of the taints.

192. Kathañca [P 0.0065] puggalo datvā avajānāti? Idhekacco puggalo yassa puggalassa deti cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhāraṃ, tassa evaṃ hoti – “ahaṃ dammi, ayaṃ ayaṃ pana (syā. ka.) a. ni. 5.141 paṭiggaṇhātī”ti, tamenaṃ datvā avajānāti. Evaṃ puggalo datvā avajānāti.

192. And how does a person give and then look down on the recipient? Here, a certain person gives robes, almsfood, lodgings, and medicines and provisions for the sick to some person, and it occurs to him: ‘I am giving, he is receiving.’ Having given, he looks down on him. This is how a person gives and then looks down on the recipient.

Kathañca puggalo saṃvāsena avajānāti? Idhekacco puggalo yena puggalena saddhiṃ saṃvasati dve vā tīṇi vā vassāni, tamenaṃ saṃvāsena avajānāti. Evaṃ puggalo saṃvāsena avajānāti.

And how does a person look down on another because of living together? Here, a certain person lives together with some person for two or three years, and because of living together with him, he looks down on him. This is how a person looks down on another because of living together.

Kathañca [M 0.0175] puggalo ādheyyamukho hoti? Idhekacco puggalo parassa vaṇṇe vā avaṇṇe vā bhāsiyamāne khippaññeva adhimuccitā hoti. Evaṃ puggalo ādheyyamukho hoti[T 0.0222].

And how is a person easily led? Here, when praise or blame of another is spoken, a certain person is quickly won over. This is how a person is easily led.

Kathañca [V 0.0176] puggalo lolo hoti? Idhekacco puggalo ittarasaddho hoti ittarabhattī ittarapemo ittarappasādo. Evaṃ puggalo lolo hoti.

And how is a person fickle? Here, a certain person has short-lived faith, short-lived devotion, short-lived affection, and short-lived confidence. This is how a person is fickle.

Kathañca puggalo mando momūho hoti? Idhekacco puggalo kusalākusale dhamme na jānāti, sāvajjānavajje dhamme na jānāti, hīnappaṇīte dhamme na jānāti, kaṇhasukkasappaṭibhāge dhamme na jānāti. Evaṃ puggalo mando momūho hoti.

And how is a person dull and bewildered? Here, a certain person does not know wholesome and unwholesome states, does not know blameworthy and blameless states, does not know inferior and superior states, and does not know dark and bright states with their counterparts. This is how a person is dull and bewildered.

193. Tattha katame pañca yodhājīvūpamā puggalā? Pañca yodhājīvā – idhekacco yodhājīvo rajaggaññeva disvā saṃsīdati visīdati na santhambhati satthambhati (sī.) a. ni. 5.141 na sakkoti saṅgāmaṃ otarituṃ. Evarūpopi idhekacco yodhājīvo hoti. Ayaṃ paṭhamo yodhājīvo santo saṃvijjamāno lokasmiṃ.

193. Therein, what are the five persons comparable to professional soldiers? There are five kinds of professional soldiers. Here, a certain professional soldier, just on seeing the cloud of dust, sinks down, becomes dejected, cannot hold his ground, and is unable to enter the battle. Such a kind of professional soldier also exists. This is the first professional soldier to be found existing in the world.

Puna caparaṃ idhekacco yodhājīvo sahati rajaggaṃ, api ca kho dhajaggaññeva disvā saṃsīdati visīdati na santhambhati na sakkoti saṅgāmaṃ otarituṃ. Evarūpopi idhekacco yodhājīvo hoti. Ayaṃ dutiyo yodhājīvo santo saṃvijjamāno lokasmiṃ.

Furthermore, here a certain professional soldier endures the dust cloud; but on seeing just the banner top, he sinks down, becomes dejected, cannot stand firm, and is unable to enter the battle. Such a kind of professional soldier also exists. This is the second professional soldier to be found existing in the world.

Puna caparaṃ idhekacco yodhājīvo sahati rajaggaṃ sahati dhajaggaṃ, api ca kho ussāraṇaññeva ussādanaṃyeva (sī.), ussādanaññeva (syā. ka.) a. ni. 5.141 sutvā saṃsīdati visīdati na santhambhati na sakkoti saṅgāmaṃ otarituṃ. Evarūpopi idhekacco yodhājīvo hoti. Ayaṃ tatiyo yodhājīvo santo saṃvijjamāno lokasmiṃ.

Furthermore, here a certain professional soldier endures the dust cloud and the banner top; but on hearing just the battle cry, he sinks down, becomes dejected, cannot stand firm, and is unable to enter the battle. Such a kind of professional soldier also exists. This is the third professional soldier to be found existing in the world.

Puna caparaṃ idhekacco yodhājīvo sahati rajaggaṃ sahati [P 0.0066] dhajaggaṃ sahati ussāraṇaṃ, api [V 0.0177] ca kho sampahāre haññati byāpajjati. Evarūpopi idhekacco yodhājīvo hoti. Ayaṃ catuttho yodhājīvo [T 0.0223] santo saṃvijjamāno lokasmiṃ.

Furthermore, here a certain professional soldier endures the dust cloud, the banner top, and the battle cry; but in the clash of battle he is wounded and overcome. Such a kind of professional soldier also exists. This is the fourth professional soldier to be found existing in the world.

Puna [M 0.0176] caparaṃ idhekacco yodhājīvo sahati rajaggaṃ sahati dhajaggaṃ sahati ussāraṇaṃ sahati sampahāraṃ. So taṃ saṅgāmaṃ abhivijinitvā vijitasaṅgāmo tameva saṅgāmasīsaṃ ajjhāvasati. Evarūpopi idhekacco yodhājīvo hoti. Ayaṃ pañcamo yodhājīvo santo saṃvijjamāno lokasmiṃ. Ime pañca yodhājīvā santo saṃvijjamānā lokasmiṃ.

Furthermore, here a certain professional soldier endures the dust cloud, the banner top, the battle cry, and the clash of battle. Having conquered that battle, victorious in battle, he dwells right at the head of that very battle. Such a kind of professional soldier also exists. This is the fifth professional soldier to be found existing in the world. These five professional soldiers are to be found existing in the world.

194. Evamevaṃ pañcime yodhājīvūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā bhikkhūsu. Katame pañca? Idhekacco bhikkhu rajaggaññeva disvā saṃsīdati visīdati na santhambhati na sakkoti brahmacariyaṃ sandhāretuṃ santānetuṃ (sī. syā.) a. ni. 5.75, sikkhādubbalyaṃ āvikatvā āvīkatvā (sī.) sikkhaṃ paccakkhāya hīnāyāvattati. Kimassa rajaggasmiṃ? Idha bhikkhu suṇāti – “asukasmiṃ nāma gāme vā nigame vā itthī vā kumārī vā abhirūpā dassanīyā pāsādikā paramāya vaṇṇapokkharatāya samannāgatā”ti. So taṃ sutvā saṃsīdati visīdati na santhambhati na sakkoti brahmacariyaṃ sandhāretuṃ, sikkhādubbalyaṃ āvikatvā sikkhaṃ paccakkhāya hīnāyāvattati. Idamassa rajaggasmiṃ.

194. In the very same way, these five persons comparable to warriors are to be found existing among the bhikkhus. What five? Here, a certain bhikkhu, on seeing just the dust cloud, sinks down, becomes dejected, cannot stand firm, and is unable to continue the holy life; having declared his weakness in the training, he renounces the training and reverts to the low life. What, for him, is the dust cloud? Here, a bhikkhu hears: ‘In such and such a village or town there is a woman or a girl who is beautiful, good-looking, pleasing, endowed with the supreme beauty of complexion.’ Having heard this, he sinks down, becomes dejected, cannot stand firm, and is unable to continue the holy life; having declared his weakness in the training, he renounces the training and reverts to the low life. This, for him, is the dust cloud.

Seyyathāpi so yodhājīvo rajaggaññeva disvā saṃsīdati visīdati na santhambhati na sakkoti saṅgāmaṃ otarituṃ, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo. Evarūpopi idhekacco puggalo hoti. Ayaṃ paṭhamo yodhājīvūpamo puggalo santo saṃvijjamāno bhikkhūsu.

Just as that warrior, on seeing just the dust cloud, sinks down, becomes dejected, cannot stand firm, and is unable to enter the battle, so too is this person comparable. Such a kind of person also exists. This is the first person comparable to a warrior to be found existing among the bhikkhus.

195. Puna caparaṃ idhekacco bhikkhu sahati rajaggaṃ, api ca kho dhajaggaññeva disvā saṃsīdati visīdati na santhambhati [T 0.0224] na sakkoti brahmacariyaṃ sandhāretuṃ, sikkhādubbalyaṃ āvikatvā sikkhaṃ paccakkhāya hīnāyāvattati. Kimassa dhajaggasmiṃ? Idha bhikkhu na heva kho suṇāti – “asukasmiṃ nāma gāme vā nigame vā itthī vā kumārī vā abhirūpā dassanīyā pāsādikā paramāya vaṇṇapokkharatāya samannāgatā”ti, api ca kho sāmaṃ sāmaṃyeva (sī.) passati itthiṃ vā kumāriṃ vā abhirūpaṃ dassanīyaṃ pāsādikaṃ paramāya vaṇṇapokkharatāya samannāgataṃ. So taṃ disvā saṃsīdati visīdati na santhambhati na sakkoti brahmacariyaṃ sandhāretuṃ, sikkhādubbalyaṃ āvikatvā sikkhaṃ paccakkhāya hīnāyāvattati. Idamassa dhajaggasmiṃ.

195. Furthermore, here a certain bhikkhu endures the dust cloud; but on seeing just the banner top, he sinks down, despairs, cannot stand firm, and is unable to continue the holy life; having declared his weakness in the training, he renounces the training and reverts to the low life. What, for him, is the banner top? Here, a bhikkhu does not just hear: ‘In such and such a village or town there is a woman or a girl who is beautiful, good-looking, pleasing, endowed with the supreme beauty of complexion,’ but he himself sees a woman or a girl who is beautiful, good-looking, pleasing, endowed with the supreme beauty of complexion. On seeing her, he sinks down, despairs, cannot stand firm, and is unable to continue the holy life; having declared his weakness in the training, he renounces the training and reverts to the low life. This, for him, is the banner top.

Seyyathāpi [P 0.0067][M 0.0177][V 0.0178] so yodhājīvo sahati rajaggaṃ, api ca kho dhajaggaññeva disvā saṃsīdati visīdati na santhambhati na sakkoti saṅgāmaṃ otarituṃ, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo. Evarūpopi idhekacco puggalo hoti. Ayaṃ dutiyo yodhājīvūpamo puggalo santo saṃvijjamāno bhikkhūsu.

Just as that warrior endures the dust cloud, but on seeing just the banner top, sinks down, despairs, cannot stand firm, and is unable to enter the battle, so too is this person comparable. Such a kind of person exists here. This is the second person comparable to a warrior to be found existing among the bhikkhus.

196. Puna caparaṃ idhekacco bhikkhu sahati rajaggaṃ sahati dhajaggaṃ, api ca kho ussāraṇaññeva sutvā saṃsīdati visīdati na santhambhati na sakkoti brahmacariyaṃ sandhāretuṃ, sikkhādubbalyaṃ āvikatvā sikkhaṃ paccakkhāya hīnāyāvattati. Kimassa ussāraṇāya? Idha bhikkhuṃ araññagataṃ vā rukkhamūlagataṃ vā suññāgāragataṃ vā mātugāmo upasaṅkamitvā ūhasati uhasati (aṭṭhakathā) a. ni. 5.75 ullapati ujjagghati uppaṇḍeti. So mātugāmena ūhasiyamāno ullapiyamāno ujjagghiyamāno uppaṇḍiyamāno saṃsīdati visīdati na santhambhati na sakkoti brahmacariyaṃ [T 0.0225] sandhāretuṃ, sikkhādubbalyaṃ āvikatvā sikkhaṃ paccakkhāya hīnāyāvattati. Idamassa ussāraṇāya.

196. Furthermore, here a certain bhikkhu endures the dust cloud and the banner top; but on hearing just the battle cry, he sinks down, despairs, cannot stand firm, and is unable to continue the holy life; having declared his weakness in the training, he renounces the training and reverts to the low life. What, for him, is the battle cry? Here, when a bhikkhu has gone to the forest, to the root of a tree, or to an empty dwelling, a woman approaches and smiles at him, speaks to him, laughs aloud, and teases him. Being smiled at, spoken to, laughed at, and teased by the woman, he sinks down, despairs, cannot stand firm, and is unable to continue the holy life; having declared his weakness in the training, he renounces the training and reverts to the low life. This, for him, is the battle cry.

Seyyathāpi so yodhājīvo sahati rajaggaṃ sahati dhajaggaṃ, api ca kho ussāraṇaññeva sutvā saṃsīdati visīdati na santhambhati na sakkoti saṅgāmaṃ otarituṃ, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo. Evarūpopi idhekacco puggalo hoti. Ayaṃ tatiyo yodhājīvūpamo puggalo santo saṃvijjamāno bhikkhūsu.

Just as that warrior endures the dust cloud and the banner top, but on hearing just the battle cry, sinks down, despairs, cannot stand firm, and is unable to enter the battle, so too is this person comparable. Such a kind of person exists here. This is the third person comparable to a warrior to be found existing among the bhikkhus.

197. Puna caparaṃ idhekacco bhikkhu sahati rajaggaṃ sahati dhajaggaṃ sahati ussāraṇaṃ, api ca kho sampahāre haññati byāpajjati. Kimassa sampahārasmiṃ? Idha bhikkhuṃ araññagataṃ vā rukkhamūlagataṃ vā suññāgāragataṃ vā mātugāmo upasaṅkamitvā abhinisīdati abhinipajjati ajjhottharati. So mātugāmena abhinisīdiyamāno abhinipajjiyamāno ajjhotthariyamāno sikkhaṃ appaccakkhāya dubbalyaṃ anāvikatvā methunaṃ dhammaṃ paṭisevati. Idamassa sampahārasmiṃ.

197. Furthermore, here a certain bhikkhu endures the dust cloud, the banner top, and the battle cry; but in the clash of battle he is struck down and perishes. What, for him, is the clash of battle? Here, when a bhikkhu has gone to the forest, to the root of a tree, or to an empty dwelling, a woman approaches him, sits down close to him, lies down close to him, and covers him. Being sat close to, lain down close to, and covered by the woman, without having renounced the training and without having declared his weakness, he engages in sexual intercourse. This, for him, is the clash of battle.

Seyyathāpi so yodhājīvo sahati rajaggaṃ sahati dhajaggaṃ sahati ussāraṇaṃ, api ca kho sampahāre haññati byāpajjati, tathūpamo [M 0.0178] ayaṃ puggalo. Evarūpopi idhekacco puggalo hoti. Ayaṃ catuttho yodhājīvūpamo puggalo santo saṃvijjamāno bhikkhūsu.

Just as that warrior endures the dust cloud, the banner top, and the battle cry, but in the clash of battle is struck down and perishes, so too is this person comparable. Such a kind of person exists here. This is the fourth person comparable to a warrior to be found existing among the bhikkhus.

198. Puna [V 0.0179] caparaṃ idhekacco bhikkhu sahati rajaggaṃ sahati dhajaggaṃ sahati ussāraṇaṃ sahati sampahāraṃ. So taṃ saṅgāmaṃ abhivijinitvā vijitasaṅgāmo tameva saṅgāmasīsaṃ ajjhāvasati. Kimassa saṅgāmavijayasmiṃ? Idha bhikkhuṃ [P 0.0068] araññagataṃ vā rukkhamūlagataṃ vā suññāgāragataṃ vā mātugāmo upasaṅkamitvā abhinisīdati [T 0.0226] abhinipajjati ajjhottharati. So mātugāmena abhinisīdiyamāno abhinipajjiyamāno ajjhotthariyamāno viniveṭhetvā vinimocetvā yena kāmaṃ pakkamati.

198. Furthermore, here a certain bhikkhu endures the dust cloud, the banner top, the battle cry, and the clash of battle. Having conquered that battle, victorious in battle, he dwells right at the head of that very battle. What, for him, is victory in battle? Here, when a bhikkhu has gone to the forest, to the root of a tree, or to an empty dwelling, a woman approaches him, sits down close to him, lies down close to him, and covers him. But when he is being sat close to, lain down close to, and covered by the woman, he disentangles himself, frees himself, and departs wherever he wishes.

So vivittaṃ senāsanaṃ bhajati araññaṃ rukkhamūlaṃ pabbataṃ kandaraṃ giriguhaṃ susānaṃ vanapatthaṃ abbhokāsaṃ palālapuñjaṃ. So araññagato vā rukkhamūlagato vā suññāgāragato vā nisīdati pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā ujuṃ kāyaṃ paṇidhāya parimukhaṃ satiṃ upaṭṭhapetvā. So abhijjhaṃ loke pahāya vigatābhijjhena cetasā viharati, abhijjhāya cittaṃ parisodheti; byāpādapadosaṃ pahāya abyāpannacitto viharati, sabbapāṇabhūtahitānukampī byāpādapadosā cittaṃ parisodheti; thinamiddhaṃ pahāya vigatathinamiddho viharati ālokasaññī sato sampajāno, thinamiddhā cittaṃ parisodheti; uddhaccakukkuccaṃ pahāya anuddhato viharati ajjhattaṃ vūpasantacitto, uddhaccakukkuccā cittaṃ parisodheti; vicikicchaṃ pahāya tiṇṇavicikiccho viharati akathaṃkathī kusalesu dhammesu, vicikicchāya cittaṃ parisodheti.

He resorts to a secluded lodging: a forest, the root of a tree, a mountain, a ravine, a mountain cave, a cemetery, a forest thicket, an open space, or a heap of straw. Having gone to the forest, or to the root of a tree, or to an empty dwelling, he sits down, folds his legs crosswise, sets his body erect, and establishes mindfulness directed towards his meditation subject. Having abandoned covetousness for the world, he dwells with a mind free from covetousness; he purifies his mind from covetousness. Having abandoned ill will and hatred, he dwells with a mind free from ill will, compassionate for the welfare of all living beings; he purifies his mind from ill will and hatred. Having abandoned sloth and torpor, he dwells free from sloth and torpor, percipient of light, mindful and clearly comprehending; he purifies his mind from sloth and torpor. Having abandoned restlessness and remorse, he dwells unagitated with a mind inwardly peaceful; he purifies his mind from restlessness and remorse. Having abandoned doubt, he dwells having crossed over doubt, free from perplexity about wholesome states; he purifies his mind from doubt.

So ime pañca nīvaraṇe pahāya cetaso upakkilese paññāya dubbalīkaraṇe vivicceva kāmehi vivicca akusalehi dhammehi savitakkaṃ savicāraṃ vivekajaṃ pītisukhaṃ paṭhamaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati; vitakkavicārānaṃ vūpasamā dutiyaṃ jhānaṃ…pe… tatiyaṃ jhānaṃ…pe… catutthaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati.

Having abandoned these five hindrances—defilements of the mind that weaken wisdom—quite secluded from sensual pleasures, secluded from unwholesome states, he enters and abides in the first jhāna, which is accompanied by applied and sustained thought, with rapture and pleasure born of seclusion. With the stilling of applied and sustained thought, he enters and abides in the second jhāna… the third jhāna… the fourth jhāna.

So evaṃ samāhite citte parisuddhe pariyodāte anaṅgaṇe vigatūpakkilese mudubhūte kammaniye ṭhite āneñjappatte āsavānaṃ khayañāṇāya cittaṃ abhininnāmeti. So “idaṃ dukkha”nti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ [T 0.0227] dukkhasamudayo”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhanirodho”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ime āsavā”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ āsavasamudayo”ti [M 0.0179] yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti[V 0.0180], “ayaṃ āsavanirodho”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, “ayaṃ āsavanirodhagāminī paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti.

When his mind is thus concentrated, purified, bright, unblemished, rid of defilements, malleable, workable, steady, and has attained imperturbability, he directs and inclines his mind to the knowledge of the destruction of the taints. He understands as it actually is: ‘This is suffering.’ He understands as it actually is: ‘This is the origin of suffering.’ He understands as it actually is: ‘This is the cessation of suffering.’ He understands as it actually is: ‘This is the path leading to the cessation of suffering.’ He understands as it actually is: ‘These are the taints.’ He understands as it actually is: ‘This is the origin of the taints.’ He understands as it actually is: ‘This is the cessation of the taints.’ He understands as it actually is: ‘This is the path leading to the cessation of the taints.’

Tassa evaṃ jānato evaṃ passato kāmāsavāpi cittaṃ vimuccati, bhavāsavāpi cittaṃ vimuccati, avijjāsavāpi cittaṃ vimuccati. Vimuttasmiṃ vimuttamiti ñāṇaṃ hoti. “Khīṇā jāti, vusitaṃ brahmacariyaṃ, kataṃ karaṇīyaṃ, nāparaṃ itthattāyā”ti pajānāti. Idamassa saṅgāmavijayasmiṃ. Seyyathāpi so yodhājīvo sahati rajaggaṃ sahati dhajaggaṃ sahati ussāraṇaṃ sahati sampahāraṃ, so taṃ saṅgāmaṃ abhivijinitvā vijitasaṅgāmo [P 0.0069] tameva saṅgāmasīsaṃ ajjhāvasati, tathūpamo ayaṃ puggalo. Evarūpopi idhekacco puggalo hoti. Ayaṃ pañcamo yodhājīvūpamo puggalo santo saṃvijjamāno bhikkhūsu. Ime pañca yodhājīvūpamā puggalā santo saṃvijjamānā bhikkhūsu.

For one who knows and sees thus, his mind is liberated from the taint of sensual desire, from the taint of existence, and from the taint of ignorance. When it is liberated, the knowledge arises: ‘It is liberated.’ He understands: ‘Destroyed is birth, the holy life has been lived, what had to be done has been done, there is no more of this state of being.’ This, for him, is victory in battle. Just as that warrior who lives by fighting withstands the cloud of dust, withstands the banner-tip, withstands the uproar, and withstands the clash of weapons, and having conquered the battle, victorious in battle, he dwells at the very head of the battle—so too is this person. Such a kind of person is found here. This is the fifth warrior-like person found among the bhikkhus. These are the five warrior-like persons found among the bhikkhus.

199. Tattha katame pañca piṇḍapātikā? Mandattā momūhattā piṇḍapātiko hoti, pāpiccho icchāpakato piṇḍapātiko hoti, ummādā cittavikkhepā piṇḍapātiko hoti, “vaṇṇitaṃ buddhehi buddhasāvakehī”ti piṇḍapātiko hoti, api ca appicchataṃyeva appicchaṃyeva (syā.) a. ni. 5.181 nissāya santuṭṭhiṃyeva nissāya sallekhaṃyeva nissāya idamatthitaṃyeva idamaṭṭhitaṃyeva (sī.) nissāya piṇḍapātiko hoti. Tatra yvāyaṃ piṇḍapātiko appicchataṃyeva nissāya santuṭṭhiṃyeva [T 0.0228] nissāya sallekhaṃyeva nissāya idamatthitaṃyeva nissāya piṇḍapātiko, ayaṃ imesaṃ pañcannaṃ piṇḍapātikānaṃ aggo ca seṭṭho ca pāmokkho mokkho (sī.) ca uttamo ca pavaro ca.

199. Herein, what are the five kinds of alms-food practitioners? One is an alms-food practitioner due to dullness and delusion. One is an alms-food practitioner because of evil desires and being overcome by wishes. One is an alms-food practitioner due to madness and mental distraction. One is an alms-food practitioner thinking, “This has been praised by the Buddhas and the Buddha’s disciples.” Furthermore, one is an alms-food practitioner relying solely on fewness of wishes, on contentment, on effacement, and on the desire for this good practice. Among these, the alms-food practitioner who relies solely on fewness of wishes, on contentment, on effacement, and on the desire for this good practice, is the foremost, the best, the chief, the highest, and the most excellent of these five alms-food practitioners.

Seyyathāpi nāma gavā khīraṃ, khīramhā dadhi, dadhimhā navanītaṃ nonītaṃ (sī.), navanītamhā sappi, sappimhā sappimaṇḍo, sappimaṇḍaṃ tattha aggamakkhāyati; evamevaṃ yvāyaṃ piṇḍapātiko appicchataṃyeva nissāya santuṭṭhiṃyeva nissāya sallekhaṃyeva nissāya idamatthitaṃyeva nissāya piṇḍapātiko hoti, ayaṃ imesaṃ pañcannaṃ piṇḍapātikānaṃ aggo ca seṭṭho ca pāmokkho ca uttamo ca pavaro ca. Ime pañca piṇḍapātikā.

Just as from a cow comes milk, from milk curds, from curds fresh butter, from fresh butter ghee, and from ghee the cream of ghee, and the cream of ghee is reckoned the best; so too, the alms-food practitioner who relies solely on fewness of wishes, on contentment, on effacement, and on the desire for this good practice, is the foremost, the best, the chief, the highest, and the most excellent among these five alms-food practitioners. These are the five alms-food practitioners.

200. Tattha [M 0.0180] katame pañca khalupacchābhattikā…pe… pañca ekāsanikā…pe… pañca paṃsukūlikā…pe… pañca tecīvarikā…pe… pañca āraññikā…pe… pañca rukkhamūlikā [V 0.0181] …pe… pañca abbhokāsikā…pe… pañca nesajjikā…pe… pañca yathāsanthatikā…pe….

200. Herein, what are the five kinds of practitioners who refuse food offered later? … the five kinds of one-session practitioners … the five kinds of rag-robe practitioners … the five kinds of triple-robe practitioners … the five kinds of forest-dwelling practitioners … the five kinds of tree-root-dwelling practitioners … the five kinds of open-air-dwelling practitioners … the five kinds of sitting practitioners … the five kinds of practitioners who accept any assigned lodging…

201. Tattha katame pañca sosānikā? Mandattā momūhattā sosāniko hoti, pāpiccho icchāpakato sosāniko hoti, ummādā cittavikkhepā sosāniko hoti, “vaṇṇitaṃ buddhehi buddhasāvakehī”ti sosāniko hoti, api ca appicchataṃyeva nissāya santuṭṭhiṃyeva nissāya sallekhaṃyeva nissāya idamatthitaṃyeva nissāya sosāniko hoti. Tatra yvāyaṃ sosāniko appicchataṃyeva nissāya santuṭṭhiṃyeva [P 0.0070] nissāya sallekhaṃyeva nissāya idamatthitaṃyeva nissāya sosāniko, ayaṃ imesaṃ pañcannaṃ sosānikānaṃ aggo ca seṭṭho ca [T 0.0229] pāmokkho ca uttamo ca pavaro ca.

201. Herein, what are the five kinds of charnel-ground practitioners? One is a charnel-ground practitioner due to dullness and delusion. One is a charnel-ground practitioner because of evil desires and being overcome by wishes. One is a charnel-ground practitioner due to madness and mental distraction. One is a charnel-ground practitioner thinking, “This has been praised by the Buddhas and the Buddha’s disciples.” Furthermore, one is a charnel-ground practitioner relying solely on fewness of wishes, on contentment, on effacement, and on the desire for this good practice. Among these, that charnel-ground practitioner who relies solely on fewness of wishes, on contentment, on effacement, and on the desire for this good practice, is the foremost, the best, the chief, the highest, and the most excellent of these five charnel-ground practitioners.

Seyyathāpi nāma gavā khīraṃ, khīramhā dadhi, dadhimhā navanītaṃ, navanītamhā sappi, sappimhā sappimaṇḍo, sappimaṇḍaṃ tattha aggamakkhāyati; evamevaṃ yvāyaṃ sosāniko appicchataṃyeva nissāya santuṭṭhiṃyeva nissāya sallekhaṃyeva nissāya idamatthitaṃyeva nissāya sosāniko hoti, ayaṃ imesaṃ pañcannaṃ sosānikānaṃ aggo ca seṭṭho ca pāmokkho ca uttamo ca pavaro ca. Ime pañca sosānikā.

Just as from a cow comes milk, from milk curds, from curds fresh butter, from fresh butter ghee, and from ghee the cream of ghee, and the cream of ghee is said to be the best; so too, the charnel-ground practitioner who relies solely on fewness of wishes, on contentment, on effacement, and on the desire for this good practice—this person, among these five charnel-ground practitioners, is the foremost, the best, the chief, the highest, and the most excellent. These are the five charnel-ground practitioners.

Pañcakaniddeso.

The Exposition of the Fives is concluded.

6. Chakkapuggalapaññatti

6. Designation of Persons in Sixes

202. Tatra yvāyaṃ puggalo pubbe ananussutesu dhammesu sāmaṃ saccāni abhisambujjhati, tattha ca sabbaññutaṃ pāpuṇāti balesu ca vasībhāvaṃ, sammāsambuddho tena daṭṭhabbo.

202. Herein, that person who, in regard to dhammas unheard before, awakens by himself to the truths, and therein attains omniscience and mastery over the powers, is to be understood as a Perfectly Self-Enlightened One.

Tatra [V 0.0182] yvāyaṃ puggalo pubbe ananussutesu dhammesu sāmaṃ saccāni abhisambujjhati, na ca tattha sabbaññutaṃ pāpuṇāti na ca balesu vasībhāvaṃ, paccekasambuddho tena daṭṭhabbo.

Herein, that person who, in regard to dhammas unheard before, awakens by himself to the truths, but does not therein attain omniscience nor mastery over the powers, is to be understood as an Independently Enlightened One.

Tatra [M 0.0181] yvāyaṃ puggalo pubbe ananussutesu dhammesu sāmaṃ saccāni anabhisambujjhati, diṭṭheva dhamme dukkhassantakaro hoti dukkhassantaṃ karoti (sī.) evamuparipi, sāvakapāramiñca pāpuṇāti, sāriputtamoggallānā tena daṭṭhabbā.

Herein, that person who, in regard to dhammas unheard before, does not awaken to the truths by himself, but makes an end of suffering in this very life and attains the perfection of a disciple—by this are to be understood Sāriputta and Moggallāna.

Tatra yvāyaṃ puggalo pubbe ananussutesu dhammesu sāmaṃ saccāni anabhisambujjhati[T 0.0230], diṭṭheva dhamme dukkhassantakaro hoti, na ca sāvakapāramiṃ pāpuṇāti, avasesā arahantā tena daṭṭhabbā.

Herein, that person who, in regard to dhammas unheard before, does not awaken to the truths by himself, but makes an end of suffering in this very life, yet does not attain the perfection of a disciple—by this are to be understood the remaining arahants.

Tatra yvāyaṃ puggalo pubbe ananussutesu dhammesu sāmaṃ saccāni anabhisambujjhati, na ca diṭṭheva dhamme dukkhassantakaro hoti, anāgāmī hoti anāgantā itthattaṃ, anāgāmī [P 0.0071] tena daṭṭhabbo.

Herein, that person who, in regard to dhammas unheard before, does not awaken to the truths by himself and does not make an end of suffering in this very life, but is a non-returner, not coming back to this state of existence—by this, he is to be understood as a non-returner.

Tatra yvāyaṃ puggalo pubbe ananussutesu dhammesu sāmaṃ saccāni anabhisambujjhati, na ca diṭṭheva dhamme dukkhassantakaro hoti, āgantā itthattaṃ, sotāpannasakadāgāmino tena daṭṭhabbā.

Herein, that person who, in regard to dhammas unheard before, does not awaken to the truths by himself and does not make an end of suffering in this very life, but is one who comes back to this state of existence—by this are to be understood stream-enterers and once-returners.

Chakkaniddeso.

The Exposition of the Six is concluded.

7. Sattakapuggalapaññatti

7. Designation of Persons: The Group of Seven

203. Kathañca puggalo sakiṃ nimuggo nimuggova hoti? Idhekacco puggalo samannāgato hoti ekantakāḷakehi akusalehi dhammehi. Evaṃ puggalo sakiṃ nimuggo nimuggova hoti.

203. And how is it that a person, having sunk once, remains sunk? Here, a certain person is endowed with exclusively dark, unwholesome states. In this way a person, having sunk once, remains sunk.

Kathañca puggalo ummujjitvā nimujjati? Idhekacco puggalo ummujjati “sāhu saddhā kusalesu dhammesu, sādhu sāhu (sī. syā.) evaṃ tīsu ṭhānesupi hirī kusalesu dhammesu, sādhu ottappaṃ kusalesu dhammesu, sādhu vīriyaṃ viriyaṃ (sī. syā.) kusalesu dhammesu, sādhu paññā kusalesu dhammesū”ti. Tassa sā saddhā neva tiṭṭhati no vaḍḍhati hāyatiyeva[V 0.0183], tassa sā hirī neva tiṭṭhati no vaḍḍhati hāyatiyeva, tassa [T 0.0231] taṃ ottappaṃ neva tiṭṭhati no vaḍḍhati hāyatiyeva, tassa taṃ vīriyaṃ neva tiṭṭhati no vaḍḍhati hāyatiyeva, tassa sā paññā neva tiṭṭhati no vaḍḍhati hāyatiyeva. Evaṃ puggalo ummujjitvā nimujjati.

And how is it that a person, having emerged, sinks back down? Here, a certain person emerges, thinking: ‘It is good to have faith in wholesome states, good to have moral shame in wholesome states, good to have moral dread in wholesome states, good to have energy in wholesome states, good to have wisdom in wholesome states.’ But that faith of his neither persists nor grows; it only declines. That moral shame of his neither persists nor grows; it only declines. That moral dread of his neither persists nor grows; it only declines. That energy of his neither persists nor grows; it only declines. That wisdom of his neither persists nor grows; it only declines. In this way a person, having emerged, sinks back down.

Kathañca [M 0.0182] puggalo ummujjitvā ṭhito hoti? Idhekacco puggalo ummujjati “sāhu saddhā kusalesu dhammesu, sādhu hirī kusalesu dhammesu, sādhu ottappaṃ kusalesu dhammesu, sādhu vīriyaṃ kusalesu dhammesu, sādhu paññā kusalesu dhammesū”ti. Tassa sā saddhā neva hāyati no vaḍḍhati ṭhitā hoti, tassa sā hirī neva hāyati no vaḍḍhati ṭhitā hoti, tassa taṃ ottappaṃ neva hāyati no vaḍḍhati ṭhitaṃ hoti, tassa taṃ vīriyaṃ neva hāyati no vaḍḍhati ṭhitaṃ hoti, tassa sā paññā neva hāyati no vaḍḍhati ṭhitā hoti. Evaṃ puggalo ummujjitvā ṭhito hoti.

And how is it that a person, having emerged, remains stationary? Here, a certain person emerges, thinking: ‘It is good to have faith in wholesome states, good to have moral shame in wholesome states, good to have moral dread in wholesome states, good to have energy in wholesome states, good to have wisdom in wholesome states.’ That faith of his neither declines nor grows but is stationary. That moral shame of his neither declines nor grows but is stationary. That moral dread of his neither declines nor grows but is stationary. That energy of his neither declines nor grows but is stationary. That wisdom of his neither declines nor grows but is stationary. In this way a person, having emerged, remains stationary.

Kathañca puggalo ummujjitvā vipassati viloketi? Idhekacco puggalo ummujjati “sāhu saddhā kusalesu dhammesu, sādhu hirī kusalesu dhammesu, sādhu ottappaṃ kusalesu dhammesu, sādhu vīriyaṃ kusalesu dhammesu, sādhu paññā [P 0.0072] kusalesu dhammesū”ti. So tiṇṇaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ [T 0.0232] parikkhayā sotāpanno hoti avinipātadhammo niyato sambodhiparāyano. Evaṃ puggalo ummujjitvā vipassati viloketi.

And how is it that a person, having emerged, looks around and surveys? Here, a certain person emerges, thinking: ‘It is good to have faith in wholesome states, good to have moral shame in wholesome states, good to have moral dread in wholesome states, good to have energy in wholesome states, good to have wisdom in wholesome states.’ With the destruction of the three fetters, he becomes a stream-enterer, no longer subject to downfall, fixed in destiny, bound for enlightenment. In this way a person, having emerged, looks around and surveys.

Kathañca puggalo ummujjitvā patarati? Idhekacco puggalo ummujjati “sāhu saddhā kusalesu dhammesu, sādhu hirī kusalesu dhammesu, sādhu ottappaṃ kusalesu dhammesu, sādhu vīriyaṃ kusalesu dhammesu, sādhu paññā kusalesu dhammesū”ti. So tiṇṇaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ parikkhayā rāgadosamohānaṃ tanuttā sakadāgāmī hoti sakideva imaṃ lokaṃ āgantvā dukkhassantakaro hoti. Evaṃ puggalo ummujjitvā patarati.

And how is it that a person, having emerged, crosses over? Here, a certain person emerges, thinking: ‘It is good to have faith in wholesome states, good to have moral shame in wholesome states, good to have moral dread in wholesome states, good to have energy in wholesome states, good to have wisdom in wholesome states.’ With the destruction of the three fetters and with the attenuation of lust, hatred, and delusion, he becomes a once-returner; having come back to this world only one more time, he will make an end of suffering. In this way a person, having emerged, crosses over.

Kathañca puggalo ummujjitvā patigādhappatto hoti? Idhekacco puggalo ummujjati “sāhu saddhā kusalesu dhammesu, sādhu hirī kusalesu dhammesu, sādhu ottappaṃ kusalesu dhammesu, sādhu vīriyaṃ kusalesu dhammesu, sādhu paññā kusalesu dhammesū”ti. So pañcannaṃ orambhāgiyānaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ [V 0.0184] parikkhayā opapātiko hoti tattha parinibbāyī anāvattidhammo tasmā lokā. Evaṃ puggalo ummujjitvā patigādhappatto hoti.

And how is it that a person, having emerged, gains a foothold? Here, a certain person emerges, thinking: ‘It is good to have faith in wholesome states, good to have moral shame in wholesome states, good to have moral dread in wholesome states, good to have energy in wholesome states, good to have wisdom in wholesome states.’ With the destruction of the five lower fetters, he is one of spontaneous birth, due to attain final Nibbāna there, not liable to return from that world. In this way a person, having emerged, gains a foothold.

Kathañca puggalo ummujjitvā tiṇṇo hoti pāraṅgato thale tiṭṭhati brāhmaṇo? Idhekacco puggalo ummujjati “sāhu saddhā kusalesu dhammesu[M 0.0183], sādhu hirī kusalesu dhammesu, sādhu ottappaṃ kusalesu dhammesu, sādhu vīriyaṃ kusalesu dhammesu, sādhu paññā kusalesu dhammesū”ti. So āsavānaṃ khayā anāsavaṃ cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ diṭṭheva dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā sacchikatvā upasampajja viharati. Evaṃ puggalo ummujjitvā tiṇṇo hoti pāraṅgato thale tiṭṭhati brāhmaṇo.

And how is it that a person, having emerged, has crossed over, gone beyond, and stands on high ground, a brahmin? Here, a certain person emerges, thinking: ‘It is good to have faith in wholesome states, good to have moral shame in wholesome states, good to have moral dread in wholesome states, good to have energy in wholesome states, good to have wisdom in wholesome states.’ By the destruction of the taints, in this very life he realizes for himself with direct knowledge the taintless liberation of mind, liberation by wisdom, and having entered upon it, he dwells in it. In this way a person, having emerged, has crossed over, gone beyond, and stands on high ground, a brahmin.

204. Katamo ca puggalo ubhatobhāgavimutto? Idhekacco puggalo aṭṭha vimokkhe kāyena phusitvā viharati paññāya cassa disvā āsavā parikkhīṇā honti. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo ubhatobhāgavimutto.

204. And what kind of person is one liberated in both ways? Here, a certain person dwells having contacted the eight liberations with the body, and his taints are destroyed by his seeing with wisdom. This is called a person liberated in both ways.

205. Katamo ca puggalo paññāvimutto…pe… kāyasakkhī… diṭṭhippatto… saddhāvimutto… dhammānusārī [T 0.0233]….

205. And what kind of person is one liberated by wisdom … a body-witness … one who has attained view … one liberated by faith … a Dhamma-follower …

206. Katamo ca puggalo saddhānusārī? Yassa puggalassa sotāpattiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipannassa saddhindriyaṃ adhimattaṃ hoti, saddhāvāhiṃ saddhāpubbaṅgamaṃ ariyamaggaṃ bhāveti. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo saddhānusārī. Sotāpattiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno puggalo saddhānusārī, phale ṭhito saddhāvimuttoti.

206. And what kind of person is a faith-follower? It is a person practicing for the realization of the fruit of stream-entry for whom the faith faculty is predominant, who develops the noble path led by faith, with faith as its forerunner. This person is called a faith-follower. The person practicing for the realization of the fruit of stream-entry is a faith-follower; established in the fruit, he is one liberated by faith.

Sattakaniddeso.

The Exposition of the Sevens is finished.

8. Aṭṭhakapuggalapaññatti

8. Designation of Persons in Eights

207. Tattha [P 0.0073][V 0.0185] katame cattāro maggasamaṅgino, cattāro phalasamaṅgino puggalā? Sotāpanno, sotāpattiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno; sakadāgāmī, sakadāgāmiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno; anāgāmī, anāgāmiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno; arahā, arahattaphalasacchikiriyāya arahattāya (syā. ka.) a. ni. 8.59 paṭipanno; ime cattāro maggasamaṅgino, ime cattāro phalasamaṅgino puggalā.

207. Therein, who are the four persons endowed with the path and the four persons endowed with the fruit? The stream-enterer, and the one practicing for the realization of the fruit of stream-entry; the once-returner, and the one practicing for the realization of the fruit of once-returning; the non-returner, and the one practicing for the realization of the fruit of non-returning; the Arahant, and the one practicing for the sake of Arahantship. These are the four persons endowed with the path; these are the four persons endowed with the fruit.

Aṭṭhakaniddeso.

The Exposition of the Eights is finished.

9. Navakapuggalapaññatti

9. Designation of Persons in Nines

208. Katamo [M 0.0184] ca puggalo sammāsambuddho? Idhekacco puggalo pubbe ananussutesu dhammesu sāmaṃ saccāni abhisambujjhati, tattha ca sabbaññutaṃ [T 0.0234] pāpuṇāti balesu ca vasībhāvaṃ. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo sammāsambuddho.

208. And what kind of person is a Perfectly Self-Enlightened One? Here, a certain person, in regard to teachings not heard before, awakens by himself to the truths, and therein he attains omniscience and mastery in regard to the powers. This person is called a Perfectly Self-Enlightened One.

Katamo ca puggalo paccekasambuddho? Idhekacco puggalo pubbe ananussutesu dhammesu sāmaṃ saccāni abhisambujjhati, na ca tattha sabbaññutaṃ pāpuṇāti na ca balesu vasībhāvaṃ. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo paccekasambuddho.

And what kind of person is a Private Buddha? Here, a certain person, in regard to teachings not heard before, awakens by himself to the truths, but he does not therein attain omniscience nor mastery in regard to the powers. This person is called a Private Buddha.

Katamo ca puggalo ubhatobhāgavimutto? Idhekacco puggalo aṭṭha vimokkhe kāyena phusitvā viharati paññāya cassa disvā āsavā parikkhīṇā honti. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo ubhatobhāgavimutto.

And what kind of person is one liberated in both ways? Here, a certain person dwells having touched the eight liberations with his body, and his taints are destroyed through his seeing with wisdom. This person is called one liberated in both ways.

Katamo ca puggalo paññāvimutto? Idhekacco puggalo na heva kho aṭṭha vimokkhe kāyena phusitvā viharati paññāya cassa disvā āsavā parikkhīṇā honti. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo paññāvimutto.

And what kind of person is one liberated by wisdom? Here, a certain person does not dwell having touched the eight liberations with his body, but his taints are destroyed through his seeing with wisdom. This person is called one liberated by wisdom.

Katamo [V 0.0186] ca puggalo kāyasakkhī? Idhekacco puggalo aṭṭha vimokkhe kāyena phusitvā viharati paññāya cassa disvā ekacce āsavā parikkhīṇā honti. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo kāyasakkhī.

And what kind of person is a body-witness? Here, a certain person dwells having touched the eight liberations with his body, and some of his taints are destroyed through his seeing with wisdom. This person is called a body-witness.

Katamo [P 0.0074] ca puggalo diṭṭhippatto? Idhekacco puggalo “idaṃ dukkha”nti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti…pe… “ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, tathāgatappaveditā cassa dhammā paññāya vodiṭṭhā honti vocaritā, paññāya cassa disvā ekacce āsavā parikkhīṇā honti. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo diṭṭhippatto.

And what kind of person is one who has attained to view? Here, a certain person understands as it really is: ‘This is suffering’ … ‘This is the path leading to the cessation of suffering’; and the teachings proclaimed by the Tathāgata have been well seen and well practiced by him with wisdom, and some of his taints are destroyed through his seeing with wisdom. This person is called one who has attained to view.

Katamo ca puggalo saddhāvimutto? Idhekacco puggalo “idaṃ dukkha”nti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti…pe… “ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā”ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, tathāgatappaveditā cassa [T 0.0235] dhammā paññāya vodiṭṭhā [M 0.0185] honti vocaritā, paññāya cassa disvā ekacce āsavā parikkhīṇā honti, no ca kho yathā diṭṭhippattassa. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo saddhāvimutto.

And what kind of person is one liberated by faith? Here, a certain person understands as it really is: ‘This is suffering’ … ‘This is the path leading to the cessation of suffering’; and the teachings proclaimed by the Tathāgata have been well seen and well practiced by him with wisdom, and some of his taints are destroyed through his seeing with wisdom, but not to the same extent as for one who has attained to view. This person is called one liberated by faith.

Katamo ca puggalo dhammānusārī? Yassa puggalassa sotāpattiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipannassa paññindriyaṃ adhimattaṃ hoti, paññāvāhiṃ paññāpubbaṅgamaṃ ariyamaggaṃ bhāveti. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo dhammānusārī. Sotāpattiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno puggalo dhammānusārī, phale ṭhito diṭṭhippatto.

And what kind of person is a Dhamma-follower? It is a person practicing for the realization of the fruit of stream-entry for whom the wisdom faculty is predominant; he develops the noble path that is led by wisdom and has wisdom as its forerunner. This person is called a Dhamma-follower. The person practicing for the realization of the fruit of stream-entry is a Dhamma-follower; established in the fruit, he is one who has attained to view.

Katamo ca puggalo saddhānusārī? Yassa puggalassa sotāpattiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipannassa saddhindriyaṃ adhimattaṃ hoti, saddhāvāhiṃ saddhāpubbaṅgamaṃ ariyamaggaṃ bhāveti. Ayaṃ vuccati puggalo saddhānusārī. Sotāpattiphalasacchikiriyāya paṭipanno puggalo saddhānusārī, phale ṭhito saddhāvimuttoti.

And what kind of person is a faith-follower? It is a person practicing for the realization of the fruit of stream-entry for whom the faith faculty is predominant; he develops the noble path that is led by faith and has faith as its forerunner. This person is called a faith-follower. The person practicing for the realization of the fruit of stream-entry is a faith-follower; established in the fruit, he is one liberated by faith.

Navakaniddeso.

The Exposition of the Nines is finished.

10. Dasakapuggalapaññatti

10. Designation of Persons in Tens

209. Katamesaṃ [V 0.0187] pañcannaṃ idha niṭṭhā? Sattakkhattuparamassa kolaṅkolassa ekabījissa sakadāgāmissa yo ca diṭṭheva dhamme arahā – imesaṃ pañcannaṃ idha niṭṭhā.

209. For which five persons is there consummation here? For the one with at most seven more existences, the one who goes from clan to clan, the one-seeder, the once-returner, and the one who is an Arahant in this very life—for these five persons, there is consummation here.

Katamesaṃ pañcannaṃ idha vihāya niṭṭhā? Antarāparinibbāyissa upahaccaparinibbāyissa asaṅkhāraparinibbāyissa sasaṅkhāraparinibbāyissa [T 0.0236] uddhaṃsotassa akaniṭṭhagāmino – imesaṃ pañcannaṃ idha vihāya niṭṭhāti.

For which five persons is there consummation, having abandoned this existence? For the one who attains Parinibbāna in the interval, the one who attains Parinibbāna upon landing, the one who attains Parinibbāna without exertion, the one who attains Parinibbāna with exertion, and the one heading upstream, bound for the Akaniṭṭha realm—for these five persons, there is consummation, having abandoned this existence.

Ettāvatā puggalānaṃ puggalapaññattīti.

Thus far is the designation of persons.

Dasakaniddeso.

The Exposition of the Tens is concluded.

Puggalapaññattipakaraṇaṃ niṭṭhitaṃ.

The Treatise on the Designation of Persons is completed.
